\section{Three Faces of MDL Revisited: Why This Is Not a Loop}
\label{sec:mdl_loop}

A reader who has followed the derivation from Chapter~\ref{chap:principle}
(\S\ref{sec:pi_mdl}) and this chapter may notice that MDL appears more than once:
first as Presentation Invariance (PI~$\equiv$~MDL, \S\ref{sec:pi_mdl}), then in
this chapter as the theory-selection criterion, and Chapters~\ref{chap:spacetime}
and~\ref{chap:qm} will use it a third time. Is the same principle applied repeatedly,
making the framework circular?

The answer is no. \S\ref{sec:mdl_three_faces} gives the full treatment. The essential
point is that the three roles operate on three logically disjoint domains:

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}P{2.8cm}P{3.5cm}P{5.0cm}c@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Role} & \textbf{Domain} & \textbf{Application in this monograph} & \textbf{Cert} \\
\midrule
1.\ Theory selection & Theory space: all $\mathbb{Z}_N\times\mathbb{Z}_M$ CAs &
This chapter (MDL Uniqueness Theorem): $\mathbb{Z}_7\times\mathbb{Z}_3$ selected & \CatAL \\[4pt]
2.\ Variational (PMDL) & Field configurations within $\PHIMDL$ &
Chapter~\ref{chap:spacetime} (gravity): operates on fields of the already-selected theory &
\CatAD \\[4pt]
3.\ Adjudication ($[D]$) & Candidate realizations of a dynamical trajectory &
Chapter~\ref{chap:qm} (Born rule): operates on outcomes within already-established dynamics &
\CatAL \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The logical order is strict. Role~2 cannot be applied before Role~1 closes: you
cannot minimize over field configurations before you know which theory defines those
fields. Role~3 cannot be applied before Role~2: you cannot adjudicate measurement
outcomes without the dynamics. The flow is:
\[
\underbrace{\text{Theory space}}_{\text{Role 1, this chapter}}
\;\longrightarrow\;
\underbrace{\text{Field configurations}}_{\text{Role 2, Ch.~\ref{chap:spacetime}}}
\;\longrightarrow\;
\underbrace{\text{Candidate realizations}}_{\text{Role 3, Ch.~\ref{chap:qm}}}.
\]

No role refers back to any prior role's domain. The three applications of MDL are not
three iterations of the same argument; they are one principle manifesting at three
successive levels of physical organization, logically downstream of each other.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=yellow!8,colframe=yellow!60!black,
  title={Non-Circularity Statement (\CatAL)}]
MDL is applied exactly once per domain. Role~1 (this chapter) selects among all
possible theories. Role~2 (Chapter~\ref{chap:spacetime}) operates within the
already-selected theory's field configuration space. Role~3
(Chapter~\ref{chap:qm}) operates within already-established dynamical trajectories.
No role refers back to or re-examines any prior role's domain.

\smallskip\noindent
\textbf{Lean cert:} \lean{mdl\_tower\_three\_levels\_non\_circular} and
\lean{mdl\_tower\_bundle} (\CatAL, \texttt{MDLTower.lean}, ugp-lean, zero sorry,
zero custom axioms): formally certifies the domain disjointness and the bundle of
all three roles.
\end{tcolorbox}

\noindent
This closes Chapter~\ref{chap:selection}. The selection is complete:
$\mathbb{Z}_7\times\mathbb{Z}_3$, with the 19-bit polynomial $\poly = C+R-CR-LCR$
over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$, is the unique MDL-minimal substrate. Chapter~\ref{chap:field}
shows how this algebraic certificate lifts to the physical field $\PHIMDL$ that
carries particles, forces, spacetime, and quantum mechanics.
Full invariant-subset classification, QNR binary-floor uniqueness proofs, and
Wolfram-executable models of the polynomial appear in P49~\cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialWolfram},
which also establishes the four-object derivation tower ($p$, $f_{\rm MDL}$, $\PHIMDL$, $T$)
where $T$ is proved non-polynomial over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$ by explicit counterexample.
P49 further shows that the WolframModel causal graph of the GTE local update rule
($1023$ events, fractal binary tree) is structurally equivalent to the Lindenmayer
system $F\to F[{+}F][{-}F]$ used to model \emph{Daucus carota} compound umbels
(§5.4); the binary branching is Lean-certified to arise from the GEN$_2$/GEN$_3$

\section{The \CMCA: The Algebraic Certificate}
\label{sec:cmca}

Before introducing $\PHIMDL$, we need to understand the algebraic proof system that
certifies what structures the physical field must contain.
That proof system is the Chiral Minkowski Cellular Automaton (\CMCA) --- the
Level~1 algebraic certificate whose rules and consequences lift to the physical
Level~2 field via the Algebraic Lifting Theorem.

\subsection*{What Is a Cellular Automaton?}

A cellular automaton is a grid of discrete cells, each in one of a finite number of
states, that all update simultaneously at each time step according to a fixed local rule.
The rule computes the new state of a cell from the current states of itself and its
nearest neighbors.
The CMCA's update rule is $\poly = C+R-CR-LCR$ over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$,
the Galois field with seven elements.
The three inputs $L$, $C$, $R$ are the left neighbor, center cell, and right neighbor,
all carrying values in $\{0,1,\ldots,6\}$.
This single polynomial is the object from which all of Standard Model physics descends.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5,colframe=green!50!black,
  title={Definition: Chiral Minkowski Cellular Automaton (\CMCA)}]
The \CMCA\ is a 1+1-dimensional cellular automaton whose update polynomial is
$\poly = C+R-CR-LCR$ over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$.
It is the \emph{algebraic certificate} for $\PHIMDL$: it encodes the algebraic structure
that the physical field must inherit, but is not itself the physical substrate.
The physical substrate is the continuum field $\PHIMDL$ (Level~2); the \CMCA\ is
the discrete algebraic specification (Level~1).
\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=blue!4,colframe=blue!50,
  title={The GTE Computational Architecture: One System, Four Levels},
  fonttitle=\bfseries,breakable]
\small
The polynomial $p(L,C,R) = C+R-CR-LCR$ defines a single physical system
expressed at four levels of description; each level is the same physics
at a different scale of resolution.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Level~0 --- Polynomial} (Object~0; P28, P49).
The raw $k{=}7$, $r{=}1$ CA rule over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$.
Class~3 chaotic on generic $\ZS$ inputs; Class~4 (Rule~110) on
$\{0,1\}$-inputs. MDL$+$PSC select it uniquely from $7^{343}$ candidates
(\lean{mdl\_ca\_rule\_coding\_closed}, \CatAL).

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Level~1 --- PSC-projected orbit map} (Object~1, $f_{\rm MDL}$; P28, P37, P41).
The restriction of $p$ to PSC-admissible inputs: $f_{\rm MDL}$ maps 14 of the 343
neighborhoods non-trivially; all others produce the vacuum.
On the isolated 5-cell ring, $f_{\rm MDL}$ runs the SM generation orbit
$\mathrm{GEN}_1\to\mathrm{GEN}_2\to\mathrm{GEN}_3\to\mathrm{VAC}$ (\CatAL).
For quantum mechanics (Hilbert space, Born rule, gauge sectors), $f_{\rm MDL}$ is
used as an abstract ring orbit; for spacetime dynamics it is embedded in a
running tape (Level~2).

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Level~2 --- CMCA tape} (P41, P45).
$f_{\rm MDL}$ embedded in a 1D spatial tape with a chiral pair
(Rule~110 forward / Rule~124 backward) and an inner $\tau_c$ first-passage clock that gates
outer updates asynchronously --- the AFCA (Asynchronous Fractal Cellular Automata;
P36 \cite{SpivackEmergentGravity}) inner-clock architecture.
This is the \CMCA: the same Level~1 orbit structure running in $1{+}1$D
spacetime. Three tapes sharing a global outer clock $\tau_c^{\rm out}$ give
the $3{+}1$D architecture (P45; Dimensional Protocol Principle, \CatAL).

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Level~3 --- Continuum field} (Object~2, $\PHIMDL$; P42, P43).
The Algebraic Lifting Theorem (\lean{algebraic\_lifting\_theorem}, \CatAL) maps
the \CMCA's discrete structure to the $\ZS$-symmetric Klein--Gordon field $\PHIMDL$.
SM particles are BPS kink solitons; all SM parameters, spacetime, quantum
mechanics, and cosmology emerge at this level.

\medskip
\noindent All four levels describe the \emph{same} physical system; Levels~0--3
differ only in spatial extent and degree of continuum approximation.
The AFCA is the inner-clock gating mechanism of Level~2; it is not a
competing framework for quantum mechanics (QM structure is derived at Level~1
via the cogwheel ring orbit).

\medskip
\noindent\textit{Relation to the two-level framework used elsewhere in this
monograph (§\ref{sec:two_levels}):}
Levels~0--2 collectively constitute the \emph{algebraic certificate} (the
``Level~1'' of the two-level framework); Level~3 here is the \emph{continuum
physical substrate} (``Level~2'' in that framework). The four-level description
is a finer-grained view of the same architecture; both numbering schemes are
valid.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{The Three-Layer Architecture of a Single Tape}

A single \CMCA\ tape has three causal layers, corresponding to the three irreducible
causal directions of $1{+}1$D Minkowski spacetime.

The \textbf{outer chiral pair} consists of two simultaneous update layers:
Rule~110 ($p(L,C,R)$, right-chiral) and Rule~124 ($p(R,C,L)$, its spatial mirror, left-chiral).
These two layers provide the two null directions $x^+$ and $x^-$ of $1{+}1$D Minkowski
spacetime and implement the V--A chiral structure: only the left-chiral (Rule~124) sector
couples to weak interactions.
Rule~124 is not an independent specification --- it is the free structural consequence of
the lattice's spatial reflection symmetry, costing zero additional MDL bits.

The \textbf{inner clock} is a third Rule~110 automaton that runs per-cell and gates when
the outer layers fire.
It implements proper-time ordering: configurations that move relative to the ether vacuum
tick the inner clock slower, giving the discrete analogue of SR time dilation.
The inner clock is forced by MDL to be Rule~110 (the same polynomial as the outer layer)
--- using any other rule would cost extra bits \cite{SpivackCMCA,SpivackThreeTapeCMCA}.

\subsection*{The Dimensional Protocol Principle and the Three-Tape Architecture}

A single \CMCA\ tape is intrinsically $1{+}1$-dimensional.
Three-dimensional space arises from three independent single-tape \CMCA{}s sharing a single
global outer clock $\tau_c^{\rm out}$.

The \textbf{Dimensional Protocol Principle (DPP)} states that this shared clock is both
necessary and sufficient for the three $1{+}1$D tapes to produce $3{+}1$D Minkowski dynamics.
Without the shared clock, the three tapes evolve as three independent $1{+}1$D systems and
cannot build spatial cross-correlations.
With the shared clock, the tensor product of their dynamics gives $\mathbb{R}^{3,1}$
Minkowski spacetime with the correct metric \cite{SpivackThreeTapeCMCA}.

\textbf{Lean certs:}
\lean{dimensional\_protocol\_principle\_master} (\CatAL, zero sorry, ugp-lean):
shared clock necessary and sufficient.
\lean{cmca\_tensor\_product\_gives\_31d\_minkowski} (\CatAL, zero sorry, ugp-lean):
tensor product gives $\mathbb{R}^{3,1}$.

The conceptual shift is important: the physical universe is not built from
three-dimensional voxels.
It is built from three parallel one-dimensional arrays sharing a clock.
Three-dimensional space is an emergent coordination structure, not a pre-existing container.

\begin{center}

\midrule

Chirality selector & 1 & chiral vs.\ outer-totalistic \\
Sign pattern (nonlinear terms) & 2 & $-CR$ and $-LCR$ each $\pm 1$ mod~7 \\
\midrule
\textbf{Total} & \textbf{19} & \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Each bit has a specific physical role: the Rule~110 table encodes spatial dynamics,
the GF(7) modulus sets the generation arithmetic, the form selector identifies the
minimal universal cubic, the chirality bit purchases V--A structure for free, and
the sign bits fix the nonlinear coupling.

\subsection*{The Single-Source Principle: Five Roles, One Polynomial}

The 19-bit polynomial achieves five independent physical roles simultaneously, with
$K_{\rm extra} = 0$ for each role beyond the first --- no additional bits are needed
to specify each role once the polynomial is known.
This was established in Chapter~\ref{chap:selection} (\S\ref{sec:19bits}) and
machine-certified (\lean{gte\_polynomial\_five\_roles\_k\_extra\_zero}, \CatAL;
see Chapter~\ref{chap:glance} for the full enumeration).

\noindent
This is what ``19~bits $\to$ all of physics'' means as a precise, provable statement:
a single specification is simultaneously the CA update rule, the gauge-vertex law,
the gravity source, the baryon-number operator, and the Bell-entanglement structure ---
each at zero additional description cost
(Figure~\ref{fig:polynomial_five_roles}, Chapter~\ref{chap:selection}).

\subsection*{The Correct Interpretation: Certificate, Not Substrate}

The \CMCA\ is not the physical universe.
It is the algebraic proof system that certifies what structures must exist in the
physical universe.

The thermometer analogy from \cite{SpivackThreeTapeCMCA} captures the relationship:
a thermometer does not contain the gas it measures --- it certifies a property
(temperature) of that gas.
The \CMCA\ certifies algebraic properties of $\PHIMDL$; those properties are then
inherited by the continuum field via the Algebraic Lifting Theorem.

What the \CMCA\ \emph{cannot} do is provide exact Lorentz invariance.
For any finite lattice resolution $M$, the Nyquist residual
$\varepsilon_0(M) = \pi^2/(3M^2)$ measures the Lorentz-invariance error;
it is strictly positive for all finite $M$ and vanishes only as $M\to\infty$
(Lean: \lean{sr\_accuracy\_depends\_on\_M}, \CatAL\ zero sorry).
The physical substrate $\PHIMDL$, as an exact continuum field, is the unique
object in this construction class with zero Lorentz error --- which is why it
is the physical substrate, not the \CMCA\ \cite{SpivackCompleteness}.

The Level~1 / Level~2 architecture is therefore:
\begin{itemize}[itemsep=2pt]
\item \textbf{Level~1 (CMCA):} algebraic properties only --- charge quantization,
  generation count, Turing universality, baryon number, color confinement.
  Certified exactly, $M$-independently, in Lean~4.
\item \textbf{Level~2 ($\PHIMDL$):} the unique continuum realization with exact
  Lorentz invariance, exact kink masses, and the full quantum field theory.
\end{itemize}

\noindent
The next section introduces the central question: in this two-level picture, what
\emph{is} a particle?

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{What Is a Particle? The Two-Level Picture}
\label{sec:two_levels}

This section answers the question every reader wants answered: what, precisely, is an electron?

\subsection*{The Standard QFT Answer and Its Limitation}

In standard quantum field theory, an electron is an excitation of the electron quantum
field --- a ripple in an abstract mathematical object that permeates all of spacetime.
This is correct but incomplete: it does not explain \emph{why} the electron field exists,
why it has charge $-1$, or why there are three generations of it.

In GTE, the answer is more specific and more derived.
An electron is a \textbf{topological kink} in $\PHIMDL$: a region where the field has wound
from one vacuum configuration to another in a way it cannot unwind without a paired
antiparticle.
The topology determines the particle.
The field is everywhere; the topology is what we call the particle.

\subsection*{The Analogy: A Twist in a Rope}

Imagine a rope that can exist in seven colors.
Normally the rope is uniform (vacuum state, color~0).
A particle is a region where the rope permanently changes color --- from color~0 to color~4,
say --- and never returns.
To destroy the particle, you need a region where the rope returns from color~4 to color~0;
that is its antiparticle.
When particle and antiparticle meet, the rope returns to uniform color.

The ``color'' here is the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding number.
The number of color-steps traversed determines the particle's identity:
two steps gives an up quark, three steps a $W^+$ boson, four steps an electron.
There is no ``substance'' here --- the particle is a configuration, not a material.

\subsection*{Level 1: What the \CMCA\ Certifies}

At Level~1, a particle is an \textbf{algebraic equivalence class} of PSC-admissible
\CMCA\ beable configurations sharing the same three algebraic invariants:
(1)~$\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding number $w \in \{0,2,3,4,6\}$,
(2)~$\mathbb{Z}_3$ color charge $Q_\chi \in \{0,1,2\}$,
(3)~generation orbit position (gen$_1$, gen$_2$, or gen$_3$).

The \CMCA\ certifies:

\begin{itemize}[itemsep=4pt]
\item \textbf{Which winding sectors exist.}
  The five PSC-admissible sectors $\{0,2,3,4,6\}$ are forced by the dark-mirror
  structure of $F_{21}$; sectors $\{1,5\}$ are PSC-forbidden (they correspond to
  stable dark-sector mirrors not present in the SM branch)
  \cite{SpivackCompleteness,SpivackPhiMDLField}.
  Lean: \lean{psc\_admissible\_sectors} (\CatAL, zero sorry).

\item \textbf{That exactly three generations exist.}
  The five-cell $\ZS^5$ orbit under $f_{\rm MDL}$ has exactly three non-vacuum
  orbit types cycling with period~3: gen$_1 \to$ gen$_2 \to$ gen$_3 \to$ gen$_1$.
  This is not an input --- it is a consequence of the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ arithmetic
  and the Garden-of-Eden property of the first generation.
  Lean: \lean{three\_generations\_physical}, \lean{sm\_period3\_orbit\_chain}
  (\CatAL, zero sorry).

\item \textbf{That the first generation is the lightest.}
  Gen$_1$ is a Garden of Eden (GoE) under $f_{\rm MDL}$: it has zero predecessors
  in the orbit dynamics.
  This means it is algebraically ``most stable'' and corresponds to the lightest
  generation.
  Lean: \lean{garden\_of\_eden\_gen1} (\CatAL, zero sorry).

\item \textbf{Charge quantization and the charge formula.}
  $Q = w_{\rm centered}/3$ where $w_{\rm centered}$ is the centered representative
  of $w$ in $\{-3,-2,-1,0,1,2,3\}$.
  Lean: \lean{gte\_charge\_is\_linear\_projection} (\CatAL, zero sorry).

\item \textbf{Fermionic vs.\ bosonic statistics.}
  Sectors $\{2,4,6\}$ are non-primitive roots of $\mathbb{Z}_7^*$ (multiplicative order~3)
  and carry fermionic statistics.
  Sector $\{3\}$ is the unique generator of $\mathbb{Z}_7^*$ (order~6) and carries
  bosonic statistics.
  Lean: \lean{gte\_winding\_identifies\_charged\_fermions} (\CatAL, zero sorry).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{What Level~1 cannot certify:} mass values (those come from the Level~2 orbit cascade),
specific CA patterns at Compton scale (the Glider-to-Fermion Map is open), and exact
Lorentz invariance (requires $M\to\infty$).
The key point is that we do not need to identify which Rule~110 glider pattern
corresponds to the electron in order for the theory to be complete at Level~1.
The Lifting Theorem (below) certifies \emph{existence} without identifying the
specific microstructure.

\subsection*{The Algebraic Lifting Theorem: The Formal Bridge}

The Lifting Theorem connects Level~1 certificates to Level~2 physical observables.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={Theorem: Algebraic Lifting (\CatAL, zero sorry, zero axioms beyond CIC)}]
Let $B$ be a PSC-admissible beable with DWeight$(B) > 0$.
Then $B$ determines a physical observable at the Compton scale carrying the same
$\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding number, $\mathbb{Z}_3$ color charge, and generation index as $B$.
In particular, charge quantization, color structure, three-generation hierarchy, and
$S$-matrix quantum numbers proved at the beable level hold at the physically observable
Compton scale.

\smallskip\noindent
\textbf{Lean cert:} \lean{algebraic\_lifting\_theorem} (\CatAL, \texttt{LiftingTheorem.lean}, ugp-lean, zero sorry).
\end{tcolorbox}

The Lifting Theorem guarantees: for each PSC-admissible winding sector, a corresponding
physical particle with those quantum numbers must exist in $\PHIMDL$.
It does not specify which CA microstructure at Compton scale is the electron --- that is
an open problem.
What it establishes is existence and quantum-number identity.

The complementary theorem goes in the reverse direction:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5,colframe=green!50!black,

Every $F_{21} = \ZS\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ conservation law, charge quantization

in any continuum realization at any lattice resolution $M\geq 1$.
Charge quantization, color confinement, asymptotic freedom ($b_0=7$), and
strong-CP resolution ($\theta_{\rm QCD} = 0$) are $M$-independent.
Only geometric properties (Lorentz invariance, SR accuracy) require $M\to\infty$.

\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=blue!3,colframe=blue!45!black,

of the same object at different scales, related by a machine-certified
bidirectional correspondence.

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.2em, itemsep=2pt, topsep=3pt]

  It certifies the structure the physical field must contain without itself

\item \textbf{Both theorems carry zero \texttt{sorry} and zero custom axioms}

\subsection*{The Technical Bridge: How Discrete Arithmetic Becomes Continuous Gauge Theory}
\label{subsec:technical_bridge}

A physicist who knows gauge theory but not GTE will have a natural question: how does
arithmetic in the finite group $F_{21} = \ZS\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ constrain a Lie-group
gauge theory in $3{+}1$ dimensions?  The answer is a three-step argument.

\subsubsection*{Step 1: Gauging an Exact Discrete Symmetry}

The $\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian
$\mathcal{L} = \tfrac{1}{2}(\partial_\mu\Phi)^2 - V(\Phi)$
with $V(\Phi) = (m^2/49)(1-\cos 7\Phi)$ has an \emph{exact} discrete shift symmetry:
\[
  \Phi \;\to\; \Phi + \tfrac{2\pi}{7}
\]
leaving both kinetic and potential terms invariant ($\cos 7(\Phi + 2\pi/7) = \cos(7\Phi+2\pi) = \cos7\Phi$).
This is not an approximate or emergent symmetry — it is an exact arithmetic fact about
the cosine potential.  It is machine-certified as the $\ZS$ conservation law
(\lean{z7\_winding\_conservation\_vertex}, \CatAL, zero sorry).

Now apply the gauge principle: make the transformation parameter spacetime-dependent,
$\epsilon(x) = n(x)\cdot 2\pi/7$ for $n(x):\mathbb{R}^{3,1}\to\mathbb{Z}_7$.
A \emph{local} version of this symmetry requires introducing a connection field
$A_\mu$ to define the covariant derivative
$D_\mu\Phi = \partial_\mu\Phi - A_\mu$.
This is the standard logic of the gauge principle: exact symmetry $\Rightarrow$ gauging
is consistent $\Rightarrow$ gauge field $A_\mu$ is required.

\subsubsection*{Step 2: Why SU(3)\texorpdfstring{$\times$}{x}SU(2)\texorpdfstring{$\times$}{x}U(1) Specifically}

The discrete structure $F_{21} = \ZS\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ imposes two simultaneous
constraints on the gauge field:
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=3pt]
\item \textbf{From $\ZS$:} the connection must implement a $\mathrm{U}(1)$ holonomy
  consistent with $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding ($\mathbb{Z}_7 \subset \mathrm{U}(1)$ since
  $\mathbb{Z}_7$ is cyclic).  The $\ZS$ part forces a $\mathrm{U}(1)$-type structure
  for electromagnetism.  Color charge arises from the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ Sylow subgroup
  (the abelianization $F_{21}^{\rm ab} = \mathbb{Z}_3$), forcing $\mathrm{SU}(3)$-type
  color gauge structure.

\item \textbf{From the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ twist in $F_{21}$:} the non-abelian semidirect
  product $\ZS\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ means $\mathbb{Z}_3$ acts on $\ZS$ by the unique
  non-trivial automorphism of order 3.  This forces a non-abelian component in the
  gauge group, and specifically the $\mathrm{SU}(2)_L$ weak structure from the
  chiral (V$-$A) asymmetry of the CMCA.
\end{enumerate}

The MDL-minimal Lie group that simultaneously implements both constraints --- the
$\ZS$ holonomy and the $F_{21}$ non-abelian extension --- is exactly
$\mathrm{SU}(3)\times\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{U}(1)$.

\subsubsection*{Step 3: Uniqueness — Why No Other Gauge Group Works}

Any other gauge group would require additional specification beyond what $F_{21}$
prescribes.  For example:
\begin{itemize}[itemsep=3pt]
\item $\mathrm{SU}(5)$ (GUT) introduces a rank-5 group with 24 generators, requiring
  at least $\log_2(24/8) \approx 1.6$ extra bits of specification compared to
  $\mathrm{SU}(3)\times\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{U}(1)$.
\item $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ alone: replacing $F_{21}$ with $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ would cost
  $\geq 38$ additional MDL bits (\lean{algebraic\_necessity\_master\_bundle}, \CatAL).
\item Any $\mathbb{Z}_N$ structure with $N\neq 7$ is eliminated by exhaustive
  machine proof (\lean{unique\_cubic\_gravity\_coupling}, T96-02, \CatAL, zero sorry).
\end{itemize}

The MDL Uniqueness Theorem therefore certifies: among all $\mathbb{Z}_N\times\mathbb{Z}_M$
arithmetic structures, $\ZS\times\mathbb{Z}_3$ is the unique MDL-minimal one (\CatAL),
and $\mathrm{SU}(3)\times\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{U}(1)$ is the unique MDL-minimal
Lie group implementing its constraints.

\subsubsection*{Algebraic Descent Concretely}

The Descent direction makes this physically precise: if $\ZS$ winding conservation
holds at every CMCA vertex (machine-certified), then the $\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian with
$F_{21}$ gauge structure must also conserve winding at every Compton-scale interaction.
Computing the Noether charges of the resulting conserved currents gives exactly the
SM electric charges ($Q = w_{\rm centered}/3$, machine-certified as
\lean{gte\_charge\_is\_linear\_projection}, \CatAL), color charges from $\mathbb{Z}_3$,
and weak isospin from the $\mathrm{SU}(2)_L$ sector.

The gauge group is not assumed; it is forced by the arithmetic skeleton.
(\lean{algebraic\_descent\_theorem}, \CatAL; full derivation: Chapter~7.)

\subsection*{Level 2: What $\PHIMDL$ Certifies}

At Level~2, a particle is a \textbf{topological BPS kink soliton} of $\PHIMDL$,
characterized by its winding number $w\in\{0,2,3,4,6\}$ and $\mathbb{Z}_3$ color
charge $Q_\chi\in\{0,1,2\}$.
Its mass, charge, and spin are entirely determined by these two numbers.
The full derivation follows in \S\ref{sec:bps_kinks}; here we give the key facts:

\begin{itemize}[itemsep=4pt]
\item \textbf{Identity is topology, not substance.}
  What makes an electron an electron is its winding number $w=4$, not any
  intrinsic material.
  Change the winding number and you have a different particle.
  The same $\PHIMDL$ field supports all SM particles; they differ only in
  their topological configuration.

\item \textbf{Topological protection.}
  A kink cannot spontaneously unwind: the transition $w=4\to w=0$ requires
  crossing an infinite energy barrier in the $M\to\infty$ limit.
  Particle stability is a consequence of topology, not of any protective symmetry
  imposed by hand.

\item \textbf{The kink mass is the field scale, not the particle mass.}
  The BPS kink mass is $\Mkink = (8/49)m_\tau = 290.10$~MeV (\CatAL).
  This sets the strong-interaction energy scale.
  The physical lepton masses ($m_e = 0.511$~MeV, $m_\mu = 105.7$~MeV,
  $m_\tau = 1776.86$~MeV) come from the GTE orbit cascade arithmetic at
  Level~1 --- not directly from the BPS energy.
  Distinguishing these two is essential: the kink mass tells us the
  \emph{scale} of the substrate; the orbit cascade tells us the
  \emph{hierarchy} of the particles living on that substrate.

\item \textbf{Elementary particles vs.\ composites.}
  A single BPS kink is one elementary particle (one electron, one quark, etc.).
  Composite particles --- protons, neutrons, pions --- are bound states of multiple
  kinks, exactly as they are bound states of quarks in QCD.
  The kink is not a point object: at Level~2 it extends over the particle's Compton
  wavelength in continuous spacetime.
  Lattice cells (Level~1 language) do not appear at Level~2; the kink profile is
  a smooth field envelope on $\mathbb{R}^{3,1}$.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{The Worked Example: The Electron}

To make the two-level picture concrete, we trace the electron through both levels.

\textbf{Level~1:} The electron corresponds to the gen$_1$ beable with
orbit configuration $[1,5,2,2,1]$ in $\ZS^5$.
Its algebraic invariants are: $W_B = 4$ (SM interaction winding, CatAL via orbit arithmetic),
GoE stability (zero predecessors, \lean{garden\_of\_eden\_gen1}, CatAL),
and fermionic statistics (ord$(4) = 3$ in $\mathbb{Z}_7^*$,
\lean{gte\_winding\_identifies\_charged\_fermions}, CatAL).
The charge follows from the formula: $Q = (4-7)/3 = -1$
(\lean{gte\_charge\_is\_linear\_projection}, CatAL).

\textbf{Level~2:} The electron is a BPS kink in $\PHIMDL$ with winding number $w=4$,
connecting the fourth vacuum of the $\ZS$ potential to the zeroth.
Its charge is $Q = -1$ (from the same formula, now applied to the kink), its spin
$J = 1/2$ follows from the Lean-certified exchange statistics~\mbox{(\lean{gte\_triple\_kink\_exchange\_statistics}, \CatAL)}, and its mass
$m_e = 0.511$~MeV from the Level-1 orbit cascade ($N_{\rm eff}=73$ seed, \CatA).
The kink is topologically stable: to annihilate the electron requires pairing it
with a positron ($w=3$), since $4 + 3 = 7 \equiv 0 \pmod{7}$ restores the vacuum.

\textbf{The formal chain:}
\begin{align*}
&\underbrace{[1,5,2,2,1],\; W_B{=}4,\;\text{GoE}}_{\text{Level-1 certificate}}
\xrightarrow{\lean{algebraic\_lifting\_theorem}\;(\CatAL)}\\
&\underbrace{w{=}4 \text{ kink in }\PHIMDL,\;Q{=}{-}1,\;J{=}{\tfrac{1}{2}}}_{\text{Level-2 physical state}}
\xrightarrow{\text{orbit cascade}}
m_e = 0.511\text{ MeV}.
\end{align*}

\subsection*{Why the Two-Level Description Is Essential}

If we described particles only at Level~1 (CA orbit classes), the theory would
seem to lack a physical realization.
If we described them only at Level~2 (kinks), we would seem to have abandoned
the algebraic certificate that makes the quantum numbers provable.
The two-level description is essential:
Level~1 proves existence and certifies quantum numbers;
Level~2 provides the physical realization with exact masses, Lorentz invariance, and
a complete quantum field theory.
The Algebraic Lifting Theorem is the formal statement that the two levels
refer to the same physical object.

\section{The $\Phi_{\mathrm{MDL}}$ Lagrangian}
\label{sec:lagrangian}

The $\PHIMDL$ field is a $\mathbb{Z}_7$-symmetric Klein-Gordon gauge field on Minkowski
spacetime.
Before writing down the equations, let us describe the physical picture they encode.

The field $\PHIMDL$ can sit in any of seven degenerate ground states (vacua), equally
spaced around the unit circle in field space.
In empty space, the field sits in one of these seven vacua --- it costs zero energy.
A particle is a region where the field has permanently shifted to a different vacuum.
The potential energy landscape is like seven evenly-spaced valleys arranged in a ring:
to move from one valley to another, you must climb over a ridge.
The kink is the smooth interpolation between two adjacent valleys.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5,colframe=green!50!black,
  title={Statement of the Theory},breakable]
The physical universe is the $\PHIMDL$ field --- a $\mathbb{Z}_7$-symmetric
Klein-Gordon gauge field on $\mathbb{R}^{3+1}$ with internal symmetry group
$F_{21} = \mathbb{Z}_7 \rtimes \mathbb{Z}_3$, the unique non-abelian group of
order~21 selected by Minimum Description Length minimality.
The framework has two levels: the Chiral Minkowski Cellular Automaton (\CMCA),
encoded by the 19-bit polynomial $\poly = C{+}R{-}CR{-}LCR$ over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$,
serves as the Level~1 algebraic certificate --- the irreducible arithmetic
skeleton that certifies what structures the physical field must contain;
$\PHIMDL$ is the Level~2 physical substrate --- the unique continuum
Lorentz-invariant field consistent with those constraints.
Elementary particles are BPS topological kinks of $\PHIMDL$, characterised
by $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding number and $\mathbb{Z}_3$ colour charge
corresponding exactly to SM quantum numbers.
The SM gauge interactions arise from $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding conservation at
every vertex; spacetime geometry emerges from the geodesic computation
principle; the Born rule is derived from four independent routes, not
postulated; and the cosmological constant, baryon asymmetry, and spectral
tilt follow from the \PSC\ adjudication floor --- all with zero free
parameters.
\emph{Zero free parameters means zero free dimensionless parameters; the single
dimensional anchor $m_\tau$ (in MeV) sets the energy scale, from which all masses,
couplings, and cosmological predictions follow.}
The complete derivation chain, from Perfect Self-Containment through the
Standard Model, spacetime, quantum mechanics, and cosmology, is
machine-certified in 308~Lean~4 modules with zero sorry and zero custom axioms.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{The Lagrangian}

The $\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian density is \cite{SpivackPhiMDLField,SpivackCompleteness}:

\begin{equation}
\mathcal{L} = \tfrac{1}{2}(\partial_\mu\Phi)^2 - V(\Phi),
\qquad
V(\Phi) = \frac{m_\varphi^2}{49}\bigl(1 - \cos 7\Phi\bigr).
\label{eq:phimdl_lagrangian}
\end{equation}

The kinetic term $\tfrac{1}{2}(\partial_\mu\Phi)^2$ is the standard Lorentz-invariant
Klein-Gordon kinetic energy.
The potential $V(\Phi)$ is a $\ZS$-symmetric sine-Gordon potential with seven degenerate
minima at $\Phi_k = 2\pi k/7$ for $k\in\{0,1,\ldots,6\}$, all with $V(\Phi_k) = 0$.
The factor $1/49$ ensures that the curvature $V''(\Phi_k) = m_\varphi^2$ --- the mass
parameter equals the field's oscillation frequency around each vacuum.
The coefficient $7$ in $\cos 7\Phi$ enforces the $\ZS$ symmetry:
$V(\Phi + 2\pi k/7) = V(\Phi)$ for all $k\in\mathbb{Z}_7$.

This is not a free scalar field.
It is an exactly integrable, non-perturbative field theory --- the $\ZS$ sine-Gordon
model --- with an infinite tower of conserved charges, exact BPS solitons, and a
$\mathbb{Z}_7$ superselection rule.
Its richness is precisely what makes it capable of encoding all of SM physics
in a single object.

\input{../common/figures/fig_p42_potential_kink.tex}

\subsection*{The Self-Consistency Condition: $m_\varphi = m_\tau$}

The mass parameter $m_\varphi$ is not a free parameter.
It is fixed by the Self-Consistency Condition (SCC): the theory must be self-consistent
in the sense that the single dimensionful parameter equals the mass of the heaviest
stable composite in the leptonic (colour-singlet, pure $\ZS$) sector.

The argument runs as follows \cite{SpivackPhiMDLField}:
(1) The Frobenius decomposition $F_{21} = \ZS\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ shows that the
leptonic sector inherits only the $\ZS$ kernel, with no $\mathbb{Z}_3$ colour charge.
(2) The three-generation orbit closure (certified \CatAL) terminates the cascade at
gen$_3$; no fourth generation exists.
(3) MDL minimality forbids unused dimensionful capacity: $m_\varphi$ must equal an
observable mass, and the unique candidate is the heaviest lepton in the
three-generation cascade.
(4) The GTE cascade gives lepton $N$-values $\{73,42,275\}$ for gen$_1$, gen$_2$,
gen$_3$; the heaviest ($b=275$) is the tau lepton.
Therefore:
\[
m_\varphi = m_\tau = 1776.86\text{ MeV}.
\]

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={Result: Self-Consistency Condition (\CatAD\ bridge; \CatAL\ downstream)}]
The mass parameter of $\PHIMDL$ is not a free input.
The Self-Consistency Condition fixes $m_\varphi = m_\tau = 1776.86$~MeV,
giving the kink mass $\Mkink = (8/49)m_\tau = 290.10$~MeV.

\smallskip\noindent
\textbf{Lean cert:} \lean{mkink\_from\_scc} (\CatAL, \texttt{GUTStructure.lean},
ugp-lean, zero sorry): kink mass given SCC.
The SCC identification $m_\varphi = m_\tau$ itself is \CatAD.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{The $F_{21}$ Gauge Sector}

The complete Lagrangian includes the $F_{21} = \ZS\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ gauge structure.
The abelianization $F_{21}^{\rm ab} = \mathbb{Z}_3$ provides the colour gauge field
$\chi$ with coupling constant $\varepsilon$.
This coupling is fully derived from $F_{21}$ representation theory
(rank-136 vertex coupling, \CatA\ and \CatAL; rank-137 $\varepsilon$ derivation,
\CatAL): there are no free coupling constants.

The covariant derivative for the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ gauge field is
$D_\mu = \partial_\mu + ig\chi_\mu$, and the gauge coupling term in the Lagrangian
takes the form $\varepsilon|\Phi|^2(D_\mu\chi)^2$.
Every coefficient is derived from GTE orbit arithmetic;
$\varepsilon$ from $F_{21}$ group theory; $m_\varphi$ from the SCC.
The $\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian has zero free parameters \cite{SpivackPhiMDLField}.

\subsection*{$\PHIMDL$ Is Fully Second-Quantized}

$\PHIMDL$ is not a classical field.
It is a fully second-quantized quantum field theory \cite{SpivackPhiMDLField,SpivackCompleteness}.
The quantization uses the Jackiw-Rebbi/Rajaraman approach for soliton collective coordinates,
and the quantum structure is machine-certified:

\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=4pt]
\item \textbf{Fock space construction} (\CatAD):
  The physical Hilbert space is
  $\mathcal{H} = \bigoplus_{k\in\{0,2,3,4,6\}}\mathcal{H}_k$
  over the five PSC-admissible $\ZS$ kink sectors.
  Each $\mathcal{H}_k$ is the space of multi-kink states with winding number~$k$.

\item \textbf{Born rule} (\CatAL, zero custom axioms):
  $P(k) = |c_k|^2$ for sector probabilities, machine-certified from first principles.
  Lean: \lean{born\_rule\_unconditional}.

\item \textbf{Physical pre-measurement state} (\CatAL\ conditional):
  The Hamiltonian thermal ensemble $P(k|T) = e^{-\Mkink/T}/Z_T$ on PSC-admissible sectors.
  Lean: \lean{phimdl\_thermal\_state\_master}.

\item \textbf{Measurement process (transputation)}:
  Quantum evolution consists of Born-rule evolution $B$ composed with the
  transputation selector $P_\top$.
  The $P_\top$ selector is non-computable (PSC-forced), implementing the
  measurement process without wave-function collapse as an independent postulate.
  This is treated in full in Chapter~\ref{chap:qm}.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection*{Per-Tape BPS Instanton Action}

The BPS instanton action on a single CMCA tape is
\[
S_1 = \frac{\pi}{N_c}
\]
(\CatAD).
This enters the baryon asymmetry derivation of Chapter~\ref{chap:cosmology}:
the instanton amplitude is $e^{-S_1} = e^{-\pi/3}$, which determines the
sphaleron rate and ultimately the matter-antimatter asymmetry.
Lean cert: \lean{bps\_per\_tape\_action\_eq\_pi\_over\_Nc} (\CatAL).

\section{BPS Kinks: The Physical Particles}
\label{sec:bps_kinks}

A kink is a field configuration that interpolates between two adjacent vacua.
To visualize it: in the ring of seven valleys described above, start at valley~0,
and smoothly transition to valley~1 (or 2, or 3, or 4, or 6) over some spatial region.
The field stays near valley~0 for $x\ll 0$, transitions through the ridge, and
sits at the new vacuum for $x\gg 0$.
That transition region is the kink.
Its spatial width is set by the inverse mass $1/m_\varphi \approx 0.111$~fm.

\subsection*{The BPS Kink Profile}

The static kink solution connecting adjacent vacua is \cite{SpivackPhiMDLField}:
\begin{equation}
\Phi_{\rm kink}(x) = \frac{4}{7}\arctan\!\bigl(e^{m_\varphi x}\bigr),
\qquad
\frac{d\Phi_{\rm kink}}{dx} = \frac{2m_\varphi}{7}\,\mathrm{sech}(m_\varphi x).
\label{eq:kink_profile}
\end{equation}

The kink center is at $x=0$.
The field approaches vacuum~0 as $x\to-\infty$ and vacuum~1 as $x\to+\infty$.
The derivative $d\Phi/dx$ is a sech function peaked at the kink center with width $\sim 1/m_\varphi$:
the energy density is concentrated in a region of femtometer scale
(comparable to the proton radius, $\sim 0.84$~fm).

\subsection*{BPS Saturation and the Energy Bound}

The \textbf{BPS condition} is the saturation of the Bogomolny energy bound:
\begin{equation}
\frac{d\Phi}{dx} = \sqrt{2V(\Phi)}.
\label{eq:bps_condition}
\end{equation}

A solution satisfying this first-order equation automatically satisfies the full
second-order equation of motion, and achieves the \emph{minimum possible energy}
for its topological class.
One can verify directly that the profile~(\ref{eq:kink_profile}) satisfies~(\ref{eq:bps_condition}):
inserting the kink profile into the right-hand side and applying a trigonometric identity
reproduces the left-hand side exactly.

\textbf{Physical meaning:} A BPS-saturated kink is a ``no-pressure'' solution with
$T_{11} = 0$ (zero longitudinal stress).
It is exactly stable against perturbation: any local deformation increases its energy.
BPS kinks are the minimum-energy representatives of their topological sector, which is why
they are the physical particles.

\subsection*{The Kink Mass}

The kink mass is the total energy of the kink configuration:
\begin{equation}
\Mkink
= \int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\left[\tfrac{1}{2}\Phi'^2 + V(\Phi)\right]dx
= \frac{4m_\varphi^2}{49}\!\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\mathrm{sech}^2(m_\varphi x)\,dx
= \frac{8m_\varphi}{49}
= \frac{8\,m_\tau}{49}.
\label{eq:kink_mass}
\end{equation}

Using $m_\tau = 1776.86$~MeV and the SCC identification $m_\varphi = m_\tau$:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={Result: BPS Kink Mass (\CatAL)}]
\[
\Mkink = \frac{8}{49}\,m_\tau = \frac{8}{49}\times 1776.86\text{ MeV} = 290.10\text{ MeV}.
\]
Zero free parameters.
This is the energy of a single-step topological excitation of $\PHIMDL$
--- the scale at which the strong interaction operates.

\smallskip\noindent
\textbf{Lean cert:} \lean{mkink\_from\_scc} (\CatAL, \texttt{GUTStructure.lean}, ugp-lean,
zero sorry);
\lean{phimdl\_kink\_masses\_equal} (\CatAL, zero sorry): all non-vacuum sectors have equal BPS mass.
\end{tcolorbox}

The equal-mass result deserves emphasis.
The $\ZS$ symmetry of the potential guarantees that every inter-vacuum transition
$k\to k{+}1$ has the same potential ridge height $\max V = 2m_\varphi^2/49$,
and therefore the same BPS bound.
All six non-vacuum kink sectors have the same mass $\Mkink$, regardless of winding number.
This is a non-trivial prediction: a theory with an arbitrary potential of lower symmetry
would generically have different kink masses for different sectors.

\subsection*{PSC-Admissible Sectors and the Standard Model Particle Assignments}

The $\ZS$ potential has seven vacuum states and thus six non-trivial kink sectors
($w \in \{1,2,3,4,5,6\}$).
Not all six are physically admissible.
PSC-admissibility (forced by the dark-mirror structure of $F_{21}$) restricts to
five sectors, eliminating $\{1,5\}$ (the dark-sector mirror partners, not stable
asymptotic states in the SM branch) \cite{SpivackPhiMDLField,SpivackCompleteness}.
The resulting five sectors $\{0,2,3,4,6\}$ map exactly to the SM particle classes.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}cclcc@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Winding $w$} & \textbf{Charge $Q = w_{\rm c}/3$} & \textbf{Particle type} &
\textbf{Statistics} & \textbf{Cert} \\
\midrule
$0$ & $0$ & Vacuum / photon / neutrino & bosonic & \CatAL \\
$2$ & $+2/3$ & Up-type quarks ($u,c,t$) & fermionic & \CatAL \\
$3$ & $+1$ & $W^+$ boson / positron & bosonic & \CatAL \\
$4$ & $-1$ & Charged leptons ($e^-,\mu^-,\tau^-$), $W^-$ & fermionic & \CatAL \\
$6$ & $-1/3$ & Down-type quarks ($d,s,b$) & fermionic & \CatAL \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent
The charge $Q = w_{\rm c}/3$ uses the centred representative
$w_{\rm c} = w$ for $w\leq 3$ and $w_{\rm c} = w-7$ for $w\geq 4$,
so that $w=4$ gives $Q = (4{-}7)/3 = -1$ (electron)
and $w=6$ gives $Q = (6{-}7)/3 = -1/3$ (down quark).
All charge assignments are algebraic consequences of the GTE polynomial;
no additional assignment rule is needed.

\textbf{Lean cert:} \lean{psc\_admissible\_sectors} (\CatAL, zero sorry, ugp-lean):
$\{0,2,3,4,6\}$ are the PSC-admissible sectors.

\subsection*{Three Generations from Three Orbit Types}

The three SM fermion generations correspond to the three non-vacuum orbit types
of the $\ZS^5$ ring under $f_{\rm MDL}$.
In 3+1D (three-tape architecture), particles are described by uniform winding triples
$(w,w,w)$ for $w$ in the PSC-admissible set --- all three spatial tapes carry the
same winding, ensuring rotational ($S_3$) invariance.
The generation index (first, second, third) is the orbit position within each
species class.
All three generations of charged leptons have $w=4$; the distinction between $e$, $\mu$,
$\tau$ is their generation index and the corresponding $b$-value from the GTE cascade,
not their winding number.

\noindent
Three generations arise from one field \cite{SpivackCMCA,SpivackThreeTapeCMCA}.
No additional field is needed for each generation.

\subsection*{Topological Stability and Superselection}

The topological charge is:
\[
Q_{\rm top} = \frac{7}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Phi'(x)\,dx
= \frac{7}{2\pi}\bigl[\Phi(+\infty)-\Phi(-\infty)\bigr] \in \mathbb{Z}_7.
\]

This charge is exactly conserved: changing it requires the field to globally
rewind, which costs infinite energy.
The $\ZS$ superselection rule follows:
$\mathcal{H} = \bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_7}\mathcal{H}_k$,
with no quantum tunneling between sectors
(tunneling amplitude $\sim e^{-S_E}$ with $S_E\approx 0.163$~GeV~$\gg 1$ in natural units).
Particle identity is an exact quantum number, not a statistical tendency.

\begin{openprob}
The kink width $1/m_\varphi \approx 0.111$~fm matches the QCD scale.
The derivation of the physical lepton mass $m_e = 0.511$~MeV as an energy of a specific
kink composite configuration in the three-tape $\PHIMDL$ field theory
--- going beyond the Level-1 orbit cascade --- is an open problem in the GTE programme.
The connection is structurally understood via the orbit cascade (\CatA) but has not
yet been derived directly from the $\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian.
\end{openprob}

\section{$F_{21}$ vs.\ SU(3): Why the Frobenius Group Is Forced}
\label{sec:f21}

Standard QCD uses $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ as the colour gauge group.
Why does GTE use $F_{21} = \ZS\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ instead?
The answer is MDL.

\subsection*{Why MDL Forces $F_{21}$ Over $\mathrm{SU}(3)$}

$\mathrm{SU}(3)$ is a continuous Lie group with infinitely many generators.
Postulating it as the colour gauge group costs many bits: you must specify that the
gauge group is $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ (as opposed to $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ for other $N$,
$\mathrm{U}(N)$, $\mathrm{Sp}(N)$, etc.), the coupling constant $g_s$, the
Casimir structure, and the representation assignments for each fermion species.

$F_{21} = \ZS\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ is the unique non-abelian group of order~21.
It has only 21 elements and is completely specified by its semidirect product structure.
The Frobenius prime identity certifies its arithmetic uniqueness:

\begin{equation}
|F_{21}| = |\ZS| = |\mathbb{Z}_3|^2 - |\mathbb{Z}_3| + 1 = 9 - 3 + 1 = 7.
\label{eq:frobenius_prime}
\end{equation}

This identity --- that 7 is the unique prime of the form $p^2 - p + 1$ for $p=3$ ---
certifies that $\ZS$ and $\mathbb{Z}_3$ form a Frobenius pair.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={Result: $F_{21}$ Is MDL-Forced (\CatA\ and \CatAL)}]
$F_{21} = \ZS\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ is the MDL-minimal group with the required properties:
$\ZS$ arithmetic (forced by the GTE generation orbit), $\mathbb{Z}_3$ colour
(required for anomaly cancellation with $N_{\rm gen}=3$), and non-abelian structure
(required for asymptotic freedom).
Replacing $F_{21}$ with $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ would cost $\geq 38$ additional MDL bits.

\smallskip\noindent
\textbf{Lean certs:}
\lean{algebraic\_necessity\_master\_bundle} (\CatAL, three GTE sector constraints, zero sorry, ugp-lean);
\lean{FrobeniusPrimeIdentity.lean} (\CatAL, zero sorry, ugp-lean).
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{$F_{21}^{\rm ab} = \mathbb{Z}_3$: Colour Emerges from the Abelianization}

The abelianization of $F_{21}$ is $F_{21}^{\rm ab} = F_{21}/[F_{21},F_{21}] = \mathbb{Z}_3$.
This $\mathbb{Z}_3$ is exactly the colour symmetry of QCD.
Quark colour indices $\in\{0,1,2\} = \mathbb{Z}_3$;
colour singlets have $\chi = 0$ (sum of colour charges $\equiv 0 \pmod{3}$).

The eight colour gluons of $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ emerge from $F_{21}$ Berry holonomy
on a single kink substrate: the $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ adjoint branching
$\mathbf{8} = \mathbf{1}'\oplus\mathbf{1}''\oplus\mathbf{3}\oplus\bar{\mathbf{3}}$
maps exactly to the GTE seven-vertex catalog.
The $\mathbf{3}\oplus\bar{\mathbf{3}}$ arise from the $\ZS$ winding-charge representations;
the $\mathbf{1}'\oplus\mathbf{1}''$ from the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ centre structure.
Lean: \lean{su3\_gluon\_charge\_vectors}, \lean{su3\_cmca\_master\_bundle} (\CatAL, zero sorry).
This mechanism is PROVISIONAL-STRONG (Case A confirmed; full holonomy construction
detailed in~\cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}).

\subsection*{Colour Confinement from MDL}

Free coloured quarks are MDL-forbidden.
Observing an isolated quark would require a description carrying
$\Delta K = \log_2(N_c^2) = \log_2 9 \approx 3.17$ extra bits to specify
which of the $N_c^2 = 9$ colour-octet states the quark is in.
MDL forbids this extra specification cost: colour must be confined.

\textbf{Lean cert:} \lean{color\_confinement\_k\_extra\_pos} (\CatAL, zero sorry, ugp-lean):
$\Delta K > 0$ for free coloured quarks;
\lean{psc\_forbids\_free\_colored\_quarks} (\CatAL, zero sorry): PSC-consistency
requires colour confinement.

\subsection*{Strong CP = 0 from $F_{21}$ Group Theory}

The strong CP problem --- why the QCD vacuum angle $\theta_{\rm QCD}$ is measured
to be less than $10^{-10}$ when the QCD Lagrangian allows it to be anything
from $0$ to $2\pi$ --- is resolved by $F_{21}$ group theory alone.
No axion is required.
Three independent \CatAL\ proofs establish $\theta_{\rm QCD} = 0$:

\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=3pt]
\item \textbf{Trivial centre}: $F_{21}$ has trivial centre, so the $\theta$-vacuum
  structure of continuum $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ does not survive the $F_{21}$ EFT.
\item \textbf{Rational phase}: the phase of $F_{21}$ representations is rational,
  so no $CP$-violating imaginary part can arise.
\item \textbf{Character sum}: the $F_{21}$ character sum equals zero modulo $\mathbb{Z}_3$,
  forbidding a $CP$-odd term in the effective Lagrangian.
\end{enumerate}

All three are machine-certified (ugp-lean, zero sorry).
The strong CP problem is not fine-tuning; it is an algebraic consequence of
MDL-minimal colour symmetry \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.

\subsection*{The No-CA-Replica Theorem: Why $\PHIMDL$ Is Unique}

We close this section with the uniqueness result that separates the \CMCA\ (algebraic
certificate) from $\PHIMDL$ (physical substrate) as two logically distinct objects.

For any finite lattice resolution $M$, the Nyquist residual
$\varepsilon_0(M) = \pi^2/(3M^2)$ measures the Lorentz-invariance error of a
discrete $M$-site lattice.
It is strictly positive for all finite $M$, and vanishes only in the $M\to\infty$ limit:

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}
\toprule
Lattice resolution & $\varepsilon_0(M) = \pi^2/(3M^2)$ & Interpretation \\
\midrule
$M=7$ (\CMCA\ scale) & $6.71\%$ & Significant error \\
$M=100$ & $0.33\%$ & Small error \\
$M=10^6$ & $3.3\times10^{-12}$ & Negligible but nonzero \\
$M\to\infty$ ($\PHIMDL$) & $0$ (exact) & Exact Lorentz invariance \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={Theorem: No-CA-Replica and $\PHIMDL$ Uniqueness (\CatAL)}]
For every finite lattice resolution $M>0$:
$\varepsilon_0(M) = \pi^2/(3M^2) > 0$.
No finite-resolution discrete model can exactly replicate $\PHIMDL$'s Lorentz invariance.
The $M\to\infty$ continuum limit is the unique exact Lorentz-invariant model
in the $\ZS$ class, and that limit is $\PHIMDL$.

\smallskip\noindent
\textbf{Lean certs:}
\lean{no\_finite\_ca\_exact\_lorentz\_replica} (\CatAL, zero sorry, ugp-lean):
$\varepsilon_0(M) > 0$ for all finite $M$.
\lean{phimdl\_is\_unique\_exact\_lorentz\_model} (\CatAL, zero sorry, ugp-lean):
$\PHIMDL$ is the unique zero-error model.
\lean{sr\_accuracy\_depends\_on\_M} (\CatAL, zero sorry): $\varepsilon_0(M)$ is
$M$-dependent and does not descend as algebraic content.
\end{tcolorbox}

The key consequence: the \CMCA\ at $M=7$ is not an approximation to $\PHIMDL$ for
\emph{algebraic} questions.
The Algebraic Descent Theorem certifies that all $F_{21}$ conservation laws are
$M$-independent.
$\PHIMDL$ is additionally required precisely for: exact Lorentz invariance, exact
kink masses, scattering amplitudes, and full quantum coherence.
The two levels are complementary, not competitive.

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{The M\"{o}bius Triple: One Object, Three Descriptions}
\label{sec:mobius}

Throughout this chapter we have introduced three apparently distinct objects: the \CMCA\
(Level-1 algebraic certificate), $\PHIMDL$ (Level-2 physical field), and $[D]$ (the
PSC-consistent coherence measure that selects outcomes during measurement).
A natural worry is that the theory posits three separate things where one should suffice.

This section explains why there are not three objects --- only one, viewed from three
different levels of description.
The GTE-Möbius architecture is the formal structure that makes this precise \cite{SpivackGTEMobius}.

\subsection*{The Triple $(A,e,[D])$}

The \textbf{GTE-Möbius architecture} is the triple $(A, e, [D])$ defined as follows:

\begin{description}[itemsep=4pt]
\item[$A$ --- the arithmetic carrier:]
The $\ZS$-indexed configuration space with winding dynamics, orbit structure, PSC-admissible
kink sectors $\{0,2,3,4,6\}$, and $f_{\rm MDL}$ update rule.
At Level~1, this is the abstract GTE orbit algebra.
At Level~2, $A$ is the PSC-admissible kink sector of $\PHIMDL$.
Properties: $\ZS$ winding alphabet; $\mathbb{Z}_5$ generation ring; GoE structure; generation orbit period~3;
SM particle classes $\{0,2,3,4,6\}$ --- all \CatAL.

\item[$e$ --- the self-encoding map:]
The map that makes $A$ capable of representing its own dynamics.
At Level~1, $e$ is the Rule~110 universality result: $f_{\rm MDL}$ can encode its own
update rule \mbox{(\lean{rule110\_z7\_poly\_rep}, \lean{phimdl\_turing\_universal}, \CatAL\ conditional)}.
The self-encoding map induces the Gödel-Turing boundary: once $A$ encodes its own dynamics,
undecidable arithmetic arises at the computation/transputation boundary.

\item[{$[D]$} --- the PSC-consistent coherence measure:]
The class of coherence measures on $\PHIMDL$'s kink configuration space satisfying five
constraints (positivity, Presentation Invariance, non-computable selection, selection
completeness in the physical sector $\{0,2,3,4,6\}$, Born-rule consistency).
$[D]$ is not a separate field or a third physical substrate.
It is the selection measure integrated into the PMDL action, specifying which
$\PHIMDL$ configuration is realised during a measurement event.
\end{description}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5,colframe=green!50!black,
  title={Definition: GTE-Möbius Architecture $(A,e,[D])$}]
The GTE-Möbius architecture is the $\PHIMDL$ field described as a computational
structure.
$A$ is the field's PSC-admissible kink sectors;
$e$ is its Turing-complete dynamics (the self-encoding that produces the computation/transputation
boundary);
$[D]$ is its PSC-consistent measurement adjudication (the Born-rule selection).
These are \emph{three descriptions of one physical object} --- not three physical objects.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{One Surface, Two Operational Modes}

The Möbius name captures the key insight: on a Möbius strip, what appears to be two
sides is actually one continuous surface.
What appears in GTE to be two separate processes --- deterministic $\PHIMDL$ evolution
and non-computable transputation --- is one continuous physical process on the same substrate.

The deterministic part ($e$, Rule~110 dynamics) and the adjudicative part ($[D]$, the
PSC-consistent measurement selector) share one physical substrate: $\PHIMDL$.
The apparent boundary between them (the Gödel-Turing boundary) is a \emph{semantic}
boundary, not a physical one.
The same field performs both operations; we cannot point to a region of spacetime
that is ``doing computation'' versus ``doing transputation'' --- the distinction is
a property of the record level, not the field level.

\subsection*{$[D]$ Is Not a Separate Physical Object}

This point requires emphasis because it prevents a significant misreading.

$[D]$ does not add new degrees of freedom to the theory.
It does not introduce a new field, a new substrate, or a new ontological layer.
It is the mechanism by which PSC-forced adjudication (transputation) operates
\emph{within} $\PHIMDL$'s already-existing dynamics.

Formally, $[D]$ enters as the weighting measure in the PMDL path integral:
\[
Z = \int\!\mathcal{D}[\Phi]\,e^{iS_{\rm PMDL}/\hbar}\,[D].
\]
This is not a second path integral over $[D]$; it is the standard path integral over
$\PHIMDL$ field configurations, with $[D]$ specifying the PSC-consistent measure on
that configuration space.
Full treatment: Chapter~\ref{chap:qm}.

\subsection*{The Correspondence Table}

The three perspectives of the same object are:

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}P{3.5cm}P{5.0cm}P{4.0cm}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Abstract (A,e,[D])} & \textbf{Physical ($\PHIMDL$)} & \textbf{Operational role} \\
\midrule
$A$ = $\ZS$ arithmetic carrier & PSC-admissible kink sectors & What the particles are \\
$e$ = self-encoding map & Klein-Gordon evolution (Rule~110 cert) & How the field evolves \\
$[D]$ = coherence measure class & PMDL action selection / PSC superselection & How measurements resolve \\
\midrule
$A\to e\to [D]$ dependency & $\PHIMDL$ field $\to$ dynamics $\to$ selection & Logical order \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent
The logical order is strict: the measurement selection $[D]$ operates on outcomes of
the dynamics $e$, which operates on configurations of the field $A$.
The three aspects do not refer back to each other across this order.

\subsection*{Practical Significance: Eliminating Apparent Redundancy}

The Möbius architecture resolves what would otherwise be a troubling apparent redundancy.
A reader might ask: if $\PHIMDL$ is the physical substrate, why do we need the \CMCA\
at all?
And if we have both the \CMCA\ and $\PHIMDL$, why do we also need $[D]$?

The answer: we do not need three separate things.
We need one field ($\PHIMDL$) described at three levels of abstraction:
\begin{itemize}[itemsep=3pt]
\item At the algebraic level, the field is the \CMCA\ --- the 19-bit polynomial and its
  orbit structure.
  This is where particle quantum numbers are proved.
\item At the dynamical level, the field is $\PHIMDL$ --- the $\ZS$ Klein-Gordon field with
  BPS kink solutions.
  This is where masses, Lorentz invariance, and the quantum state live.
\item At the epistemic level, the field's interaction with macroscopic records is
  mediated by $[D]$ --- the PSC-consistent coherence measure.
  This is where the Born rule and the measurement process are derived.
\end{itemize}

\noindent
No new ontological commitments are required beyond $\PHIMDL$ itself.
The three levels are three ways of asking different questions about the same object.

This closes the description of the one field.
Chapter~\ref{chap:particles} takes the particle assignments established here and derives
the complete Standard Model spectrum from first principles.

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Winding Sectors and Quantum Numbers}
\label{sec:winding_qn}

The $\ZS$ winding number is the particle's identity card in GTE.  Just as a knot is
classified by how many times a strand winds around an axis, each SM particle is classified
by how many steps its kink transitions between adjacent vacua of the $\PHIMDL$ potential.
The winding number $w \in \{0,1,2,3,4,5,6\}$ labels the 7 degenerate vacua.  Of these
7 values, only 5 appear as stable asymptotic states in the SM branch of the
PSC-consistent Hilbert space: $w \in \{0,2,3,4,6\}$.  The sectors $\{1,5\}$ are
PSC-forbidden in the SM branch; they belong to the dark-mirror sector
\cite{Spivack2025UGP} (App.~\ref{app:new_physics_predictions}).

\subsection*{The PSC-Admissible Sectors}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5,colframe=green!50!black,title={Definition: PSC-Admissible Winding Sector}]
A winding sector $w\in\ZS$ is \emph{PSC-admissible} if it appears as a stable
asymptotic state in the SM-branch PSC-consistent Hilbert space of $\PHIMDL$.
The five admissible sectors are $w\in\{0,2,3,4,6\}$.\\[2pt]
Lean certification: \lean{psc\_admissible\_sectors}, \lean{psc\_admissible\_card},
\lean{psc\_sectors\_partition} (\CatAL, zero sorry)
\cite{SpivackPhiMDLField,SpivackThreeTapeCMCA}.
\end{tcolorbox}

The derivation of this set uses the orbit structure of $F_{21} = \ZS \rtimes \mathbb{Z}_3$.
The 7 elements of $\ZS$ split into: the vacuum ($w=0$); the SM-admissible set
$\{2,3,4,6\}$ whose orbits under the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ Sylow subgroup reproduce the
SM color and charge assignments; and the dark-mirror set $\{1,5\}$, identified with
anti-quark mirror states in the dark sector \cite{Spivack2025UGP}.  The dark-mirror
identification is itself PSC-derived: sectors $\{1,5\}$ are stable in the dark branch
but not in the SM branch.  Appending the vacuum gives the five PSC-admissible sectors.

\subsection*{Electric Charge from the Polynomial: $Q = p(0,w,0)/3$}

Electric charge is not an independent assignment.  It is the degree-1 projection of
the GTE polynomial $p(L,C,R)$ evaluated at the center input $(L=R=0)$:
\begin{equation}
  3Q = p(0,\, w,\, 0) = w \pmod{7}.
  \label{eq:charge_formula}
\end{equation}
Physical charge uses the centered $\ZS$ representative to place $Q$ in the interval
$(-1,1]$:
\begin{equation}
  w_{\rm centered} = \begin{cases} w & w \leq 3,\\ w - 7 & w \geq 4,\end{cases}
  \qquad Q = \frac{w_{\rm centered}}{3}.
  \label{eq:centered}
\end{equation}
The proof is one line over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$: $p(0,w,0)=0+w-0\cdot w-0=w$.
Machine-certified as \lean{gte\_charge\_is\_linear\_projection} (\CatAL, zero sorry)
\cite{SpivackThreeTapeCMCA,SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.

Charge quantization --- the fact that all electric charges are multiples of $e/3$ ---
is therefore not a postulate but a theorem about the degree-1 polynomial projection.

Three features stand out.  First, every charge value in the SM corresponds to a distinct
winding sector; there are no free choices.  Second, the fermion/boson split follows
algebraically from the order of $w$ in $\ZS^*$ (see below).  Third, all three
generations of a given fermion species share the \emph{same} winding number;
the generations are distinguished by orbit position within the cascade, not by winding.

\subsection*{Color Charge from the $F_{21}$ Sylow-3 Subgroup}

Color charge is the second topological quantum number.  The internal symmetry group of
$\PHIMDL$ is the Frobenius group $F_{21} = \ZS \rtimes \mathbb{Z}_3$.  The Sylow-3
subgroup $\mathbb{Z}_3 \subset F_{21}^{ab}$ acts by $\sigma_i \mapsto g^3 \sigma_i$
(where $g=3$ is the generator of $\ZS^*$), and color charge is the unique additive
invariant under this action:
\begin{equation}
  Q_\chi = \sum_i \bigl[\log_3 \sigma_i \pmod{3}\bigr].
  \label{eq:color}
\end{equation}
This gives $Q_\chi \in \{0,1,2\}$ --- colorless, red, blue/green.  No SM color
assignment is used as input; color emerges from the arithmetic of $\ZS$.  The
identification $F_{21}^{ab} = \mathbb{Z}_3$ (pure color, zero CP-odd phase) is
machine-certified (\CatAL) and implies $\theta_{\rm QCD} = 0$ with three independent
proofs \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.

Leptons are colorless ($Q_\chi = 0$) as physical asymptotic states.  At Level~1 a
single lepton beable carries $Q_\chi = 1$; the observed $Q_\chi = 0$ results from
$[D]$-coherence selection over beable superpositions.  This color-neutralization
mechanism is understood structurally but the explicit $[D]$-weight computation is
open (\CatD).

\subsection*{Fermion/Boson Split from $\ZS^*$ Order}

The multiplicative group $\ZS^* = \{1,2,3,4,5,6\}$ has order 6.  A key structural
result assigns statistics from the group-theoretic order of $w$:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5,colframe=green!50!black,title={Theorem: Fermion/Boson Split (\CatAL)}]
The fermionic SM sectors are exactly the non-primitive roots of $\ZS^*$: $w\in\{2,4,6\}$
(orders 3, 3, 2 respectively).  The bosonic sector is $w=3$ (primitive root, order~6).
Lean certification: \lean{gte\_winding\_identifies\_charged\_fermions} (\CatAL, zero
sorry) \cite{SpivackThreeTapeCMCA,SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.
\end{tcolorbox}

The physical interpretation: an element of order 3 generates a subgroup of size 3 ---
one for each quark color.  An element of order 6 generates all of $\ZS^*$, corresponding
to the gauge bosons that carry the full $\ZS$ charge spectrum.  The vacuum sector
$w=0$ is outside $\ZS^*$ (it is the identity of $\ZS$ as an additive group) and
corresponds to the massless neutral sector.

\subsection*{Spin $J=\tfrac{1}{2}$ from Three-Kink Exchange Statistics}

Fermionic statistics --- and the spin value $J=1/2$ --- follow from the phase acquired
when two kinks are exchanged through a third.  The exchange introduces a factor of
$(-1)$, establishing Fermi--Dirac statistics.  Machine-certified as
\lean{gte\_triple\_kink\_exchange\_statistics} and
\lean{gte\_fermionic\_sectors\_get\_minus\_phase} (\CatAL) \cite{SpivackThreeTapeCMCA}.
The angular-momentum assignment $J^P = 1/2^+$ is then fixed by the beable statistics
(\CatAL) \cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.

In $3+1$D, each SM fermion is a \emph{uniform winding triple} $(w,w,w)$: all three
spatial tapes of the CMCA carry the same winding number, making the state
$S_3$-invariant and isotropic.

\subsection*{Baryon Number as a Topological Invariant}

Baryon number is not postulated in GTE.  It is the topological invariant:
\begin{equation}
  B = \frac{1}{3}\sum_{j} \chi_q(w_j),
  \label{eq:baryon_number}
\end{equation}
where the sum is over color charges of the three constituent kinks of a baryon.
Machine-certified: \lean{gte\_baryon\_number\_topological\_charge} (\CatAL)
\cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.

Because $B$ is a topological winding number, it is \emph{exactly} conserved:
no continuous deformation of the field can change it.  An immediate corollary
is that proton decay via dimension-4 baryon-number violating operators is
absolutely forbidden (\CatAL): baryon number is a topological charge of the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding field,
and topological charges are exactly conserved (\lean{gte\_baryon\_number\_topological\_charge},
\texttt{Universality/GUTStructure.lean}, zero sorry; \cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}).
The proton is topologically stable.
This is one of the sharpest predictions of the theory, falsifiable by a
single event at Hyper-Kamiokande or DUNE.

\subsection*{Braid Atlas and an Open Problem}

The Braid Atlas \cite{SpivackBraidAtlas} provides the full topological classification
of SM fermions as braid types, with generation index corresponding to braid crossing
count.  This chapter uses those results.

\begin{openprob}[The Compton-Scale CA Structure of Particles (Rank~2-ZGM)]
\label{oprob:glider_fermion}
The Algebraic Lifting Theorem guarantees that a physical observable with the quantum
numbers of each SM fermion exists at the Compton scale in $\PHIMDL$.
The question of \emph{what CA structure corresponds to each particle} is open ---
and the answer is almost certainly not a single glider.

Particles in $\PHIMDL$ are BPS topological kinks: collective, extended field configurations
whose width is set by the kink mass ($\sim\!1/m_\varphi \sim 10^{-13}$~cm, hadronic scale).
This is twenty orders of magnitude larger than the Planck-scale lattice spacing of the CMCA.
At the resolutions fine enough for individual-cell dynamics to matter, the proton Compton
wavelength alone corresponds to order $10^{13}$--$10^{15}$ lattice cells
(\cite{SpivackCompUniversality}, P28 §Physical Substrate).
A single SM fermion at the Compton scale is therefore an enormously complex collective
excitation of the CA --- not a single Rule~110 glider, but a many-body configuration of
perhaps trillions of interacting cells.

The open problem is not identifying a single glider type per particle, but characterising the
class of many-body CA configurations whose collective behaviour lifts, via the Algebraic
Lifting Theorem, to a BPS kink with the correct winding number, charge, and spin.
This is analogous to the relationship between individual atomic vibrations and a phonon:
the phonon is a collective normal mode of the entire lattice, not an individual atomic
displacement.  Direct CA simulation of such a configuration at the required scale is
computationally intractable with current technology, but the algebraic certificates
(winding sectors, orbit types, topological charges) that constrain the answer are fully
established at the Level~1 CMCA level.
\end{openprob}

\section{Three Generations: Why Exactly Three}
\label{sec:three_gen}

The number of fermion generations is one of the deepest unexplained features of the SM.
Three families of quarks and leptons appear experimentally; the SM offers no explanation
for this number.  GTE derives $\Ngen = 3$ from four independent mechanisms that all
converge on the same answer.  Full derivations appear in earlier chapters; we summarize
the arguments and state their certification status.

\subsection*{Four Independent Mechanisms}

\textbf{Mechanism~1: Garden-of-Eden orbit depth (\CatAL).}
The generation-1 triple $(1, 73, 823)$ is a \emph{Garden of Eden} (GoE) state under
the $f_{\rm MDL}$ update map: it has no CA predecessor.  The Lean theorem
\lean{garden\_of\_eden\_gen1} (\CatAL) \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure,SpivackBraidAtlas}
establishes this property.  The orbit of the gen$_1$ triple under $f_{\rm MDL}$
has period exactly 3: $\text{gen}_1 \to \text{gen}_2 \to \text{gen}_3 \to \text{gen}_1$.
This period is not an input; it is forced by $\ZS$ arithmetic via the Lawvere
fixed-point theorem.  $\Ngen = 3$ is the orbit period, certified by
\lean{three\_generations\_physical}, \lean{sm\_period3\_orbit\_chain} (\CatAL)
\cite{SpivackBraidAtlas,SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.

\textbf{Mechanism~2: QCD $\beta$-function integrality (\CatAL).}
Asymptotic freedom requires $b_0 > 0$ to be a positive integer.  The Casimir relation
(Ch.~\ref{chap:forces}, eq.~\eqref{eq:casimir}) gives
$b_0 = \Nfam + \Ngen - 1 = 5 + \Ngen - 1$.  The identity $b_0 = |\ZS| = 7$
(machine-certified as \lean{casimir\_relation\_arithmetic}, \CatAL
\cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}) then forces $\Ngen = 3$.

\textbf{Mechanism~3: PSC Layer~II minimum complexity (\CatAD conditional).}
A PSC enumeration of $\mathbb{Z}_N \times \mathbb{Z}_M$ internal symmetry candidates
begins with $34{,}560$ candidates and reduces to 12 PSC-Layer-I survivors.  Among these,
the minimum-complexity solution under the Physical Information criterion ($\equiv \MDL$)
has $\Ngen = 3$.  Lean certification: \lean{psc\_enumeration\_forces\_ngen\_3} (\CatAL)
\cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.  The identification of the minimum-PI solution with our
universe is \CatAD.

\textbf{Mechanism~4: Cosmological constant bracket uniqueness (\CatAD).}
As shown in Ch.~\ref{chap:cosmology}, the $\Omega_\Lambda$ bracket from the PSC
adjudication of two independent routes contains the Planck~2018 value
$\Omega_\Lambda = 0.6889 \pm 0.0056$ only for $\Ngen = 3$.  For other values the
bracket misses the Planck measurement.

All four mechanisms agree on $\Ngen = 3$.  Mechanisms~1--2 are unconditionally \CatAL;
Mechanisms~3--4 are \CatAD.  No other value of $\Ngen$ satisfies all four constraints.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Theorem: Three-Generation Uniqueness (\CatAL)}]
Every universe consistent with the five Layer~I PSC axioms (RC, NM*, TV, SA, AC) has
exactly $N_{\rm gen} = 3$ fermion generations. An exhaustive enumeration of all 34,560
candidate gauge topologies confirms that every PSC survivor has $N_{\rm gen}=3$; none has
$N_{\rm gen}=2$ or $N_{\rm gen}\geq 4$.

\textbf{Lean cert:} \lean{psc\_enumeration\_forces\_ngen\_3}
(\texttt{native\_decide}, \texttt{TE22/ScanCertificate.lean}, \texttt{ugp-lean}, zero sorry).
This is machine-verified by exhaustive computation: the \lean{native\_decide} tactic compiles
the enumeration to native code and evaluates it mechanically.
\end{tcolorbox}

with seed $(1, 73, 823)$.  The lepton seed is itself certified by the FGCI theorem
(Ch.~\ref{chap:selection}): $F(n) = n^4 - n^2 + 1 = G(n) = n^4 - (n^2+1)/2 - n$ if
and only if $n = \Nc = 3$ (\lean{fgci\_unique\_at\_nc}, \lean{fgci\_bundle}, \CatAL)
\cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Theorem: $N_{\rm gen}=3$ Three-Mechanism Unified Certificate (\CatAL)}]

period-3 chain gen${}_1\to$gen${}_2\to$gen${}_3\to$vacuum
(\lean{three\_generations\_physical}).

\textbf{Mechanism~2 (Casimir/$\beta$-function):}
$b_0 = |\mathbb{Z}_7|=7$ and $N_{\rm fam}=5$ uniquely determine $N_{\rm gen}$ via
$b_0=N_{\rm fam}+N_{\rm gen}-1$; parametric uniqueness: \lean{ngen\_unique\_from\_casimir}
(omega, zero sorry).

\textbf{Mechanism~3 (PSC Layer~I enumeration):}
Every PSC-admissible universe has $N_{\rm gen}=3$
(\lean{psc\_enumeration\_forces\_ngen\_3}, \texttt{native\_decide}).

Mechanism~4 (cosmological constant bracket) remains \CatAD.
Uniqueness corollary: \lean{ngen\_three\_is\_unique\_catal}.

\section{The Hadronic Sector}
\label{sec:hadrons}

The SM cannot compute hadron masses from its own inputs.  Even with QCD established
as the correct theory of the strong force, deriving $m_\pi$ or $m_\omega$ from first
principles requires lattice QCD --- an approximate numerical computation.  GTE derives
the pion mass and $\omega$ meson mass in closed form from structural constants of
$\PHIMDL$, with zero hadronic inputs.

\subsection*{The GTE Chiral Condensate}

The QCD vacuum is characterized by the chiral condensate $\langle\bar\psi\psi\rangle$
--- the order parameter of spontaneous chiral symmetry breaking.  In GTE, this
condensate is identified with the kink mass cubed:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5,colframe=green!50!black,title={Definition: GTE Chiral Condensate (\CatAL)}]
\[
  \langle\bar\psi\psi\rangle_{\rm GTE} = -\Mkink^3 = -(290.10~\text{MeV})^3.
\]
Lean certification: \lean{chiral\_condensate\_kink\_identification},
\lean{pion\_mass\_from\_gor} (\CatAL, zero sorry) \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.
\end{tcolorbox}

This identification is not a postulate.  The lowest-energy state of $\PHIMDL$ consistent
with the PSC is a condensate of domain walls (kinks); by the BPS saturation condition
the condensate energy density is $\Mkink^3$ per unit volume.  The numerical value
$-(290.10~\text{MeV})^3$ is approximately $6.5\%$ above the lattice FLAG~2024
value $-(272~\text{MeV})^3$; this offset reflects higher-order kink--kink interaction
corrections not included in the leading-order identification.

\subsection*{The Pion Mass from the Gell-Mann--Oakes--Renner Relation}

The Gell-Mann--Oakes--Renner (GOR) relation expresses the pion mass squared in terms
of the quark masses and the chiral condensate:
\[
  m_\pi^2 f_\pi^2 = -(m_u + m_d)\,\langle\bar\psi\psi\rangle.
\]
GTE supplies all inputs from its own arithmetic.  The pion decay constant follows
directly from the kink mass:
\begin{equation}
  f_\pi^{\rm GTE} = \frac{\Mkink}{\pi} = \frac{290.10}{\pi} = 92.34~\text{MeV}
  \qquad (\CatAL;\;\lean{fpi\_from\_scc}).
  \label{eq:fpi}
\end{equation}
This agrees with the PDG~2024 value $f_\pi = 92.1$~MeV at $0.26\%$ accuracy.
GTE uses the non-relativistic (NR) convention for decay constants throughout;
the PDG relativistic convention gives $f_\pi^{\rm rel} = f_\pi\sqrt{2} = 130.59$~MeV,
within $0.90\sigma$ of the PDG value $130.41\pm0.20$~MeV.

Substituting the GTE light quark masses ($m_u = 2.157$~MeV, $m_d = 4.647$~MeV, both
\CatAD; \lean{m\_u\_pdg\_interval}, \lean{m\_d\_pdg\_interval})
and the GTE chiral condensate into the GOR relation:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Headline Result: Pion Mass (\CatAL)}]
\[
  m_\pi^{\rm GTE} = 139.57~\text{MeV}.
\]
PDG~2024: $m_\pi^\pm = 139.5703$~MeV.  Deviation: $\mathbf{0.00\sigma}$ ($0.001\%$).
Zero free hadronic parameters.\\[2pt]
Lean certifications: \lean{pion\_mass\_numerical\_certificate},
\lean{pion\_mass\_from\_gor} (\CatAL, zero sorry)
\cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure,SpivackThreeTapeCMCA}.\\[2pt]
\emph{This is the first closed-form derivation of a hadron mass from a unified
framework, using no hadronic inputs.}
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{The Casimir Relation and Vector Meson Couplings}

The one-loop QCD $\beta$-function coefficient is a topological count of the $F_{21}$
orbit structure:
\begin{equation}
  b_0 = \Nfam + \Ngen - 1 = 5 + 3 - 1 = 7 = |\ZS|.
  \label{eq:casimir}
\end{equation}
Machine-certified (\CatAL; \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}):
\lean{casimir\_relation\_arithmetic}, \lean{b0\_casimir\_relation},
\lean{CasimirB0Relation.lean}.  The Casimir
orbit ratio recovers the KSRF parameter $a=2$:
\begin{equation}
  \frac{C(7,3)}{C(7,2)} = \frac{35}{21} = \frac{5}{3}, \qquad a = 2
  \quad (\CatAL;\;\lean{casimir\_orbit\_ratio}).
  \label{eq:casimir_ratio}
\end{equation}
The vector meson coupling constants follow from orbit counting within $F_{21}$:
\begin{align}
  g_\omega^2 &= \Ngen \times c_Z = 3 \times 12 = 36
    && (\CatAL;\;\lean{g\_omega\_sq\_eq\_ngen\_times\_cz}),
    \label{eq:g_omega}\\
  g_\rho^2 &= C(7,3) = 35
    && (\text{arithmetic \CatAL};\;\lean{g\_rho\_sq\_eq\_casimir\_orbit}).
    \label{eq:g_rho}
\end{align}
Here $c_Z = 12$ is the $Z$-sector orbit count derived from the $F_{21}$ Casimir
structure.

\subsection*{The $\omega$ and $\rho$ Meson Masses from KSRF}

The KSRF relation $m_V = g_V f_\pi^{\rm rel}$ (using the relativistic convention
$f_\pi^{\rm rel} = 130.59$~MeV) gives:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Headline Result: $\omega$ Meson Mass (\CatAL)}]
\[
  m_\omega = \sqrt{g_\omega^2}\cdot f_\pi^{\rm rel}
  = 6 \times 130.59~\text{MeV} = 783.54~\text{MeV}.
\]
PDG~2024: $m_\omega = 782.65$~MeV.  Deviation: $+0.11\%$.\\[2pt]
Lean certifications: \lean{m\_omega\_pdg\_agreement}, \lean{MesonMasses.lean} (\CatAL,
partial) \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure,SpivackGTEUnification}.
\end{tcolorbox}

The $\rho$ meson mass from $g_\rho^2 = C(7,3) = 35$ gives:
\begin{equation}
  m_\rho = \sqrt{35} \times 130.59~\text{MeV} = 772.57~\text{MeV}
  \qquad\text{PDG: } 775.26~\text{MeV},\quad{-0.35\%} \quad (\CatB).
  \label{eq:m_rho}
\end{equation}

The $\phi$ meson involves the strange-quark correction and the kaon decay constant:
\begin{equation}
  m_\phi = 1011.3~\text{MeV} \qquad\text{PDG: }1019.46~\text{MeV},\quad{-0.80\%}
  \quad (\CatB).
\end{equation}

The $\rho$ and $\phi$ meson masses are \CatB: the arithmetic of $g_V^2$ is Lean-certified
(\CatAL for the integers; \lean{g\_rho\_sq\_eq\_casimir\_orbit}), but the formal
derivation of the KSRF coupling constants from the $\PHIMDL$ Hidden Local Symmetry (HLS)
Lagrangian is not yet complete.

\begin{openprob}[OQ-HVP-6: $\PHIMDL\to\text{HLS}$ Coupling]
\label{oprob:hvp6}
The formal derivation --- via a path-integral reduction of the $\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian onto
an HLS effective theory --- of the mode-counting formula giving $g_\rho^2 = 35$ and
$g_\omega^2 = 36$ is open.  Until this derivation is complete, the meson coupling
constants remain \CatB for their physical mechanism, even though the arithmetic
values are \CatAL.
\end{openprob}

\subsection*{QCD String Tension}

The GTE QCD string tension follows from the kink mass scale:
\begin{equation}
  \sigma_{\rm string} = \frac{9}{4}\Mkink^2
  = \frac{9}{4}(290.10~\text{MeV})^2 = 0.18920~\text{GeV}^2.
  \label{eq:string_tension}
\end{equation}
This is within $0.04\sigma$ of the $\text{SU}(3)$ lattice QCD value \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure},
certified at \CatAD.  The prefactor $9/4 = (N_c^2)/N_c = N_c$ comes from the
Casimir factor of the adjoint representation.
