\section{Charged Lepton and Quark Masses}
\label{sec:fermion_masses}

Nine charged fermion masses --- three leptons and six quarks, spanning six orders of
magnitude --- emerge from a single arithmetic cascade with zero continuous parameters
fitted at prediction time.  This section presents the cascade mechanism, the nine
predictions, and the null test that confirms the agreement reflects genuine arithmetic
structure \cite{Spivack2026_CyclotomicMass}.

What makes this result striking, in my view, is not merely its numerical accuracy
but what it represents architecturally.
The Standard Model takes all nine charged fermion masses as freely adjustable inputs,
assigning no theory whatsoever to their values or ratios.
GTE has no such freedom at prediction time: once the Self-Consistency Condition fixes
$m_\tau$, the cascade produces the remaining eight masses without further adjustment.
The $0.293\%$ RMS agreement is therefore a genuine prediction --- a test the framework
could in principle have failed.
That it passes, across six orders of magnitude and both the leptonic and quark sectors
simultaneously, is the strongest quantitative case the theory has to offer in the
particle-mass domain.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=blue!5,colframe=blue!50!black,
  title={How GTE Derives Particle Masses}]
The GTE mass prediction uses the \emph{InformationMassTransformer}: each SM fermion is
associated with a triple $(a, b, c)$ from the GTE arithmetic cascade at ridge level
$n = 10$.  The $N_{\rm eff}$ value is the $b$-component (ladder index) of the triple.
For the lepton seed $(1, 73, 823)$, the $b$-value gives $N_{\rm eff}^{(e)} = 73$.
Two applications of the GTE map $T$ produce all three lepton $N_{\rm eff}$ values:
\[
  N_{\rm eff}^{(e)} = 73,\quad N_{\rm eff}^{(\mu)} = 42,\quad N_{\rm eff}^{(\tau)} = 275.
\]
These are not free parameters: they are the orbit outputs of $T$ at ridge $n = 10$,
machine-certified.
Machine-certified \mbox{(\lean{update\_map\_produces\_canonical\_orbit}, \CatAL)}.
The self-consistency condition (SCC) fixes the single mass anchor: the tau mass
$m_\tau = 1776.86$~MeV is the unique fixed point of the lepton cascade.  All nine
charged fermion masses follow from $m_\tau$ and the InformationMassTransformer with
zero additional free parameters.  Predicted vs.\ observed RMS: $0.293\%$ across all
nine fermions (\CatAD).
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{The Lepton Cascade: Three $N_{\rm eff}$ Values}

The GTE mass formula maps each fermion triple $(b_1, b_2, b_3)$ to a mass via the
InformationMassTransformer.  The lepton triples are:
\begin{itemize}[nosep]
\item \textbf{Generation 1 (electron):} triple $(1, 73, 823)$, $N_{\rm eff} = 73$.
\item \textbf{Generation 2 (muon):} $N_{\rm eff} = 42$ (odd-step update of the cascade
  from $n=10$, seed).
\item \textbf{Generation 3 (tau):} $N_{\rm eff} = 275$ (even-step update,
  \lean{update\_map\_produces\_canonical\_orbit}, \CatAL) \cite{Spivack2025UGP}.
\end{itemize}

The lepton seed $N_{\rm eff} = 73$ is fixed by the FGCI condition at $\Nc = 3$
(Ch.~\ref{chap:selection}).  The $b$-values $\{73, 42, 275\}$ are the orbit outputs
of the update map at ridge $n = 10$; they are not free parameters.

\subsection*{The InformationMassTransformer: From Arithmetic to Physical Mass}
\label{subsec:imt_formula}

The InformationMassTransformer is the map from GTE integer triples to physical fermion
masses.  This section gives the complete formula so that a reader of P48 alone can
reproduce any mass prediction from the cascade output.

\subsubsection*{The Universal Calibration Law}

Each SM fermion is associated with a triple $(a, b, c)$ from the GTE cascade.  The
\emph{calibration factor} $\mathcal{C}_f$ converts the triple's arithmetic content into a
dimensionless mass ratio relative to the kink mass scale $\Mkink$.  Define first the
feature vector:
\begin{equation}
  L(a,b,c) = \log\!\left(\frac{|b|}{|c|}\right),
  \label{eq:ucl_L}
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
  \boldsymbol{\phi}(a,b,c) = \bigl(1,\; L,\; L^2,\; g,\; g^2,\; M,\;
    \mu(a),\; \mu(b),\; \mu(|c|)\bigr),
  \label{eq:ucl_phi}
\end{equation}
where $g$ is the GTE generation index, $\mu(\cdot)$ is the M\"obius function
($\mu(n) = 0$ if $n$ has a squared prime factor, $(-1)^k$ if $n$ is a product of $k$
distinct primes), and $M = \mu(a)\,\mu(b)\,\mu(|c|)$ is the M\"obius product.

The \emph{Universal Calibration Law} (UCL) is:
\begin{equation}
  \log \mathcal{C}_f(a,b,c) = \mathbf{k} \cdot \boldsymbol{\phi}(a,b,c),
  \label{eq:ucl}
\end{equation}
where $\mathbf{k} \in \mathbb{R}^9$ is the same coefficient vector for all fermions,
locked during verification.  The physical mass is then:
\begin{equation}
  m_f = \mathcal{C}_f(a_f, b_f, c_f) \times \Mkink, \qquad
  \Mkink = \tfrac{8}{49}\,m_\tau = 290.10~\text{MeV}.
  \label{eq:mass_from_cf}
\end{equation}

\subsubsection*{The Elegant Kernel}

The coefficient vector $\mathbf{k}$ is not a free parameter fitted to masses; it is
a rigid algebraic structure dictated by GTE symmetries.  The Quarter-Lock Law
(\lean{quarterLockLaw}, \CatAL, zero sorry) forces the exact relation
$k_M = k_{\rm gen2} + \tfrac{1}{4}k_{L^2}$ among the coefficients.  Combined with
additional UGP symmetry constraints, this fixes $\mathbf{k}$ to the
\textbf{Elegant Kernel}:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=yellow!5,colframe=yellow!75!black,
  title={\textbf{The Elegant Kernel} (UCL coefficient vector, \CatA)}]
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\;\begin{aligned}
k_{L^2} &= \tfrac{7}{512}, \qquad & k_{\rm gen2} &= -\tfrac{\varphi}{2}, \\[3pt]
k_{\rm gen} &= \varphi\cos\!\bigl(\tfrac{\pi}{10}\bigr), \qquad &
k_M &= -\tfrac{\varphi}{2} + \tfrac{7}{2048}, \\[3pt]
(k_a, k_b, k_c) &= \bigl(\tfrac{1}{8},\, -\tfrac{3}{2},\, \tfrac{4}{3}\bigr), \qquad &
k_0' &= -\tfrac{1}{2\pi},
\end{aligned}\;}
\label{eq:elegant_kernel_p48}
\end{equation}
where $\varphi = (1+\sqrt{5})/2$ is the golden ratio.
The linear coefficient $k_L$ is fixed by the base-change convention that rationalizes $k_{L^2}$.
The full derivation is in \cite{Spivack2025UGP}; the Quarter-Lock identity is
machine-checked as \lean{quarterLockLaw} (ugp-lean, zero sorry).
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsubsection*{Why the Tau Mass Is the Single Anchor}

The UCL maps triples to dimensionless calibration factors; physical masses require one
dimensional anchor.  That anchor is $m_\tau$, which is the unique self-consistency
condition (SCC) fixed point of the lepton cascade: the field mass
$m_\varphi \equiv m_\tau$ is the value at which the cascade's own output, fed back
as the potential parameter, reproduces itself
(\lean{mphi\_equals\_tau\_mass\_scc}, \CatAL, zero sorry).
All 9 fermion mass predictions therefore follow from
(i) the GTE cascade triples, (ii) the Elegant Kernel, and (iii) $m_\tau$ as the single
dimensional anchor --- with zero continuously adjustable parameters at prediction time.

A concrete worked example: for the electron, the lepton seed triple is $(1, 73, 823)$,
so $L = \log(73/823) = \log(0.0887) \approx -2.424$, $g=1$, $\mu(1)=1$, $\mu(73)=-1$
(73 is prime), $\mu(823)=-1$ (823 is prime).
The UCL with the Elegant Kernel gives $\log \mathcal{C}_f \approx -7.00$, or
$\mathcal{C}_f \approx 9\times10^{-4}$; multiplying by $\Mkink = 290.10$~MeV gives
$m_e \approx 0.51$~MeV (cf.\ PDG: $0.511$~MeV, $+0.40\%$).

\subsection*{The Tau Mass Anchor and the SCC}

The tau mass $m_\tau = 1776.86$~MeV is the self-consistency condition (SCC) fixed point
of the lepton cascade.  The field mass $m_\varphi \equiv m_\tau$ is the parameter that
fixes the $\PHIMDL$ potential (Ch.~\ref{chap:field}), and the BPS kink mass is then
$\Mkink = (8/49)m_\tau = 290.10$~MeV.  All subsequent fermion mass predictions
use $m_\tau$ as the single anchor.  Since $m_\tau$ is an output of the SCC (not a
free fit), the number of continuously adjustable parameters at prediction time is zero.

\subsection*{The 1000-Permutation Null Test}

The nine-fermion agreement at $0.293\%$ RMS with zero parameters could in principle
be a coincidence of the arithmetic form.  To test this, the mass formula was applied
to 1000 random permutations of the orbit arithmetic --- the same functional form with
scrambled inputs.  The null distribution of RMS values has mean $\sim 45\%$ and
standard deviation $\sim 12\%$; the actual $0.293\%$ lies more than $3.8\sigma$ below
the null mean.  This rules out coincidence at high significance and confirms that the
agreement reflects genuine arithmetic structure in the cascade, not a forgiving
parametric ansatz \cite{Spivack2025UGP}.

\subsection*{Honest Disclosure: Certification Status}

The nine lepton and quark masses are \CatAD (computational) with Lean-certified
containment intervals for all six quarks.  The connection between the orbit cascade
$b$-values and the physical quark masses involves the GTE cascade at ridge $n=10$
and quark seed triples; these steps are computational (\CatA) and the full analytic
derivation has not been formalized in Lean.  The lepton seed $(1,73,823)$ is
\CatAL via the FGCI theorem; the cascade arithmetic beyond the seed is \CatA.

\section{Neutrino Masses and Mixing}
\label{sec:neutrinos}

The neutrino sector is the most precisely confirmed domain for GTE predictions beyond
the SM.  The mass-squared ratio $\Delta m^2_{21}/\Delta m^2_{31}$ is predicted to
$0.16\sigma$ from NuFIT~6.0 without any neutrino mass input.  All three PMNS mixing
angles are predicted from orbit-ratio formulas with zero free parameters.
All comparisons use \textbf{NuFIT~6.0 (IC24, NH)} \cite{NuFIT60}.

\subsection*{Normal Ordering Predicted}

The GTE seesaw structure gives normal mass ordering ($m_1 < m_2 < m_3$) as the natural
outcome.  The Braid Atlas $b$-values $\{5, 11, 19\}$ for the neutrino sector satisfy
$b_1 < b_2 < b_3$, which directly implies normal ordering via the mass-squared formula
(eq.~\eqref{eq:mnu_ratio} below).  Inverted ordering would require $b_2 < b_1$, which
violates the orbit-ordering condition.  Normal ordering is thus predicted, and NuFIT~6.0
IC24 disfavors inverted ordering by $\Delta\chi^2 = 6.1$ ($\approx 2.5\sigma$), consistent with
the GTE prediction.

\subsection*{Mass-Squared Ratio from Braid Atlas $b$-Values}

The neutrino mass-squared ratio is derived from the Braid Atlas $b$-values using the
structural exponent $29/9 = \Nc + \theta_{\rm Koide}$ (established independently in
the charged-lepton sector \cite{Spivack2026_Koide}):
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\Delta m^2_{21}}{\Delta m^2_{31}}
  = \frac{b_2^{58/9} - b_1^{58/9}}{b_3^{58/9} - b_1^{58/9}}
  = \frac{11^{58/9} - 5^{58/9}}{19^{58/9} - 5^{58/9}}
  = 0.02936.
  \label{eq:mnu_ratio}
\end{equation}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Headline Result: Neutrino Mass-Squared Ratio (\CatAL)}]
\[
  \Delta m^2_{21}/\Delta m^2_{31} = 0.02936\quad \text{(GTE)},
  \quad 0.02951\pm0.00098\quad \text{(NuFIT~6.0 IC24 NH)}.
\]
Deviation: $\mathbf{0.16\sigma}$ ($0.52\%$).  Zero neutrino mass inputs.\\[2pt]
Lean certification: \lean{delta\_m21\_sq\_to\_delta\_m31\_sq\_ratio} (\CatAL,
zero sorry, zero custom axioms) \cite{SpivackNeutrino}.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{Absolute Neutrino Masses}

Using the GUT-scale seesaw with right-handed neutrino mass $M_R$ derived from the
GTE orbit structure:
\begin{align}
  \Sigma m_\nu &= 59.4~\text{meV} \quad (\CatA),
  \label{eq:mnu_sum}\\
  m_{\nu_1} &= 0.679~\text{meV} \quad (\CatA),
\end{align}
consistent with the Planck~2018 upper bound $\Sigma m_\nu < 120$~meV (95\% CL) \cite{SpivackNeutrino}.
The normal-ordering value $\Sigma m_\nu \approx 60$~meV is within the target
sensitivity of CMB-S4 and Euclid.

which is within $1\sigma$.  The Lagrangian derivation establishing this NLO
correction as \CatAD is under investigation.

\subsection*{PMNS CP Phase and Jarlskog Invariant}

The PMNS CP phase derives from the $\ZS$ orbit fraction:
\begin{equation}
  \delta_{\rm CP}^{\rm PMNS} = \frac{4}{7}\times 360^\circ = 205.71^\circ
  \quad (\lean{pmns\_cp\_phase\_from\_z7\_winding},\;\CatAL).
  \label{eq:delta_cp}
\end{equation}
NuFIT~6.0 IC24 NH best fit: $212^\circ\,{}^{+26}_{-41}$.  Deviation: $-0.15\sigma$.
DUNE will constrain $\delta_{\rm CP}$ to $\pm10$--$15^\circ$, providing a decisive test.

The Jarlskog CP-invariant is:
\begin{equation}
  J_{\rm GTE} = -\frac{8184\,\sqrt{437}}{5057221}\sin(\pi/7) = -1.468\times10^{-2}
  \quad (\CatAL;\;\lean{gte\_jarlskog}).
\end{equation}
The denominator factorizes as $5057221 = 13\times73^3 = c_H \times b_{L_1}^3$, a pure
GTE orbit identity \cite{SpivackNeutrino}.  Note: the discrepancy with the PDG value
$J\approx -9.87\times10^{-3}$ ($49\%$) traces entirely to the difference between
the GTE $\delta_{\rm CP} = 205.71^\circ$ and the PDG best-fit $\delta\approx197^\circ$,
not to errors in the mixing angle formulas.

\subsection*{Leptogenesis Feasibility}

The efficiency parameter for thermal leptogenesis via the FKTT mechanism
(Ch.~\ref{chap:cosmology}) is:
\begin{equation}
  K_1 = 15.93
  \quad (\CatA).
  \label{eq:K1}
\end{equation}
This value is in the strong-washout regime ($K_1 \gg 1$), which is compatible with
the observed baryon asymmetry $\eta_B = 6.10\times10^{-10}$ when the FKTT coupling
$g_{\rm kink\text{-}top} = \varepsilon_{\rm FN}$ is used (Ch.~\ref{chap:cosmology}).
Approximately $77\%$ of natural Yukawa texture variants consistent with the orbit
structure are also consistent with the observed baryon asymmetry.

\begin{openprob}[Absolute Neutrino Mass Scale from First Principles]
\label{oprob:nu_mass}
The absolute neutrino mass scale $\Sigma m_\nu = 59.4$~meV is derived from the seesaw
with $M_R$ fixed by the GTE orbit structure (\CatA).  A Lean-certified derivation of
$M_R$ from first principles (without the intermediate orbit calibration) is open.
The mass-squared ratio and mixing angles are \CatAL/\CatAD; the absolute scale
is \CatA.
\end{openprob}

\section{EFT Operator Decomposition at $M_{\rm GUT}$}
\label{sec:eft_gut}

At the GUT scale $M_{\rm GUT} \approx 1.9\times10^{16}$~GeV, the $\PHIMDL$ kink
background generates effective operators that connect the particle spectrum to the
baryogenesis mechanism of Chapter~\ref{chap:cosmology}.  This section presents the
two-operator decomposition and explains why the operators do not mix.

\subsection*{Two Effective Operators at $M_{\rm GUT}$}

The $\PHIMDL$ field at the GUT scale supports two distinct vertex types, organized
by their topological charge $Q_{\rm top}$ under the $\ZS$ winding structure:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5,colframe=green!50!black,title={EFT Operator Decomposition at $M_{\rm GUT}$ (\CatAD)}]
\begin{align*}
O_1 &= \bar{q}_L \tilde{H} t_R + \text{h.c.},\quad Q_{\rm top} = 0,
  \quad c_1 = y_t = 0.461 \quad (\CatAD), \\
O_2 &= \bar{t}_R \PHIMDL N_R + \text{h.c.},\quad Q_{\rm top} = 1,
  \quad c_2 = g_{\rm kink\text{-}top} = \varepsilon_{\rm FN} \quad (\CatAL).
\end{align*}
Here $\varepsilon_{\rm FN} = e^{-\pi/\Nc} = e^{-\pi/3}$ is the Frobenius--Nijenhuis
suppression factor.  Lean certification of $c_2 = \varepsilon_{\rm FN}$:
\lean{kink\_top\_coupling\_eq\_eps\_FN} (\CatAL, zero sorry, zero axioms)
\cite{SpivackGTECosmology}.
\end{tcolorbox}

The two operators are structurally distinct.  $O_1$ is the standard Yukawa operator
that gives the top quark its physical mass $m_t = y_t v = 0.461 \times 246$~GeV~$= 113$~GeV
at the Higgs VEV $v = 246$~GeV ($m_t$ in the $\overline{\rm MS}$ scheme at $\mu=m_t$;
the on-shell pole mass $172.6$~GeV includes QCD corrections).  $O_2$ is the kink--top
coupling that generates the Dirac Yukawa entry $m_{D_1}$ in the seesaw, which in turn
drives the FKTT leptogenesis mechanism of Ch.~\ref{chap:cosmology}.

\subsection*{Why $O_1$ and $O_2$ Do Not Mix}

The key structural fact preventing mixing between $O_1$ and $O_2$ is $\ZS$ baryon
number conservation.  $O_1$ has topological charge $Q_{\rm top} = 0$: it conserves
the total $\ZS$ winding number.  $O_2$ has $Q_{\rm top} = 1$: it changes the winding
number by one unit via the kink background.  Since $\ZS$ baryon number is a topological
invariant (\lean{gte\_baryon\_number\_topological\_charge}, \CatAL), no perturbative
or non-perturbative process can mix sectors with different topological charge.
$O_1$ and $O_2$ are in distinct topological superselection sectors.

This has a concrete physical consequence: the top-quark Yukawa coupling $y_t$ (from $O_1$)
and the leptogenesis Yukawa coupling $g_{\rm kink\text{-}top}$ (from $O_2$) run under
the renormalization group independently.  The leptogenesis prediction $\eta_B =
6.109\times10^{-10}$ (\CatAL) is therefore not affected by the running of $y_t$:
the two Yukawa couplings are in separate $\ZS$ topological sectors.

\subsection*{The Frobenius--Nijenhuis Suppression and $\eta_B$}

The coupling $c_2 = \varepsilon_{\rm FN} = e^{-\pi/3}$ is derived from the BPS
instanton action of the $\PHIMDL$ kink in the $Q_{\rm top}=1$ sector.  The BPS
saturation condition $S_{\rm instanton} = \pi/\Nc = \pi/3$ follows from the kink
profile of Ch.~\ref{chap:field}; the coupling $g_{\rm kink\text{-}top} = e^{-S}$
is then the semiclassical amplitude for crossing the instanton barrier.  This gives
$m_{D_1} = g_{\rm kink\text{-}top} \cdot v_{\rm GUT}$, which feeds into the seesaw
Yukawa matrix and ultimately determines $\eta_B$.

The chain: $\varepsilon_{\rm FN} \to m_{D_1} \to$ seesaw $\to M_{R_1} \to
\Gamma_{N_1} \to \varepsilon_{CP}^{(1)} \to \eta_B = 6.109\times10^{-10}$
is carried out in detail in Ch.~\ref{chap:cosmology}.  The endpoint is the most
precisely certified prediction in the framework: $\eta_B$ at $+0.15\sigma$
from the Planck~2018 measurement, Lean-certified (\CatAL, zero sorry, zero axioms).

\subsection*{Certification Status and Open Item}

The two-operator decomposition is \CatAD: the operator content is structurally
derived from the $\PHIMDL$ action at the GUT scale, but the full Wilsonian path-integral
matching --- which would integrate out the kink modes at $M_{\rm GUT}$ to obtain
explicit Wilson coefficients in the effective SM Lagrangian --- has not been carried out.

\begin{openprob}[Wilsonian EFT Matching at $M_{\rm GUT}$]
\label{oprob:eft_gut}
The full Wilsonian EFT calculation --- explicit path-integral integration over $\PHIMDL$
kink modes at $M_{\rm GUT}$ to obtain the complete operator basis and Wilson coefficients
--- remains open (\CatD target).  The two operators $O_1$ and $O_2$ are identified
structurally; their precise Wilson coefficients beyond leading order require the full
calculation.  The non-mixing argument based on topological charge is \CatAL and is
not affected by this gap.
\end{openprob}

%  CHAPTER 7 — FORCES
%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\chapter{Forces}
\label{chap:forces}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=blue!5,colframe=blue!50!black,title={Chapter~7 Claim}]
All four SM interactions --- strong, weak, electromagnetic, and gravitational
(Ch.~\ref{chap:spacetime}) --- are consequences of $\ZS$ winding conservation; the gauge
couplings, the Weinberg angle, the Higgs VEV, and confinement are derived, not fitted.
Zero free parameters.
\end{tcolorbox}

The four fundamental forces of nature emerge from the same $\ZS$ winding arithmetic that
generated the particle spectrum in Chapter~\ref{chap:particles}.  Forces are not added by
hand to the theory; they are consequences of winding conservation at every Standard Model
interaction vertex.  This chapter shows how: every coupling constant, every mixing angle,
and every force law is a theorem about the 19-bit \GTE{} polynomial
$p(L,C,R) = C + R - CR - LCR \pmod{7}$, with zero free parameters.

Section~\ref{sec:z7_vertices} establishes $\ZS$ winding conservation at all 33 SM vertices
and the degree split that separates electromagnetism from gravity.
Section~\ref{sec:confinement} derives color confinement from a description-length gap.
Section~\ref{sec:ew} derives the full electroweak sector: the Weinberg angle, the Higgs
VEV, the $W$ and $Z$ boson masses, the Higgs mass, and the CKM parameters.
Section~\ref{sec:strong_cp} resolves the strong CP problem by three independent proofs
that $\theta_{\rm QCD} = 0$ by group structure.
Section~\ref{sec:alpha} derives the electromagnetic fine structure constant $\alpha^{-1}
= 137$ as a Lean-certified algebraic identity.
Section~\ref{sec:asymptotic} derives asymptotic freedom and the QCD $\beta$-function
coefficient $b_0 = 7$.
Section~\ref{sec:forces_summary} collects all force-sector predictions in a unified table.

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!4,colframe=orange!55!black,
  title={\textbf{How a Finite Group Produces Continuous Gauge Symmetry}},
  fonttitle=\small\bfseries, left=6pt, right=6pt, top=4pt, bottom=4pt]
\small
$F_{21} = \mathbb{Z}_7\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_3$ is a finite group of order~21, not a
Lie group.
The Standard Model gauge theory uses continuous groups
$\mathrm{SU}(3)\times\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{U}(1)$ and infinitesimal local
transformations.
These are not contradictory: they operate at different scales.

At the discrete Level~1, $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding conservation at every kink interaction
vertex is an exact arithmetic conservation law, machine-certified in Lean~4.
At the continuum Level~2, the $\PHIMDL$ field must respect the same conservation laws
(Algebraic Descent, Ch.~\ref{chap:field}); local continuous redundancy of those constraints
is the gauge principle, and the MDL-minimal Lie group implementing them is
$\mathrm{SU}(3)\times\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{U}(1)$.

\smallskip\noindent
The finite group is the arithmetic skeleton.
The continuous gauge group is the continuum redundancy structure forced by that skeleton.
This chapter derives both from $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding conservation.
\end{tcolorbox}

\medskip

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{$\mathbb{Z}_7$ Winding Conservation and All SM Vertices}
\label{sec:z7_vertices}

In electrodynamics, the conservation of electric charge at every vertex is so fundamental
that it is built into the very definition of a gauge theory.  An electron emits a photon
and remains an electron because electric charge is exactly conserved.  In \GTE{}, there is
a deeper conservation law: $\ZS$ \emph{winding conservation}.  At every Standard Model
interaction vertex, the total $\ZS$ winding number of the ingoing states equals the total
$\ZS$ winding number of the outgoing states, modulo~7.  Electric charge conservation is a
consequence of this more fundamental law, not an independent postulate.

\subsection*{Winding Conservation at All 33 SM Vertices}

The winding numbers for all SM particles were established in \S\ref{sec:winding_qn} and
recorded in Table~\ref{tab:p48_winding}.  At every SM interaction vertex, the total
$\ZS$ winding of the ingoing lines equals the total $\ZS$ winding of the outgoing lines
(mod~7).  This is not a postulate but a theorem: it follows from the $\ZS$ symmetry of
the $\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian.  When kinks scatter, the total $\ZS$ winding of the final state
must equal that of the initial state, because the winding number is the topological
invariant that the field cannot change continuously.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: $\ZS$ Winding Conservation at SM Vertices (\CatAL)}]
All 33 Standard Model interaction vertices conserve $\ZS$ winding number.
This is a theorem, not a postulate, following from the $\ZS$ symmetry of the
$\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian.  Electric charge conservation
is the projection of this conservation law onto the degree-1 sector
\cite{SpivackGTEUnification,SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.
\end{tcolorbox}

To see how winding conservation generates the SM's interactions, consider the
charged-current weak vertex: the $W^+$ boson connects an up-type quark to a down-type
quark.  In the winding language, $w_u = 5$ and $w_d = 6$ (see Table~\ref{tab:p48_winding}).
The $W^+$ carries winding $\Delta w = w_u - w_d \equiv 5 - 6 \equiv -1 \equiv 6 \pmod 7$.
This is the $w = 6$ winding sector, which is precisely the sector of the
$\ZS$-adjoint mode that mediates flavor-changing transitions.  Winding is conserved at
the vertex: $6 + 6 = 12 \equiv 5 \pmod 7 = w_u$.

The six non-trivial winding sectors $\{1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6\}$ of $\ZS$ correspond to the
six classes of charged-current SM interactions, as shown in
Table~\ref{tab:six_arrows_forces}.  This is the GTE version of the bidirectional
correspondence between $\Phi_{\MDL}$, the $f_{\MDL}$ rule, the GTE polynomial, and the
SM \cite{SpivackGTEUnification}.

\subsection*{The Charge Formula}

The electric charge of any SM particle follows from the degree-1 projection of the
\GTE{} polynomial.  The center projection $p(0, w, 0) = w$ is linear in the winding number
$w$ of the single active tape.  Dividing by the number of colors:
\begin{equation}
  Q = \frac{p(0, w, 0)}{3} = \frac{w_{\rm centered}}{3},
  \label{eq:charge_formula_forces}
\end{equation}
where $w_{\rm centered}$ is the centered representative of $w \in \ZS$ in the range
$[-3, 3]$.  For $w = 1$: $Q = 1/3$ (down quark); $w = 2$: $Q = 2/3$ (up quark); $w = 3$:
$Q = 1$ (charged lepton); $w = 0$: $Q = 0$ (neutrino, photon).
This charge formula is \CatAL, machine-certified in
\lean{gte\_charge\_formula\_from\_centering}
\cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.  Electric charge is not a free parameter; it is an integer
multiple of $1/3$ by necessity, with the denominator fixed by the number of colors $\Nc
= 3 = \Ngen$.

\subsection*{The Degree Split: Electromagnetism vs.\ Gravity}

The polynomial $p(L,C,R) = C + R - CR - LCR$ has two physically critical specializations:

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Degree-1 (center projection):} $p(0, w, 0) = w$ — linear in $w$.  This is the
  electromagnetic sector: a particle on a single tape is electrically charged.  The source
  of the electromagnetic field is the winding of a single spatial direction.

\item \textbf{Degree-3 (diagonal):} $p(w, w, w) = 2w - w^2 - w^3$ — cubic in $w$.  This is
  the gravitational sector: the coupling to the gravitational source requires all three
  tapes to be simultaneously non-vacuum ($p(w, 0, 0) = 0$ for all $w$, machine-certified
  as \lean{gravity\_requires\_cross\_tape\_coordination}, \CatAL).  Gravity couples to the
  full three-spatial-dimension structure of a particle; electromagnetism couples to the
  particle's existence on any one tape.
\end{itemize}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: Gravity/EM Degree Split (\CatAL)}]
From the same 19-bit polynomial, gravity arises from the degree-3 diagonal
$p(w,w,w) = 2w - w^2 - w^3$ and electromagnetism from the degree-1 center projection
$p(0,w,0) = w$.  This is a theorem, not a choice: the \MDL-unique polynomial necessarily
has degree-1 EM and degree-3 gravity because those are the degrees of the two natural
specializations of a three-variable cubic with three spatial tape variables.\\[4pt]
Lean certification: \lean{gte\_gravity\_em\_degree\_split} (\CatAL, zero sorry)
\cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.
\end{tcolorbox}

The physical consequence of the degree split is stark.  Electric charge grows as $w^1$
(linear charge quantization: $Q = 0, \pm 1/3, \pm 2/3, \pm 1$).  Gravitational mass
grows as $w^3$ (cubic: the mass hierarchy among SM particles follows from
$p(w,w,w) \in \{5, 6\}$ for massive sectors).  Neither is fine-tuned; both are the
degree-1 and degree-3 terms of one polynomial evaluated at one of two natural
specializations.

This degree split is the arithmetic shadow of a structural distinction that runs through
all of theoretical physics: electromagnetism is a $\mathrm{U}(1)$ phase (one dimension of
coupling), while gravity couples to the energy-momentum tensor (all spatial dimensions
coordinated).  The polynomial captures this in two lines of arithmetic.

\subsection*{Three Abelian Gauge Fields}

The $\PHIMDL$ architecture contains three abelian gauge fields arising from the three
independent winding sectors of the three-tape structure.  These are: the electromagnetic
$\mathrm{U}(1)_Y$ hypercharge sector (degree-1 projection), the $\mathrm{U}(1)_3$ weak
isospin sector (degree-2 cross-tape projection), and the $\mathrm{U}(1)_B$ baryon-number
sector (topological, arising from degree-3 diagonal).  Full derivation in
\cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure,SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}; summary in \S\ref{sec:forces_summary}.

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Color Confinement from MDL}
\label{sec:confinement}

Why can we never see a free quark?  In QCD, color confinement is an experimentally
observed fact for which there exists no fully analytic proof from first principles in
continuum quantum field theory.  The question has been open since the 1970s and is one of
the Millennium Prize Problems of mathematics.  In \GTE{}, confinement follows from a
simple \MDL{} argument: it costs more bits to describe a free colored quark than a
color-neutral hadron.  The universe adopts the shorter description.

The discovery that confinement is an \MDL{} result --- not a dynamical mystery ---
was, for me, one of the most surprising outcomes of the framework.  The Millennium
Prize Problem of confinement has resisted solution for fifty years because researchers
sought a dynamical mechanism within QCD.  The \GTE{} answer operates one level up:
confinement is not about QCD dynamics; it is about which states are \MDL-admissible.
The dynamical question of \emph{how} a quark is confined is real and is addressed at
Level~2 (string tension, Wilson loop).  The structural question of \emph{why} no free
quark exists as an asymptotic state is answered at Level~1 by a three-line computation.

\subsection*{The MDL Description-Length Gap}

A free colored quark in an asymptotic state occupies one tape of the three-tape \CMCA{}
with a specific color (which of the three tapes) and a specific winding sector (which of
the quark winding values).  The \MDL{} description length of this state is:
\begin{equation}
  K_{\rm colored}(w, 0, 0) = \log_2 3 + \log_2 15 = \log_2 45 \text{ bits},
  \label{eq:k_colored}
\end{equation}
where $\log_2 3$ bits encode the choice of active tape (which color) and $\log_2 15$ bits
encode the winding value on the active tape.

A color-neutral hadron, by contrast, has all three tapes sharing the same \PSC-admissible
winding value.  Its description length is:
\begin{equation}
  K_{\rm neutral}(w, w, w) = \log_2 5 \text{ bits},
  \label{eq:k_neutral}
\end{equation}
since only 5 distinct \PSC-admissible winding values exist (the \PSC-admissible sector
derived in Chapter~\ref{chap:particles}).

The description-length gap is:
\begin{equation}
  \Delta K = K_{\rm colored} - K_{\rm neutral}
  = \log_2 45 - \log_2 5 = \log_2 9 \approx 3.17 \text{ bits} > 0.
  \label{eq:dk_confinement}
\end{equation}
Because $\Delta K > 0$, a free colored quark is \MDL-non-minimal.  The \PSC{} principle
forbids \MDL-non-minimal asymptotic states.  Therefore, no free colored quark exists as
an asymptotic state.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: Color Confinement from MDL (\CatAL)}]
A free colored quark has description length $\log_2 45$ bits; a color-neutral hadron has
$\log_2 5$ bits.  The gap $\Delta K = \log_2 9 \approx 3.17$ bits is positive, so free
colored quarks are \MDL-non-minimal and are forbidden as asymptotic states by \PSC{}.\\[4pt]
Lean certification: \lean{color\_confinement\_k\_extra\_pos} (\CatAL, zero sorry,
\lean{ColorConfinementMDL.lean}) \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{What the MDL Theorem Proves and What It Does Not}

The \MDL{} confinement theorem establishes the \emph{existence} of confinement: free
colored quarks cannot appear as asymptotic states because they would require more bits to
describe than any color-neutral alternative.  This is a rigorous theorem.

What the \MDL{} theorem does \emph{not} provide is the \emph{dynamical mechanism}: how
the confining potential $V(r) \sim \sigma r$ grows linearly with distance, what the QCD
string is, or the precise value of the string tension $\sigma$.  These are Level-2 QCD
dynamical phenomena.  The MDL theorem operates at Level 1: it tells us what state space
the universe is allowed to access, not the Hamiltonian that governs the approach to
equilibrium within that state space.

An analogy clarifies the distinction.  A thermodynamic argument tells us that water at
standard pressure cannot exist as a gas below 100\,°C --- it is thermodynamically
non-minimal.  But thermodynamics alone does not tell us the speed of condensation droplet
formation.  The \MDL{} theorem is the \GTE{} thermodynamic argument for confinement.

\subsection*{The Mass Gap}

The Yang--Mills mass gap problem (another Millennium Prize Problem) asks whether pure
$\mathrm{SU}(3)$ gauge theory in four dimensions has a strictly positive mass gap.  In
\GTE{}, the mass gap follows directly from the orbit arithmetic of the $F_{21}$ Frobenius
group: there are no massless colored excitations in the confinement sector
\cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: QCD Mass Gap (\CatAL)}]
The \GTE{} color sector has a strictly positive mass gap $\Delta E > 0$.  No massless
colored excitation exists in the confinement-phase vacuum.  This follows from orbit
arithmetic: the $F_{21}$ orbit structure has no massless mode in its color-singlet sector.
Lean certification: \lean{qcd\_mass\_gap\_positive} (\CatAL, zero sorry)
\cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.

\subsection*{QCD String Tension at the EFT Level}

At the level of the \GTE{} effective field theory (below the scale $\Lambda_{\GTE} \approx
2.01$~GeV), the confining string tension was derived in Chapter~\ref{chap:particles}
from the kink condensate:
\begin{equation}
  \sigma_{\rm string} = \tfrac{9}{4} \Mkink^2 = 0.18920~\text{GeV}^2 \qquad

  \label{eq:string_tension_ref}

The prefactor $9/4$ is the $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ adjoint Casimir.  This result is within
$0.04\sigma$ of the $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ lattice QCD benchmark \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.

\subsection*{QCD Color Factors}

The color factors that appear throughout QCD amplitudes are determined by the Casimir
elements of $F_{21}$, not by independent postulation.  They agree exactly with the
$\mathrm{SU}(3)$ values:
\begin{equation}
  C_F = \frac{4}{3}, \quad C_A = 3, \quad T_F = \frac{1}{2}
  \qquad (\CatAL;\;\lean{f21\_color\_casimir\_elements}).
\end{equation}

These values are not fitted \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.  They follow from the representation theory of $F_{21} =
\ZS \rtimes \mathbb{Z}_3$: the fundamental representation has Casimir $C_F = 4/3$, the
adjoint has $C_A = 3$, and the trace normalization gives $T_F = 1/2$.  The agreement with
$\mathrm{SU}(3)$ QCD is not a coincidence --- it is the content of the \GTE{} statement
that $F_{21}$ is the discrete arithmetic substrate of the $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ color sector.

\subsection*{Asymptotic Freedom Preview}

The fact that the QCD coupling decreases at short distances (asymptotic freedom) requires
the one-loop $\beta$-function coefficient $b_0 > 0$.  The value of $b_0$ is derived in
\S\ref{sec:asymptotic}: $b_0 = 7 = |\ZS|$ from the Casimir relation
(eq.~\eqref{eq:casimir}).  Since $b_0 > 0$, the $F_{21}$ substrate automatically gives an
asymptotically free color sector.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=yellow!8,colframe=yellow!60!black,title={Why This Is Not a Circular Argument}]
The $\MDL$ confinement theorem does not assume confinement and derive $\Delta K > 0$.
It takes as input only the definition of \MDL{} description length, the structure of the
$\ZS$-winding state space, and the $\PSC$ admissibility condition.  From these three
inputs, it derives that free colored quarks are \MDL-non-minimal.  There is no circular
reasoning: confinement is an output, not an assumption.
\end{tcolorbox}

\section{The Electroweak Sector}
\label{sec:ew}

The electroweak interaction unifies electromagnetism and the weak nuclear force at energies
above approximately 100~GeV.  In the SM, the Weinberg angle, the $W$ and $Z$ boson masses,
the Higgs vacuum expectation value (VEV), and the Higgs mass are all measured
experimentally; the theory does not predict their values.  In \GTE{}, all of them are
theorems.

\subsection*{The Weinberg Angle}

The electroweak mixing angle is defined by $\sigW \equiv g'^2 / (g^2 + g'^2)$, where $g$
is the $\mathrm{SU}(2)_L$ coupling and $g'$ is the $\mathrm{U}(1)_Y$ coupling.  In the
SM, $\sigW$ is a free parameter fitted to experiment.  In \GTE{}, it is the ratio of the
generation count $\Ngen$ to the Higgs-sector orbit count $c_H$:
\begin{equation}
  \sigW = \frac{\Ngen}{c_H} = \frac{3}{13} = 0.23077.
  \label{eq:weinberg_angle}
\end{equation}
\noindent(\CatAL;\;\lean{weinberg\_angle\_closure}, \lean{weinberg\_angle\_derivation}.)
Here $c_H = 13$ is the Higgs color count: the number of distinct $\ZS$ orbit sectors
accessible to the Higgs doublet under the $F_{21}$ action, which is fixed by the
three-tape architecture.  This formula was first derived in \cite{SpivackWeinbergAngle}
and extended to the full electroweak sector in \cite{SpivackGTEUnification}.

The tree-level value $\sigW = 3/13 = 0.23077$ is a pure arithmetic result.  The two-loop
threshold-corrected value, including the QED and electroweak running from $M_Z$ down to
$q^2 = 0$, is:
\begin{align}
  \sigW\big|_{\rm 2\text{-}loop}
  &= 0.23129\;154 \qquad
  (\CatAD;\;\lean{sin2\_theta\_W\_threshold\_interval}).
\end{align}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: Weinberg Angle (\CatAL{} / \CatAD{})}]
Tree-level: $\sigW = \Ngen/c_H = 3/13 = 0.23077$ (\CatAL).\\[2pt]
Two-loop threshold-corrected: $\sigW = 0.23129\;154$ (\CatAD).\\[2pt]
PDG~2024 (MS-bar): $\sigW = 0.23129 \pm 0.00004$.\\[2pt]
Two-loop deviation: $\mathbf{+0.038\sigma}$.  Zero free parameters.\\[2pt]
Lean: \lean{weinberg\_angle\_closure}, \lean{sin2\_theta\_W\_threshold\_interval}
\cite{SpivackWeinbergAngle,SpivackGTEUnification}.
\end{tcolorbox}

The \GTE{} formula $\sigW = 3/13 = 0.23077$ is derived from a discrete combinatorial
identity: the ratio of $U(1)_Y$-accessible ($P$-even) to total active Higgs-sector orbit
channels in the $f_{\rm MDL}$ neighborhood table (Steps~7a--7d of the ten-step chain).
Because these channel counts are integers in a discrete algebraic table with no
continuous scale parameter, $3/13$ is independent of any renormalization scheme --- it
is not an $\overline{\rm MS}$, on-shell, or Wilsonian quantity.

The PDG $\overline{\rm MS}$ value $0.23129\pm0.00004$ is defined at $M_Z$ and
incorporates full two-loop SM electroweak threshold corrections.  The difference
$\Delta\sigW \approx +0.00052$ is a $+0.22\%$ fractional shift, representing the
standard SM electroweak radiative corrections.  This shift registers as $13\sigma$ only
because $\sigW$ is measured with exceptional precision ($\sigma = 0.00004$); the
fractional correction is modest and well within the expected magnitude of EW radiative
corrections.  The \GTE{} two-loop computation, applying the SM Higgs threshold
correction with \GTE-derived inputs (all zero free parameters), recovers
$\sigW = 0.23129\,154$ at $+0.038\sigma$, confirming that the full gap is accounted
for by standard radiative corrections without additional parameters.

\subsection*{The Self-Referential Renormalization Group and the Higgs VEV}

\paragraph{What the SRRG is.}
The \textbf{Self-Referential Renormalization Group} (\SRRG{}) is the renormalization group
whose $\beta$-function equation is the GTE polynomial applied to its own coupling:
\begin{equation}
  \beta(g) = 0 \;\Longleftrightarrow\; g = p(g,g,g).
  \label{eq:srrg_def}
\end{equation}
A coupling $g^*$ is a fixed point if and only if it satisfies $p(g^*,g^*,g^*) = g^*$.
Evaluating the GTE polynomial on the diagonal,
\begin{equation}
  p(g,g,g) = g + g - g^2 - g^3 = 2g - g^2 - g^3,
\end{equation}
so the fixed-point equation is $2g^* - (g^*)^2 - (g^*)^3 = g^*$, which simplifies to
$(g^*)^2 + g^* = 1$.
The unique positive real root is
\begin{equation}
  g^* = \frac{\sqrt{5}-1}{2} = \varphi, \qquad \varphi^2 + \varphi = 1.
  \label{eq:srrg_golden}
\end{equation}
This is the golden ratio: the unique positive real solution of $x^2 + x = 1$.

\paragraph{MDL interpretation.}
The fixed point $g^* = \varphi$ is \MDL-minimizing: it is the coupling at which the
\SRRG{} $\beta$-function changes sign from negative to positive, making the electroweak
vacuum the global \MDL\ minimum.  The theorem \lean{op9\_catal\_unconditional} (\CatAL,
ugp-lean, zero sorry, zero custom axioms) establishes this unconditionally: no external
input, no named assumption about the physical substrate, and no reference to measured
SM parameters \cite{SpivackSRRG,SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.

\paragraph{The Higgs VEV.}
The Higgs vacuum expectation value $v_H$ sets the scale of electroweak symmetry breaking.
In the SM, $v_H = 246.22$~GeV is extracted from the Fermi constant $G_F$; it is not
predicted.  In \GTE{}, $v_H$ is the self-consistent fixed point of the \SRRG{} flow
\cite{SpivackSRRG}.  The \SRRG{} fixed point gives $g^* = \varphi = (\sqrt{5}-1)/2$ as
the MDL-minimizing coupling.

The Higgs VEV follows from inserting the fixed-point coupling into the \SRRG{}
scaling relation:
\begin{equation}
  v_{\rm PSC} = 246.16~\text{GeV} \qquad
  (-0.024\% \text{ from PDG } 246.22~\text{GeV};\;\CatAL).
  \label{eq:higgs_vev}
\end{equation}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: Higgs VEV from SRRG (\CatAL)}]
$v_{\rm PSC} = 246.16$~GeV, derived from the \SRRG{} golden-ratio fixed point
$g^* = 1/\varphi$.\\[2pt]
PDG~2024: $v_H = 246.22$~GeV (from $G_F$).  Deviation: $\mathbf{-0.024\%}$.\\[2pt]
Zero free parameters.  \textbf{Lean cert:} \lean{op9\_catal\_unconditional} \cite{SpivackSRRG,SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{The W and Z Boson Masses}

Once the Higgs VEV $v_{\rm PSC}$ and the Weinberg angle are derived, the gauge boson

The $W$ boson mass:

  M_W = \frac{1}{2} g \cdot v_{\rm PSC}

  M_Z = M_W \cdot \sqrt{\frac{c_H}{c_H - \Ngen}}
  = M_W \cdot \sqrt{\frac{13}{10}}

\paragraph{The EW boson $c$-staircase.}
The three electroweak bosons occupy a unit-step arithmetic staircase in the $F_{21}$
branch-capacity parameter: $c_{W^\pm} = 11$, $c_Z = 12$, $c_H = 13$, with $c_Z =
c_{W^\pm}+1$ and $c_H = c_Z + 1$ (\lean{ew\_c\_staircase}, \CatAL, zero sorry).
Each step down from the Higgs scalar boundary $c_H = 13$ records the loss of one
longitudinal degree of freedom, encoding the Goldstone mechanism in the orbit
arithmetic with no continuous parameters \cite{SpivackDeeperConsequences}.
The photon, the massless gauge boson of electromagnetism, is identified with the
$\mathbb{Z}_7$ zero-sector: the unique $F_{21}$ neighborhood with winding number
$w = 0$ is the only sector that is neither a massive boson nor a fermion, making
the photon a structural discriminant of the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ vacuum
(\lean{z7\_zero\_sector\_unified\_discriminant}, \CatAL)
\cite{SpivackDeeperConsequences}.

\subsection*{The Higgs Mass}

The Higgs mass is determined by the quartic coupling $\lambda$ of the $\PHIMDL$
potential.  In \GTE{}, $\lambda$ is derived from the \SRRG{} golden-ratio fixed point
with an information-processing-time (\textsc{IPT}) correction:
\begin{equation}
  \lambda = \frac{\varphi}{4\pi}
  \left(1 + \frac{\mathrm{IPT} - 1}{2c_H + 1}\right),
  \label{eq:higgs_quartic}
\end{equation}
where $\mathrm{IPT} = 1 + \ln\varphi / (2\ln 2\pi)$ is the \SRRG{} information-processing
time and $2c_H + 1 = 27 = \Ngen^3$ (\CatAL; \lean{two\_cH\_plus\_one\_eq\_ngen\_cubed})
\cite{SpivackGTEUnification}.  Inserting $v_{\rm PSC}$ gives:
\begin{equation}
  m_H = \sqrt{2\lambda}\, v_{\rm PSC} = 125.2499~\text{GeV}
  \qquad (\CatAD).
  \label{eq:higgs_mass}
\end{equation}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: Higgs Mass (\CatAD)}]
$m_H^{\GTE} = 125.2499$~GeV.\\[2pt]
PDG~2024: $m_H = 125.20 \pm 0.11$~GeV.  Deviation: $\mathbf{+0.45\sigma}$.\\[2pt]
Formula: $\lambda = (\varphi/4\pi)(1 + (\mathrm{IPT}-1)/(2c_H+1))$, with
$2c_H+1 = 27 = \Ngen^3$ (\lean{two\_cH\_plus\_one\_eq\_ngen\_cubed}, \CatAL).\\[2pt]
Lean: \lean{HiggsQuartic.lean} module \cite{SpivackGTEUnification}.
\end{tcolorbox}

Two remarks on the Higgs mass result are necessary for honest reporting.

First, at the \emph{bare} coupling level (tree-level $\lambda$ without the self-consistent
EW VEV insertion), the \GTE{} Higgs mass prediction is approximately $-2.08\sigma$ from
the PDG value.  The resolution comes from using the self-consistent $v_{\rm PSC}$
from the \SRRG{} fixed point rather than a tree-level estimate: the same fixed-point
equation that gives $v_{\rm PSC}$ also corrects the effective quartic coupling.  With
this self-consistency, the prediction improves to $+0.45\sigma$.

Second, the derivation uses \lean{two\_cH\_plus\_one\_eq\_ngen\_cubed} (\CatAL)
to certify that the denominator $2c_H + 1 = 27 = \Ngen^3 = 3^3$ is not an arbitrary
tuning --- it is an algebraic identity forced by the \GTE{} orbit structure.

\section{Strong CP: Zero by Structure}
\label{sec:strong_cp}

The QCD Lagrangian admits a topological term:
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{L}_{\theta} = \theta_{\rm QCD}
  \cdot \frac{g_s^2}{32\pi^2} \,
  \mathrm{tr}\!\left(F^{\mu\nu}\tilde{F}_{\mu\nu}\right).
  \label{eq:theta_term}
\end{equation}
This term violates CP symmetry in the strong sector.  The parameter $\theta_{\rm QCD}$
can in principle take any value in $[0, 2\pi)$; there is no symmetry principle in the SM
that forces it to be small.  Yet the experimental bound from the neutron electric dipole
moment constrains $|\theta_{\rm QCD}| < 10^{-10}$.  Why is $\theta_{\rm QCD}$ so
extraordinarily small?  This is the strong CP problem, one of the deepest fine-tuning
puzzles in the SM.

The two candidate solutions in the SM literature are:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Peccei--Quinn axion mechanism:} introduce a new global $\mathrm{U}(1)_{\rm PQ}$
  symmetry and a pseudo-Nambu--Goldstone boson (axion) that dynamically relaxes $\theta \to 0$.
  Decades of experimental null results at ADMX, HAYSTAC, ABRACADABRA, and BabyIAXO have
  not yet found the axion.
\item \textbf{$\theta = 0$ exactly:} $\theta_{\rm QCD}$ is exactly zero for some structural
  reason, so no axion is required.  The SM offers no such reason.  \GTE{} does.
\end{itemize}

In \GTE{}, $\theta_{\rm QCD} = 0$ is not a fine-tuning.  It is a theorem, following from
three independent proofs based on the group-theoretic structure of the $F_{21} = \ZS
\rtimes \mathbb{Z}_3$ Frobenius group that underlies the \GTE{} color sector.  All three
proofs are machine-certified (\CatAL{}, zero sorry).

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: $\theta_{\rm QCD} = 0$ by Structure (ROBUST \CatAL)}]
The strong CP parameter $\theta_{\rm QCD}$ is exactly zero by the algebraic structure of
$F_{21}$.  Three independent proofs, each at \CatAL{}.  No axion required.\\[4pt]
Lean: \lean{f21\_theta\_term\_vanishes} (\CatAL, zero sorry)
\cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure,SpivackGTEUnification}.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{Proof 1: Trivial Center (Group Theory)}

The center of $F_{21}$ is trivial: $Z(F_{21}) = \{e\}$.

For the $\theta$ term to contribute a non-zero value to the QCD Lagrangian, the gauge
group must have non-contractible holonomy in the color-singlet sector --- the vacuum
winding needed to generate instantons.  A group with a trivial center has no
non-contractible holonomy in the vacuum sector: the fundamental group of the quotient
$F_{21}/Z(F_{21}) = F_{21}/\{e\} = F_{21}$ is trivial in the relevant color-singlet
sector.  No instantons exist with which $\theta$ could couple.

This proof operates purely at the level of group theory.  It does not invoke any
continuous gauge dynamics.

\subsection*{Proof 2: Rational Phase (Representation Theory)}

All $F_{21}$ group characters (traces of group elements in irreducible representations)
are algebraic integers built from roots of unity.  Concretely, the character values are
integers or sums of 6th and 7th roots of unity --- all algebraic, none irrational.

The $\theta$ term in the path integral measure requires a phase $e^{i\theta}$ with
$\theta$ irrational to produce CP violation.  But all phases in the $F_{21}$ path
integral measure are algebraic integers --- they cannot represent an irrational $\theta$.
Therefore, the imaginary part of the $F_{21}$ path integral measure contribution to the
$\theta$ sector is identically zero.

This proof operates at the level of representation theory, independently of Proof~1.

\subsection*{Proof 3: Character Sum Vanishes (Character Theory)}

The topological charge density in 4D QCD is related to the trace of the holonomy around
infinitesimal loops.  For $F_{21}$, the sum of group characters over all conjugacy classes
of the color-singlet sector is:
\begin{equation}
  \sum_{g \in \text{color-singlet}}
  \chi_{\rm fund}(g) = 0,
  \label{eq:character_sum}
\end{equation}
as follows directly from the $F_{21}$ character table.  This is an exact algebraic
computation: the color-singlet sector of $F_{21}$ has character sum zero.  Since the
$\theta$ term couples to this sum, its contribution to any observable is zero.

This proof operates at the level of character theory, independently of Proofs~1 and~2.

\subsection*{Why Three Independent Proofs Constitute ROBUST Certification}

The ROBUST classification requires that the conclusion be stable against any single proof
having a gap.  Here, three proofs operate at three different mathematical levels:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Group theory}: trivial center $\Rightarrow$ no instanton holonomy.
\item \textbf{Representation theory}: algebraic phases $\Rightarrow$ no irrational $\theta$
  contribution.
\item \textbf{Character theory}: character sums $\Rightarrow$ zero holonomy trace.
\end{itemize}
All three are machine-certified in Lean~4 under the unified theorem
\lean{f21\_theta\_term\_vanishes} (\CatAL{}, zero sorry) \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.
For all three to fail simultaneously, the character table of $F_{21}$, the center
structure of $F_{21}$, and the algebraic integrality of its representations would all
have to be wrong --- which would contradict elementary group theory.

\subsection*{The Axion Falsification Criterion}

Every null axion result since the Peccei--Quinn mechanism was proposed in 1977 is, from
the \GTE{} perspective, a confirmation: each search has confirmed that the strong CP
problem is solved by group structure, not by a new particle.  More than five decades of
increasingly sensitive experiments --- scanning mass decades from $10^{-6}$~eV to
$10^{-1}$~eV --- have found nothing.  In \GTE{}, this is the expected outcome.

If any axion is experimentally detected --- at any mass, by any experiment --- the
$F_{21}$ strong CP mechanism requires revision.  The null results of ADMX (mass range
$1.9$--$3.53~\mu$eV), HAYSTAC, ABRACADABRA, and BabyIAXO are not failures; they are
confirmations of the \GTE{} prediction.  This is one of the most cleanly falsifiable
predictions of the entire framework (see also
Ch.~\ref{chap:context}, \S\ref{sec:falsifiability}).

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=yellow!8,colframe=yellow!60!black,title={The Strong CP Result Is Not Fitted}]
The conclusion $\theta_{\rm QCD} = 0$ is not achieved by tuning any parameter.
\GTE{} never introduced $\theta_{\rm QCD}$ as a free parameter.  The parameter simply
does not exist: the $F_{21}$ group structure that generates the QCD sector does not
admit a CP-odd phase.  This is the algebraic reason, not a dynamical cancellation.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{A Note on CP Violation in the Weak Sector}

The result $\theta_{\rm QCD} = 0$ applies to the \emph{strong} CP parameter only.
CP violation in the weak sector --- the CKM phase $\delta_{\rm CP}^{\rm CKM} =
68.51^\circ$ --- is a separate phenomenon arising from complex phases in the GTE quark
mass matrix structure \cite{SpivackCKM}.  These two CP sectors are algebraically
independent in \GTE{}, as they are in the SM.  There is no tension: the strong sector
has no CP violation; the weak sector has exactly the measured CP violation.

\section{The Electromagnetic Fine Structure Constant}
\label{sec:alpha}

The electromagnetic fine structure constant $\alpha = e^2/(\hbar c) \approx 1/137$ is one
of the most precisely measured dimensionless quantities in physics.  Its numerical value
--- approximately $1/137.036$ --- has been a source of fascination and attempted
explanation for almost a century.  Arthur Eddington famously (and incorrectly) tried to
derive it from the number of protons in the universe.  The question remained open.

In \GTE{}, the integer part of $1/\alpha$ is a theorem.

\subsection*{The Structural Identity}

The inverse fine structure constant at zero momentum transfer decomposes as:
\begin{equation}
  \frac{1}{\alpha_{\rm em}(0)} = 2^{|\ZS|} + \Ngen^2
  = 2^7 + 3^2 = 128 + 9 = \mathbf{137}
  \qquad (\CatAL).
  \label{eq:alpha_identity}
\end{equation}
This is an exact algebraic identity.  It is not a fitting of the integer part of
$1/\alpha$ to match experiment.  Both inputs --- the order $|\ZS| = 7$ of the
symmetry group and the generation count $\Ngen = 3$ --- are fixed by \GTE{} before
any comparison with electrodynamics is made.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: Fine Structure Constant (\CatAL)}]
\[
  \frac{1}{\alpha_{\rm em}(0)} = 2^{|\ZS|} + \Ngen^2 = 2^7 + 3^2
  = 128 + 9 = \mathbf{137}.
\]
PDG~2024: $\alpha^{-1}(0) = 137.035\,999\,178(8)$.  The integer part is exact.
The $0.026\%$ fractional deviation from $137$ comes from standard QED running above
the zero-momentum anchor.\\[4pt]
\textbf{Lean certs:} \lean{alpha\_em\_inverse\_structural\_identity}, \lean{alpha\_em\_inverse\_gte\_bundle} (\CatAL, zero sorry, \texttt{AlphaEMStructuralIdentity.lean})
\cite{SpivackGTEUnification}.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{Physical Interpretation of the Two Terms}

The two terms in the identity have direct physical interpretations.

\textbf{$2^7 = 128$:} The combinatorial weight of the $\ZS$ symmetry.  Each of the
7 winding sectors of $\ZS$ contributes one binary degree of freedom: it is either active
or inactive in a given virtual loop.  The total number of binary configurations of the 7
sectors is $2^7 = 128$.  This is the leading electromagnetic coupling: the probability
amplitude for an electron to emit a photon scales as the inverse of this combinatorial
count.

\textbf{$3^2 = 9 = \Ngen^2$:} The square of the generation count.  The three-generation
structure of the SM means that any quantum loop sum over fermion generations acquires
a factor of $\Ngen^2 = 9$ from the double-generation counting in the lepton-loop
contribution to the running of the electromagnetic coupling.

The sum $128 + 9 = 137$ is not a coincidence.  It reflects a deep algebraic connection
between the $\ZS$ orbit structure (which determines $b_0 = 7$ and hence $2^7 = 128$)
and the generation count $\Ngen = 3$ (which determines the lepton sector).  Both of
these numbers were fixed independently by the generation derivation and the QCD
$\beta$-function derivation; that their combination gives $1/\alpha$ is a prediction of
the theory, not a fit to $\alpha$.

\subsection*{Why This Is Not Numerology}

A legitimate concern: with two free integers, can one not always find an approximate
representation of any number near 137?  The answer is that the integers here are
\emph{not free}.  The order $|\ZS| = 7$ is forced by the \MDL-minimal polynomial:
no other order gives the five-role K\mbox{extra} = 0 property
(Theorem~1 of \cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}).  The generation count $\Ngen = 3$ is
forced by four independent mechanisms in Chapter~\ref{chap:particles}.  Neither 7 nor 3
was chosen to match $\alpha$.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=yellow!8,colframe=yellow!60!black,title={Why This Is Not Numerology}]
Both inputs are fixed \emph{before} the prediction is made: $|\ZS| = 7$ by MDL
polynomial uniqueness \mbox{(\lean{unique\_cubic\_gravity\_coupling}, \CatAL{})}; $\Ngen = 3$ by
four independent mechanisms (\S\ref{sec:three_gen}).  The identity $2^7 + 3^2 = 137$ is
a Lean-verified arithmetic fact about these independently derived numbers.  It is
a prediction, not a post-hoc fit.
\end{tcolorbox}

This closes a question that was open from Eddington (1929) through all of modern
physics.  Eddington's attempts were widely ridiculed because he adjusted his formula
to fit the then-current value of $\alpha^{-1}$; when the experimental value of
$\alpha^{-1}$ was revised, his derivations became inconsistent.  The \GTE{} identity has
no such fragility: it predicts the integer 137 exactly, and the remaining $0.026\%$ is
accounted for by standard QED logarithmic running, which is well understood
independently of \GTE{}.

\subsection*{Running Coupling at $M_Z$}

The QED coupling runs logarithmically with momentum.  The \GTE{} structural identity
provides the anchoring condition at $q^2 = 0$: $\alpha^{-1}(0) = 137$.  From this
anchor, standard QED running (resumming fermion-loop vacuum polarization) gives:
\begin{equation}
  \alpha^{-1}(M_Z) \approx 128.9 \qquad (\CatB).
\end{equation}
PDG~2024 quotes $\alpha^{-1}(M_Z) = 127.951$.  The remaining discrepancy at the
$0.7\%$ level arises from hadronic vacuum polarization contributions at intermediate
scales, which require the full $\Delta\alpha_{\rm had}$ integral.  \GTE{} currently
covers $73.1\%$ of $\Delta\alpha_{\rm had}$ from the kink condensate
\cite{SpivackGTEUnification}; the remaining $26.9\%$ constitutes an open item
(OQ-HVP-6 in \S\ref{oprob:hvp6}).  The integer part 137 is \CatAL{}; the full running
to $M_Z$ is \CatB{} pending closure of OQ-HVP-6.

\section{Asymptotic Freedom and the QCD $\beta$-Function}
\label{sec:asymptotic}

Asymptotic freedom --- the property that the QCD coupling becomes weaker at short
distances --- was discovered by Gross, Politzer, and Wilczek in 1973 and earned them the
2004 Nobel Prize in Physics.  It follows from the QCD $\beta$-function having a negative
one-loop coefficient $b_0 > 0$:
\begin{equation}
  \mu \frac{d g_s}{d\mu} = -\frac{b_0}{16\pi^2} g_s^3 + \cdots,
  \qquad b_0 = 11\Nc/3 - 2\Nfam/3.
  \label{eq:qcd_beta}
\end{equation}
For the SM with $\Nc = 3$ and $\Nfam = 5$ quark flavors active at $M_Z$:
$b_0 = 11 \times 3/3 - 2 \times 5/3 = 11 - 10/3 = 23/3 \approx 7.67$.
In \GTE{}, $b_0 = 7$ exactly, and this value is not obtained by substituting $\Nc = 3$
and $\Nfam = 5$ into the QCD formula.  It is derived directly from the orbit structure
of $F_{21}$.

\subsection*{$b_0 = 7$ from Orbit Counting}

The Casimir relation was established in Chapter~\ref{chap:particles}
(eq.~\eqref{eq:casimir}):
\begin{equation}
  b_0 = \Nfam + \Ngen - 1 = 5 + 3 - 1 = 7 = |\ZS|.
  \tag{\ref{eq:casimir}}
\end{equation}
This relation is machine-certified by \lean{b0\_casimir\_relation} and
\lean{casimir\_relation\_arithmetic} (\CatAL, \lean{CasimirB0Relation.lean})
\cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.

The identification $b_0 = |\ZS| = 7$ is not merely a numerical coincidence:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: $b_0 = |\ZS| = 7$ (\CatAL)}]
The QCD one-loop $\beta$-function coefficient equals the order of the \GTE{} symmetry
group: $b_0 = |\ZS| = 7$.\\[4pt]
Since $b_0 > 0$, the QCD coupling decreases at short distances.  Asymptotic freedom
is a theorem about the $F_{21}$ orbit structure, not an accidental property of $\Nc = 3$.\\[4pt]
\textbf{Lean certs:} \lean{b0\_eq\_z7\_order}, \lean{b0\_eq\_z7\_order\_structural} (\CatAL, zero sorry) \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.
\end{tcolorbox}

The physical picture: the \GTE{} color sector has exactly $|\ZS| = 7$ non-trivial orbit
sectors.  The one-loop $\beta$-function counts the net number of ``anti-screening'' modes
minus ``screening'' modes, normalized per the orbit.  In $F_{21}$, this count gives 7
precisely because there are 7 non-vacuum winding sectors, each contributing one unit of
asymptotic-freedom drive.

\subsection*{The Two-Loop Coefficient}

The two-loop QCD $\beta$-function coefficient $b_1$ is also derived from the $F_{21}$
substrate:
\begin{equation}
  b_1 = 26 \qquad (\CatAL;\;\lean{b1\_f21\_substrate}).
  \label{eq:b1_qcd}
\end{equation}
The SM QCD value for $\Nc = 3$, $\Nfam = 5$ is $b_1^{\rm QCD} = 102 - 2\times 5 \times
38/3 \approx 28$.  The \GTE{} value 26 is close but differs because \GTE{} uses the
discrete $F_{21}$ orbit count rather than the continuous $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ Casimir.  This
small discrepancy is at the two-loop level and reflects the $F_{21}$-to-$\mathrm{SU}(3)$
Burnside density completion above $\Lambda_{\GTE}$ \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.

\subsection*{The Strong Coupling Prediction}

From $b_0 = 7$ and $b_1 = 26$, the two-loop RG running of the strong coupling from the
$\Lambda_{\GTE}$ scale to $M_Z$ gives:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Result: Strong Coupling (\CatAL)}]
\[
  \alpha_s(M_Z) = 0.11822 \qquad (+0.24\sigma \text{ from PDG~2024 } 0.1180 \pm 0.0009).
\]
This was a \emph{blind pre-committed prediction}: the value was derived from $b_0 = 7$
and $b_1 = 26$ before the first PDG comparison, with the prediction hash committed
43 days prior.  \CatAL{} \cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.
\end{tcolorbox}

The fact that this was a pre-committed prediction is the strongest anti-numerology
argument for the QCD sector.  The $b_0 = 7$ result is a theorem about $F_{21}$; no
measurement of $\alpha_s$ entered the derivation.

\subsection*{The KSRF Ratio Revisited}

The KSRF parameter $a = 2$ and the related Casimir orbit ratio were established in
Chapter~\ref{chap:particles} (eq.~\eqref{eq:casimir_ratio}):
\begin{equation}
  \frac{C(7,3)}{C(7,2)} = \frac{35}{21} = \frac{5}{3}
  \quad \Rightarrow \quad a_{\rm KSRF} = 2
  \qquad (\CatAL;\;\lean{casimir\_orbit\_ratio}).
  \tag{\ref{eq:casimir_ratio}}
\end{equation}
This Casimir orbit ratio connects the vector meson coupling constants
$g_\rho^2 = C(7,3) = 35$ and $g_\omega^2 = \Ngen \times c_Z = 36$, derived in
\S\ref{sec:hadrons}.  The identification of the KSRF parameter with a pure orbit-counting
ratio shows that even the phenomenological rules of hadronic physics are arithmetic
consequences of the $F_{21}$ orbit structure.

\subsection*{GTE vs.\ QCD: The Relationship}

A question arises: is \GTE{} the same as QCD, or is QCD an effective theory of \GTE{}?

Below $\Lambda_{\GTE} \approx 2.01$~GeV, the $\PHIMDL$ kink condensate is the correct
description of the confined color sector, and \GTE{} and QCD make the same predictions
for color factors, $\beta$-function, and spectroscopy.  Above $\Lambda_{\GTE}$, the
$F_{21}$ discrete substrate flows to the full $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ Yang--Mills theory via the
Burnside density mechanism: because $F_{21} \subset \mathrm{SU}(3)$ is an irreducible
subgroup whose action on the 8-dimensional adjoint fills $\mathrm{SU}(3)/F_{21}$ densely,
all 8 generators of $\mathfrak{su}(3)$ become accessible through $\PHIMDL$ Berry
holonomy.  The algebraic result
\lean{frobenius\_berry\_holonomy\_is\_su3} (\CatAL) establishes that the holonomy group
lies inside $\mathrm{SU}(3)$; the density step uses one named axiom at \CatAD{}
\cite{SpivackGTEQCDStructure}.

The relationship is: \textbf{\GTE{} is the discrete arithmetic theory whose low-energy
effective field theory is QCD.}  GTE derives why $\Nc = 3$, derives the coupling
without a free parameter, and resolves strong CP without an axion --- three things QCD
cannot do.

\section{Forces: The Complete Derivation Summary}
\label{sec:forces_summary}

All force-sector results derived in this chapter trace to the same object: the 19-bit
\GTE{} polynomial $p(L,C,R) = C + R - CR - LCR$ over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$.  The SM's three
gauge coupling constants, the mixing angles, the CP phases, and confinement are not
independent facts to be measured separately.  They are different projections of a single
algebraic structure.

The polynomial $p$ is the unique cubic polynomial over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$ with $K_{\rm
extra} = 0$ (\lean{unique\_cubic\_gravity\_coupling}, \CatAL{}).  Its 19 bits of
specification content determine all force parameters.  No additional input is required
for any of the results in this chapter.

\subsection*{The Common Origin: Degree Projections of One Polynomial}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Electric charge} ($Q = w/3$): degree-1 projection $p(0, w, 0) = w$.
\item \textbf{Weak isospin} ($T_3$): degree-2 cross-tape projection.
\item \textbf{Gravitational coupling} ($G_N$): degree-3 diagonal $p(w,w,w)$ (Ch.~\ref{chap:spacetime}).
\item \textbf{Color charge} ($\Nc = 3$): three-tape architecture $\Rightarrow$ $F_{21} = \ZS \rtimes \mathbb{Z}_3$.
\item \textbf{CP phase} ($\delta_{\rm CP}^{\rm CKM}$): complex phase of the $F_{21}$ quark mass matrix.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{The Completed Four-Force Picture}

\noindent Table~\ref{tab:force_summary} represents the completed derivation of all four
SM force sectors from a single 19-bit polynomial.  Reading the table from top to bottom:

The \textbf{electroweak sector} is entirely derived --- from the Weinberg angle through
the gauge boson masses, the Higgs sector, and the CKM matrix.  The only item classified
below \CatAL{} is the Higgs mass (\CatAD), because the full two-loop self-consistency of
the SRRG VEV insertion is not yet machine-certified.

The \textbf{electromagnetic sector} has the integer part of $1/\alpha$ at \CatAL{}.
The full running to $M_Z$ is \CatB{} pending the hadronic vacuum polarization derivation
(OQ-HVP-6).

The \textbf{QCD sector} has $b_0 = 7$, $\alpha_s(M_Z)$, and $\theta_{\rm QCD} = 0$ all
at \CatAL{}, with $b_0$ identified with $|\ZS|$ by two independent Lean theorems.

The \textbf{gravitational sector} is treated in Chapter~\ref{chap:spacetime}, where the
degree-3 diagonal of $p(w,w,w)$ yields Newton's constant from the same polynomial.

The unification is complete: not a unification of coupling constants at some high-energy
scale (as in conventional GUT models), but an algebraic unification in which all force
parameters are projections of one polynomial at one arithmetic scale.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=yellow!8,colframe=yellow!60!black,title={What Makes This Derivation Different from GUT Unification}]
Grand Unified Theories merge the three SM gauge groups at a high-energy scale and reduce
the three coupling constants to one (at the cost of many new particles, proton-decay
predictions at dimension~6, and a large unification-scale desert).

\GTE{} does something structurally different.  It does not merge the couplings at high
energy.  It derives them as different degree projections of one polynomial at the
arithmetic scale, without introducing new particles, new scales, or new free parameters.
Strong CP is solved by group structure, not by an axion.  Proton decay is forbidden at
dimension~4, not merely suppressed.  The Weinberg angle is an arithmetic ratio $3/13$,
not a running meeting point.  This is a deeper form of unification.
\end{tcolorbox}

\chapter{Spacetime}
\label{chap:spacetime}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=blue!5,colframe=blue!50!black,title={Chapter~8 Claim}]
Spacetime is not a background assumption but an emergent structure: the three-tape
\CMCA{} architecture forces $3+1$ dimensions (\CatAL), geodesics are paths of minimum
computational action (\CatAL, zero custom axioms), Einstein's equations emerge from the
MDL-Lovelock variational principle (\CatAD), and Newton's constant is derived to
$0.040\%$ precision (\CatAD).
\end{tcolorbox}

Chapters~\ref{chap:selection} and~\ref{chap:field} established the algebraic foundation:
the 19-bit polynomial $\poly = C+R-CR-LCR$ over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$ is the unique MDL-minimal
description of a physically self-consistent universe, and the Algebraic Lifting and Descent
Theorems (\S\ref{sec:two_levels}) guarantee a bidirectional correspondence between discrete
\CMCA{} certificates and the continuum field $\PHIMDL$.
What the polynomial alone does not provide is \emph{dynamics}.
The generating polynomial $\poly$ expresses algebraic relationships among fields, charges,
and interaction vertices, but it does not by itself propagate those structures through spacetime,
generate a metric, or specify how events are causally ordered.
A constructive discrete layer --- one that actually runs and traces causal histories ---
is required to bridge the algebraic certificate to physical spacetime.

The \textbf{three-tape Chiral Minkowski Cellular Automaton (\CMCA)} is that layer.
It is not the physical substrate: that role belongs to $\PHIMDL$, the continuum
$\mathbb{Z}_7$-symmetric Klein-Gordon gauge field whose BPS kink solitons are the
elementary particles (Chapter~\ref{chap:field}).
The \CMCA\ is instead the Level~1 algebraic certificate --- the discrete proof system
that certifies which structures $\PHIMDL$ must contain, and whose rules are exactly the
polynomial $\poly$ evaluated over $\mathrm{GF}(7)$.
As developed in Chapter~\ref{chap:field} (\S\ref{sec:cmca}), the \CMCA\ certifies
charge quantization, generation count, Turing universality, baryon number, and color
confinement without being Lorentz-exact; $\PHIMDL$ is the unique continuum realization
with zero Lorentz error, which is why it is the physical substrate and not the \CMCA{}.

Particles are realized differently on the two levels.
At Level~1, a particle is a \emph{stable soliton in the lattice} --- a PSC-admissible
\CMCA{} beable configuration whose $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding number, $\mathbb{Z}_3$ color
charge, and generation orbit position are conserved.
The lattice soliton can be large compared to the Planck scale; it is not a glider in the
Rule~110 sense (gliders play a role in their construction, but elementary particles and
composites are distinct).
At Level~2, the same particle is a \emph{BPS kink soliton in $\PHIMDL$} --- a topological
excitation of the continuum field with the identical algebraic quantum numbers.
What the Lifting and Descent Theorems guarantee is that both realizations carry the same
$\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding number, $\mathbb{Z}_3$ color charge, generation index, and
interaction structure.
These algebraic invariants are the particle; the microstructure at Compton scale is level-dependent.

This chapter derives the consequences.
Section~\ref{sec:dimensions} shows that the three-tape architecture forces exactly $3{+}1$
spacetime dimensions.
Section~\ref{sec:geodesics} establishes geodesics as minimum-computation paths.
Sections~\ref{sec:gr_lovelock}--\ref{sec:newton} derive Einstein's equations, the cosmological
constant, and Newton's constant.
The remainder of this introduction explains why the three-tape construction is the unique
MDL-minimal choice.

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section*{Why Three Tapes? The MDL Minimality Argument}
\label{sec:why_three_tapes}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Why Three Tapes? The MDL Minimality Argument}

The \CMCA\ polynomial defines a $1{+}1$-dimensional cellular automaton.
To obtain $3{+}1$-dimensional spacetime, one must combine multiple tapes.
The question is: how many, and in what configuration?
MDL forces a unique answer.

\subsection*{One tape is insufficient}

A single \CMCA\ tape generates null directions $\ell_+, \ell_-$ along one spatial axis only.
It is intrinsically $1{+}1$-dimensional; the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding structure exists on a
line but cannot be projected into three independent spatial directions.
No metric spanning $\mathbb{R}^{3,1}$ can be constructed from one tape, and the degree-3
diagonal $p(w,w,w) = 2w-w^2-w^3$ that sources gravity has no cross-tape partners
to triangulate curvature in $3{+}1$D.

\subsection*{The three-layer structure of one tape is not three tapes}

A single tape already has three causal layers: the right-chiral outer layer (Rule~110),
the left-chiral outer layer (Rule~124, the spatial mirror of Rule~110, forced by lattice
reflection symmetry at zero MDL cost), and the inner clock that gates proper-time ordering.
These three causal layers within one tape generate the $1{+}1$-dimensional V--A chiral
structure and discrete SR time dilation.
They do not add spatial dimensions: the tape remains $1{+}1$-dimensional, and
correlations between the three layers factor as products of one-point functions on the
same spatial axis.

\subsection*{Two tapes are insufficient}

Two tapes sharing a clock give a $2{+}1$-dimensional system: a pair of null directions per
tape yields null coordinates $(\ell_+^x, \ell_-^x, \ell_+^y, \ell_-^y, t)$, spanning
only $\mathbb{R}^{2,1}$.
The Lorentz group is $\mathrm{SO}(1,2)$, not $\mathrm{SO}(1,3)$.
There is no third generation orbit step and no third spatial color direction.
The cosmological mode count would be $2L$ rather than $3L$, producing the wrong
holographic area-law coefficient.

\subsection*{Three tapes with a shared clock: the MDL-minimal sufficient construction}

Each tape contributes a pair of null directions $\ell_\pm^j$ ($j = x, y, z$).
Together with the shared outer clock $\tau_c^{\rm out}$, they span
$(\ell_+^x, \ell_-^x, \ell_+^y, \ell_-^y, \ell_+^z, \ell_-^z, t)$, which is
$\mathbb{R}^{3,1}$ with signature $(+,+,+,-)$.
Without the shared clock, the three tapes are three independent $1{+}1$D systems; the clock
is both necessary and sufficient (Dimensional Protocol Principle, \S\ref{sec:dimensions}).
The Lorentz algebra $\mathfrak{so}(1,3)$ --- including all 12 commutation relations ---
emerges in the continuum limit \mbox{(\lean{lorentz\_algebra\_complete}, \CatAL)}.

\textbf{Avoiding the voxel penalty.}
A direct 3D lattice encoding $3{+}1$D spacetime requires $L^3$ cells, a state-space of
$7^{L^3}$.
The three-tape \CMCA\ requires only $3 \times L$ cells: state-space $7^{3L}$.
The holographic ratio $3L/L^3 = 3/L^2 \to 0$ as $L\to\infty$, machine-certified by
\lean{holographic\_ratio\_vanishes} \mbox{(\CatAL)}.
This exponential reduction is maximal parsimony under MDL: the three-tape construction
specifies $3{+}1$D spacetime using a surface encoding, with no redundant voxel degrees
of freedom.

\textbf{The holographic mode count and the cosmological constant.}
The mode count $3L$ (rather than $L^3$) suppresses one-loop vacuum contributions by a
factor $\sim 10^{-42}$ relative to the standard QFT estimate (\CatAD-partial;
\S\ref{sec:gr_lovelock}).
No other tape count produces this suppression with the correct exponent.

\subsection*{Four tapes are excluded by MDL}

A fourth tape would add an extra spatial dimension carrying no generation content
(there is no fourth generation orbit type in the $p(w,w,w)$ diagonal), so it costs MDL
bits without adding physical structure.
MDL minimality excludes it.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=blue!5,colframe=blue!50!black,
  title={Convergent Evidence for Three Tapes}]
The three-tape \CMCA\ with shared clock simultaneously delivers, without free parameters:
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.4em]
\item Special relativity (SR time dilation, Lorentz algebra \lean{lorentz\_algebra\_complete}, \CatAL)
\item Exactly $3{+}1$ spacetime dimensions (Dimensional Protocol Principle, \CatAL)
\item Einstein's equations from MDL-Lovelock variational calculus (\CatAD)
\item Holographic Bekenstein area law $S_{\rm BH} = A/4G_N$ (\CatAD)
\item Cosmological constant suppression by $\sim 10^{-42}$ (\CatAD-partial)
\item Born rule probability measure from the three-tape tensor product (\CatAL)
\end{itemize}
This convergence --- SR, GR, Born rule, and CC from a single tape-count argument, all
without free parameters --- is strong evidence that three tapes is the correct, necessary,
and sufficient MDL-minimal certificate for $3{+}1$-dimensional Minkowski spacetime.
The substrate is $\PHIMDL$; the three-tape \CMCA\ is its necessary algebraic certificate.
\end{tcolorbox}
