\section{Dimensions from the Three-Tape Architecture}
\label{sec:dimensions}

Why does space have three dimensions? In the Standard Model this is given by assumption.
In GTE it follows from the orbit structure of the generating polynomial.

\subsection*{From $N_{\rm gen} = 3$ to $D_{\rm spatial} = 3$}

The Garden of Eden (GoE) orbit analysis --- GoE states are CA configurations with no predecessor under the CA rule; the first generation (electron) is a GoE, making it the unique stable starting point --- of the degree-3 diagonal $p(w,w,w) = 2w - w^2 - w^3$
forces three generation orbit types, establishing $N_{\rm gen} = 3$
(\lean{three\_generations\_physical}, \CatAL).
A minimum description-length argument then forces $D_{\rm spatial} = N_{\rm gen} = 3$.
A theory with fewer spatial dimensions cannot accommodate all three generation orbit
steps, each of which requires an independent spatial direction.
A theory with more spatial dimensions introduces axes carrying no generation content,
excluded by MDL minimality.
The result is a theorem:
\begin{equation}
  N_{\rm gen} = 3 \;\Longrightarrow\; D_{\rm spatial} = 3.
  \label{eq:dim_from_ngen}
\end{equation}
Lean-certified:
\lean{three\_dim\_fmdl\_structure\_forced} (\CatAL; P36)
and \lean{gte\_spacetime\_dimension} (\CatAL).

\subsection*{The Dimensional Protocol Principle (DPP)}

Three independent 1+1D \CMCA{}s without synchronisation cannot generate spatial
cross-correlations.
Without a shared clock, each tape evolves on its own causal parameter; correlations
between tapes factor as independent one-point functions; no spatial geometry spans the
three systems.

\input{../common/figures/fig_p41_cell_stack.tex}

\begin{theorem}[Dimensional Protocol Principle]
A shared outer clock $\tau_c^{\rm out}$ is both necessary and sufficient for three
$1{+}1$D \CMCA{}s to produce one $3{+}1$D Minkowski dynamical system.
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textit{Necessity.} Without the shared clock, the three tapes are three
independent $1{+}1$D systems with no cross-tape geometry; no spatial metric connecting
them can be constructed.

\noindent\textit{Sufficiency.} Each tape contributes a pair of null directions
$\ell_\pm^j$ from its Rule~110 and Rule~124 layers.
The shared clock provides the time coordinate $t$.
Together, $(\ell_+^x, \ell_-^x, \ell_+^y, \ell_-^y, \ell_+^z, \ell_-^z, t)$
span $\mathbb{R}^{3,1}$ with signature $(+,+,+,-)$.

Lean certifications:
\lean{dimensional\_protocol\_principle\_master} (\CatAL, zero sorry; P45)
and \lean{cmca\_tensor\_product\_gives\_31d\_minkowski} (\CatAL, zero sorry; P45).

\input{../common/figures/fig_p45_three_rails.tex}

\subsection*{Temporal structure is logically prior to spatial structure}

The DPP reveals a structural asymmetry hidden in standard Minkowski spacetime.
The shared outer clock $\tau_c^{\rm out}$ is not a bookkeeping convenience but the
precondition for spatial geometry: without it, simultaneity across tapes is undefined,
and no spatial metric connecting the tapes can be constructed.
Time, in GTE, is not coordinate-companion to space.
It is the condition that makes space possible.

\subsection*{The $\tau_c$ clock and built-in SR}

The outer computational clock is derived from the Rule~110 ether dynamics.
The ether period is 14 steps; the clock fires every 7 steps.
The proper-time rate is:
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\tau_{\rm inner}}{\tau_{\rm outer}} = \frac{N_{\rm spatial}}{|\ZS|} = \frac{3}{7},
  \label{eq:tau_ratio}
\end{equation}
derived analytically in \lean{EtherProperTimeRate.lean} (\CatAD; P45).
This ratio generates built-in SR time dilation: a particle's internal clock runs
slower than the coordinate clock in proportion to its motion, without any additional
postulate.

\subsection*{The PCT Trinity and the three colours}

The three spatial dimensions coincide with the three QCD colours.
This is not a coincidence.
Every PSC-admissible kink simultaneously (a)~carries Standard Model quantum numbers,
(b)~implements Boolean computation, (c)~sources spacetime curvature.
This triple identity --- particle, computation, spacetime --- is the PCT Trinity,
Lean-certified by \lean{particles\_computation\_spacetime\_trinity}
(\CatAL, zero sorry; P45).
It is the reason $N_{\rm gen} = N_c = N_{\rm tapes} = 3$: all three counts refer to
the same degree-of-freedom structure.

\subsection*{$4$D spacetime is the unique result}

The chain $N_{\rm gen} = 3 \to D_{\rm spatial} = 3 \to 3{+}1$D (via the DPP) gives
exactly four spacetime dimensions.
Not $2{+}1$, not $4{+}1$.
No other dimensionality is compatible with the GoE orbit structure, MDL minimality,
and the PSC three-tape architecture simultaneously.

At finite lattice size $L$, the three-tape \CMCA{} carries exact discrete symmetry
$\mathrm{SO}(1,1)^3 \times S_3$.
The continuum Lorentz group $\mathrm{SO}(1,3)$ is the $L\to\infty$ limit, established
by PSC Presentation Invariance \mbox{(\lean{PSCPILorentzMain}, \CatAL)}.
All 12 commutation relations of the Lorentz algebra --- including the Thomas precession
relation $[K_x, K_y] = -J_z$ --- are machine-certified
(\lean{lorentz\_algebra\_complete}, \CatAL, zero sorry; P45).
The three-tape causal diamond satisfies $V(T) = T^4/4$ with boundary
$\propto \mathrm{Vol}(S^3) = 2\pi^2$ --- the Lorentzian 4D formula arising automatically
from the three-tape structure \mbox{(\lean{three\_tape\_causal\_diamond\_t4}, \CatAL)}.

\section{Geodesics as Minimum-Computation Paths}
\label{sec:geodesics}

In general relativity, free particles follow geodesics --- the extremal paths in
curved spacetime.
In GTE this fact holds for a deeper reason: particles follow the paths that minimise
the PMDL computational action.
Curvature is not a background given; it reflects the computational complexity required
to sustain a physical state.

\subsection*{The Geodesic Computation Theorem}

A PSC-admissible kink propagating through the $\PHIMDL$ field follows the path that
minimises the accumulated PMDL action.
In the continuum limit this path is a geodesic of the metric induced by the variational
principle.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,title={Theorem: The Geodesic Computation Theorem (\CatAL, zero custom axioms)}]
PSC-admissible matter kinks propagating through $\Phi_{\mathrm{MDL}}$ follow geodesics of
minimum computational action. That is, the worldline of a PSC-admissible kink minimises
the PMDL description-length action $S_{\mathrm{PMDL}}$.

\textbf{Lean certs} (both zero sorry, zero custom axioms beyond standard foundations):
\lean{psc\_orbit\_is\_curvature\_geodesic} and \lean{dweight\_centroid\_tracks\_geodesic}
(\texttt{Spacetime/GeodesicTheorem.lean}, \texttt{ugp-lean}).

This replaces the geodesic postulate of general relativity with a theorem: free fall is the
path of least computational description.
\end{tcolorbox}

Four Lean certifications underpin this claim:
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{psc\_orbit\_is\_curvature\_geodesic}
  (\CatAL, zero custom axioms; P43) --- PSC-admissible orbits follow curvature geodesics.
\item \lean{dweight\_centroid\_tracks\_geodesic}
  (\CatAL, zero custom axioms; P43) --- the $[D]$-weighted centroid of a kink
  state tracks the geodesic (the GTE Ehrenfest theorem for gravity).
\item \lean{massive\_timelike\_geodesic} (\CatAL; P36) --- massive kinks follow
  timelike geodesics.
\item \lean{photon\_null\_geodesic} (\CatAL; P36) --- massless (photon-type) kinks
  follow null geodesics.
\end{itemize}
The phrase \emph{zero custom axioms} means zero axioms beyond the standard Mathlib
foundations --- the strongest certification level achievable in Lean~4.

The Weak Equivalence Principle is a corollary:
\lean{gte\_equivalence\_principle} (\CatAL; P36) states that no local
PSC-admissible experiment can distinguish free fall from inertial motion.
This is not an additional postulate in GTE; it is a consequence of the arithmetic
structure of the generating polynomial.

\subsection*{The $\tau_c$ computational metric}

The ether clock rate $\tau_c$ at a spacetime point measures how much computational
work is required to sustain the local $\PHIMDL$ field configuration.
In the presence of matter kinks, $\tau_c$ is elevated: more computation is required
to maintain the concentrated field energy.
The gradient of $\tau_c$ acts as an effective gravitational potential
$\Phi_{\rm grav} \propto -\nabla\tau_c$.

A kink moving from a high-$\tau_c$ region to a low-$\tau_c$ region gains proper
time relative to coordinate time --- exactly the gravitational time dilation of GR.
Three equivalent formulations are established at \CatAD{} (P36, P38, P45):
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textit{Gradient kick.}
  $\delta v \propto \nabla\tau_c$ (the Newtonian force, derived);
\item \textit{Metric perturbation.}
  $g_{00} = -(1 + 2\Phi_{\rm grav}/c^2)$ in Newtonian gauge (the metric perturbation);
\item \textit{Equivalence principle.}
  \lean{gte\_equivalence\_principle} (\CatAL) --- no local observable distinguishes
  free fall from inertial motion.
\end{enumerate}
All three formulations are equivalent and derive from the same $\tau_c$ gradient-kick
mechanism of the three-tape \CMCA{}.

\subsection*{The Gorard chain: from Rule~110 to smooth curvature}

To see how discrete arithmetic generates smooth spacetime, it helps to hold a mental
model of the three-level description.  At Level~0, the \MDL-minimal function $f_{\MDL}$
is Rule~110 on the SM generation orbit --- a finite arithmetic statement certified in
Lean.  At Level~1, Rule~110 on a large spatial tape generates a causal graph
$\mathcal{G}_L$ whose Ollivier--Ricci curvature is a well-defined discrete geometric
quantity.  At Level~2, in the continuum limit $L\to\infty$, this discrete curvature
converges to the smooth Riemann tensor and the vacuum Einstein equations emerge from the
Ricci-flat condition.  At each level, the framework checks consistency: the arithmetic
level is \CatAL; the discrete-geometry level is \CatAL; the continuum bridge is \CatAD,
pending Mathlib Riemannian geometry support (OQ-QG-1).  The key insight is that no
additional physical postulate is introduced at any transition: the passage
\mbox{arithmetic $\to$ causal graph $\to$ smooth spacetime} is forced by \MDL{}
and \PSC.

The bridge from discrete Rule~110 orbit dynamics to smooth spacetime curvature follows
four steps.
\begin{enumerate}
\item $f_{\rm MDL}$ selects Rule~110 on the SM generation orbit
  (CUP-4, \CatAL).
\item Rule~110 on a large spatial tape generates a causal graph $\mathcal{G}_L$.
\item Gorard geometry: the discrete Ollivier-Ricci curvature on $\mathcal{G}_L$
  satisfies
  \begin{equation}
    \CGorard = \frac{N_{\rm spatial}}{2D^2} = \frac{3}{32},
    \label{eq:c_gorard}
  \end{equation}
  exact, machine-certified:
  \lean{c\_gorard\_eq\_n\_spatial\_over\_two\_dsq} (\CatAL).
  The SD-deviation coefficient is $\kSD = 10/13$ (exact; \lean{GorardRationalFormula},
  \CatAL).
\item In the vacuum ether state, Gorard curvature vanishes: $\kappa = 0$ at all fixed
  points, establishing exact Ricci flatness of empty spacetime
  (\lean{three\_tape\_gorard\_vacuum\_ricci\_flat}, \CatAL).
  In the presence of a matter kink, $\kappa = +1$ at the kink site and $0$ elsewhere.
\end{enumerate}
In the large-$L$ continuum limit, the Gorard Ollivier-Ricci curvature on
$\mathcal{G}_L$ converges to the smooth Riemann curvature tensor, and the vacuum
Einstein equations emerge from the Gorard Ricci-flat condition.

\textit{Status.}
The algebraic level of the Gorard chain is \CatAL.
The formal Gromov-Hausdorff convergence of the \CMCA{} causal graph to smooth
Riemannian spacetime --- the continuum bridge --- is established at \CatAD{}:
the structural argument is complete, and formal Lean certification of manifold
convergence is open, blocked on Mathlib Riemannian geometry (open problem OQ-QG-1).

\section{Einstein's Equations from MDL-Lovelock}
\label{sec:gr_lovelock}

Einstein's equations relate spacetime curvature to matter and energy.
In standard GR they are derived by postulating the Einstein-Hilbert action.
In GTE, that action is the unique MDL-minimal generally-covariant action in four
dimensions --- a theorem, not a postulate.

\subsection*{The MDL-Lovelock correspondence}

Lovelock's theorem characterises all second-order generally-covariant field equations in
$d$ dimensions: in $d = 4$, the unique such equations are Einstein's with a cosmological
constant.
MDL minimality selects the simplest action consistent with the symmetry constraints.
The two principles --- physical necessity (Lovelock) and computational necessity (MDL)
--- identify the same object: the Einstein-Hilbert action.

The formal dictionary, established at \CatAD{} (P35, P36, P38) \cite{SpivackEmergentGravity,SpivackPhiMDLGravity}:

\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering
\caption{The MDL-Lovelock correspondence: GTE concepts and their GR counterparts.
This dictionary shows that the PMDL variational principle and Lovelock's uniqueness
theorem identify the same gravitational action, grounding the Einstein equations in
MDL minimality rather than an independent geometric postulate.}
\label{tab:mdl_lovelock}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.25}
\begin{tabular}{P{5.5cm}P{6.5cm}}
\toprule
GTE concept & GR counterpart \\
\midrule
PMDL action ($\PHIMDL$ on curved background) & Einstein-Hilbert + matter action \\
MDL minimality & Lovelock uniqueness \\
$\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian & Klein-Gordon + $V(\phi)$ + $\xi R\phi^2/2$ \\
$\xi = 0$ (forced; see below) & Minimal coupling \\
$\ZS$ kink sources & $T_{\mu\nu}$ \\
Gorard discrete Ricci curvature & Riemann tensor $R$ \\
Vacuum ether state & Ricci-flat background \\
Holographic mode count $3L$ & Bekenstein area law \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection*{Why $\xi = 0$ is forced}

The non-minimal coupling $\xi R\phi^2/2$ is excluded by three independent arguments,
all \CatAD{} (P44) \cite{SpivackQGR}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textit{MDL minimality.} The $\xi R$ term increases the description length of
  the Lagrangian by introducing a new parameter; MDL minimality excludes it.
\item \textit{Wald entropy consistency.} A non-zero $\xi$ modifies the Wald entropy
  formula for black holes; consistency with the Bekenstein-Hawking result requires
  $\xi = 0$.
\item \textit{$\ZS$ topological constraint.} The winding-sector topology of $\PHIMDL$
  is incompatible with a non-zero $\xi$ coupling in the presence of horizon topology
  changes.
\end{enumerate}
All three independently require $\xi = 0$.
With this established, the $\PHIMDL$ Lagrangian on a curved background is uniquely
determined.

\subsection*{The stress-energy tensor (CatAD)}

The $\PHIMDL$ stress-energy tensor is derived analytically from the Lagrangian:
\begin{equation}
  T_{\mu\nu} = \partial_\mu\PHIMDL\,\partial_\nu\PHIMDL
               - g_{\mu\nu}\!\left[\tfrac{1}{2}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_\alpha\PHIMDL\partial_\beta\PHIMDL
               - V(\PHIMDL)\right].
  \label{eq:tmunu}
\end{equation}
Machine-certified in \lean{StressEnergyTensor.lean} (\CatAL).
The integrated kink rest-mass energy gives:
\begin{equation}
  \int T_{00}\,d^3x = M_{\rm kink} = 290.10\;\text{MeV},
  \label{eq:kink_mass_integral}
\end{equation}
verified against the BPS kink profile (CatAL; P43, P38).

\subsection*{Einstein's field equations (CatAD)}

Variation of the PMDL action with respect to the metric yields the full nonlinear
Einstein field equations:
\begin{equation}
  G_{\mu\nu} + \Lambda g_{\mu\nu} = 8\pi \GN\,T_{\mu\nu}[\PHIMDL].
  \label{eq:EFE}
\end{equation}
The three left-hand side components are derived independently.
$G_{\mu\nu}$ emerges from the Gorard chain (discrete Ricci curvature on the \CMCA{}
causal graph converging to the smooth Einstein tensor; \S\ref{sec:geodesics}).
$\Lambda$ is established in the following subsection.
$\GN$ is derived in \S\ref{sec:newton}.
The cross-tape coupling established in Chapter~\ref{chap:forces} ---
\lean{gravity\_requires\_cross\_tape\_coordination} (\CatAL) --- is the algebraic
source: the degree-3 diagonal $p(w,w,w) = 2w - w^2 - w^3$ contributes only when
all three tapes are present, which is why gravity requires $3{+}1$D and cannot be
localised to a single tape.

\subsection*{Classical $\Lambda = 0$ (CatAL)}

The $\ZS$ symmetry of the GTE potential enforces a zero classical cosmological
constant.
The potential satisfies $V(\phi_k) = 0$ at each of the seven $\ZS$ vacuum sectors
$k = 0,\ldots,6$.
Because all seven vacua are equiprobable and each contributes zero vacuum energy,
the classical cosmological constant vanishes identically.
Three Lean theorems certify this chain:
\lean{z7\_vacuum\_sectors\_equiprobable} (\CatAL),
\lean{phimdl\_tmunu\_vacuum\_zero} (\CatAL),
\lean{classical\_lambda\_zero} (\CatAL; P38, P44).
There is no fine-tuning: $\Lambda_{\rm classical} = 0$ is a consequence of the $\ZS$
symmetry group, not an adjustment.

\subsection*{Quantum correction and the CC hierarchy}

One-loop corrections from the Coleman-Weinberg potential give
$\Delta V_{\rm CW} \approx -1.7\times10^7\;\text{MeV}^4$, roughly $10^{42}$ above
the observed dark energy density $\rho_\Lambda^{\rm obs} \approx 10^{-35}\;\text{MeV}^4$.
The holographic mode count $3L$ (rather than $L^3$) suppresses one-loop contributions
by a factor $\sim 10^{-42}$ relative to the standard QFT estimate (\CatAD-partial).
The complete quantum protection argument is linked to the geometric continuum limit and
remains open; it is the dominant open problem in the gravitational sector.

The non-zero observed $\Omega_\Lambda$ is not a classical cosmological constant.
It arises from the PSC adjudication floor $\Dres > 0$ --- the irreducible entropy cost
of physical undecidability.
This is a non-perturbative structural contribution, not a loop correction.
Chapter~\ref{chap:cosmology} derives $\Omega_\Lambda$ from first principles via the
$\Dres$ mechanism.

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Newton's Constant and the Gravitational Hierarchy}
\label{sec:newton}

Why is gravity so weak?
In the Standard Model the ratio $\MPl/\mtau \approx 6.87\times10^{18}$ is a
free parameter.
In GTE it is a theorem of the orbit decomposition of the \CMCA{} neighbourhood space.

\subsection*{The $b_0 = |\ZS|$ identity}

The one-loop QCD $\beta$-function coefficient with $N_c = 3$ colours and
$N_f = 6$ active flavours is:
\begin{equation}
  b_0 = \frac{11N_c - 2N_f}{3}
      = \frac{11\cdot3 - 2\cdot6}{3}
      = \frac{33-12}{3} = 7.
  \label{eq:b0}
\end{equation}
The GTE substrate contains the Frobenius group
$F_{21} = \ZS \rtimes \mathbb{Z}_3$ of order $|F_{21}| = 21$,
whose unique normal $\ZS$ subgroup has order $|\ZS| = 7$.
The identity $b_0 = |\ZS| = 7$ is a theorem:
writing $11N_c = 11|\mathbb{Z}_3| = 33$ and $2N_f = 2\cdot2N_{\rm gen} = 12$,
\[
  b_0 = \frac{|F_{21}|}{|\mathbb{Z}_3|} = |\ZS| = 7.
\]
Machine-certified by \lean{b0\_eq\_z7\_order}
(\lean{BetaCoefficientIdentity.lean}, zero sorry; P38).

\subsection*{\CMCA{} orbit decomposition}

The \CMCA{} rule operates on 3-cell neighbourhoods drawn from $\ZS^3$ (343 states).
The Frobenius group $F_{21}$ acts diagonally on $\ZS^3$ by
$(a,s)\cdot x = 2^s x + a$ (componentwise).
By Burnside's lemma, this action has $(7^3 + 14)/21 = 17$ orbits, decomposing as:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
Neighbourhood type & States & Orbit size & Orbits ($n_{\rm type}$) \\
\midrule
All-equal $(k,k,k)$ & $7$ & $7$ & $1$ \\
Exactly-two-equal & $126$ & $21$ & $6$ \\
All-distinct & $210$ & $21$ & $10$ \\
\midrule
Total & $343$ & --- & $17$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The number of degenerate orbits is $1 + 6 = 7 = b_0 = |\ZS|$: the QCD one-loop
$\beta$-coefficient equals the number of degenerate $F_{21}$-orbit types on $\ZS^3$.
The number of generic (all-distinct) orbits is $n = 10$.
Machine-certified by \lean{gte\_planck\_cascade\_group\_identity}
(\lean{BetaCoefficientIdentity.lean}).

\subsection*{The Planck hierarchy formula}

Let $n = 10$ (generic orbits) and $b_0 = 7$ (degenerate orbits).
The Planck-scale ratio satisfies:
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\MPl}{\mtau}
  = \frac{|F_{21}|^n\,|\ZS|^{b_0}}{2}
  = \frac{21^{10}\times 7^7}{2}
  = 6.8683\times10^{18},
  \label{eq:planck_hierarchy}
\end{equation}
against the PDG value $\MPl/\mtau = 6.871\times10^{18}$
($\MPl = 1.220\times10^{22}$~MeV, $\mtau = 1776.86$~MeV),
a relative error of $\mathbf{0.040\%}$
(\CatAD; P38 \S hierarchy, \lean{gte\_gravity\_mass\_hierarchy}).

Every parameter in~\eqref{eq:planck_hierarchy} is independently derived:
$n = 10$ counts the all-distinct $F_{21}$-orbit types (Burnside, \CatAL);
$b_0 = 7 = |\ZS|$ is the QCD $\beta$-coefficient and the normal subgroup order
(\CatAL);
$|F_{21}| = 21$ and $|\ZS| = 7$ are the group orders of the GTE substrate.
There are no free parameters.
The expression admits an MDL interpretation: $\ln(\MPl/\mtau) = n\ln|F_{21}| +
b_0\ln|\ZS| - \ln 2$ is the total orbit entropy of the \CMCA{} neighbourhood space.

\subsection*{Newton's constant}

The derivation of Newton's constant follows immediately:
\begin{equation}
  \GN = \frac{\mtau^2}{\MPl^2}
      = \frac{(1776.86\;\text{MeV})^2}{(1.220\times10^{22}\;\text{MeV})^2}.
  \label{eq:GN}
\end{equation}
The algebraic structure of this derivation chain is machine-certified in
\lean{PMDLGravityTheorems.lean} (\CatAL; full derivation chain \CatAD; P38, P43) \cite{SpivackPhiMDLGravity}.

\subsection*{The hierarchy is dissolved, not solved}

In the Standard Model, the ratio $\MPl/\mtau \sim 10^{18}$ is an unexplained
numerical coincidence.
No mechanism within the SM predicts it; the hierarchy problem consists precisely
in the absence of such a mechanism.

In GTE the ratio is~\eqref{eq:planck_hierarchy}: $F_{21}^{10} \times 7^7 / 2$.
The weakness of gravity is the consequence of raising the Frobenius group to the tenth
power --- one factor per all-distinct orbit type in the \CMCA{} neighbourhood space.
There is no problem to dissolve at the SM level because no SM explanation was ever
available; the GTE explanation closes the question.

\subsection*{Newtonian gravity from $\tau_c$ gradient kicks}

The three-tape \CMCA{} produces Newtonian gravity from the $\tau_c$ gradient-kick
mechanism (\CatAD; P36, P45) \cite{SpivackEmergentGravity}.
The Poisson equation
$\nabla^2\Phi_{\rm grav} = 4\pi\GN\rho$
follows from the three-dimensional Green's function of the $\tau_c$ field.
In the far field ($b \gg \sigma_{\rm AL}$) the force law is:
\begin{equation}
  F(b) = \frac{\GN M}{4\pi b^2},

\end{equation}
the standard Newtonian inverse-square law.
Corrections at $b \sim \sigma_{\rm AL}$ (the algebraic length) are computable

\section{Black Hole Entropy and Hawking Radiation}
\label{sec:bh}

Black holes are among the most constrained laboratories for quantum gravity.
Two fundamental properties --- the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy and Hawking radiation ---
are derived in GTE by independent routes, providing strong internal consistency
evidence.

\subsection*{Bekenstein-Hawking entropy: two independent routes (\CatAD)}

The Bekenstein-Hawking formula
\begin{equation}
  S_{\rm BH} = \frac{A\,\MPl^2}{4}
  \label{eq:SBH}
\end{equation}
(in natural units $8\pi\GN = \MPl^{-2}$)
is established by two independent derivations.

\textit{Route~1: holographic mode count.}
The three-tape \CMCA{} has area-law scaling: the number of independent modes in a
spatial region of boundary area $A$ scales as $3L$ rather than $L^3$
\mbox{(\lean{three\_tape\_holographic\_L7}, \CatAL; P45)}.
Combined with the PMDL variational principle, this gives the area-law entropy of
equation~\eqref{eq:SBH}.
This route implements the Ryu-Takayanagi formula for arbitrary subregions without
assuming an AdS geometry (\CatAD; P44) \cite{SpivackQGR}.

\textit{Route~2: Gorard causal diamond.}
The Gorard Ricci-flat vacuum condition, combined with the area scaling of the
three-tape causal diamond $V(T) = T^4/4$, yields equation~\eqref{eq:SBH} directly
from the discrete geometry (\CatAD; P44, P43).

Agreement between two independent routes --- holographic mode counting and discrete
causal geometry --- provides strong evidence that both derivations are capturing the
same underlying physics.

\subsection*{Hawking radiation as kink emission (\CatAD)}

Near a black hole horizon, the $\PHIMDL$ field supports kink emission.
The Hawking temperature is unmodified by the field mass:
\begin{equation}
  T_H = \frac{\MPl^2}{8\pi M_{\rm BH}},
  \label{eq:TH}
\end{equation}
machine-certified by \lean{phimdl\_hawking\_unchanged}
(\lean{UgpLean.Gravity.HawkingRadiation}, zero sorry; P44) \cite{SpivackQGR}.

A critical mass is identified:
\begin{equation}
  M_{\rm crit} = M_{\rm kink} = 290.10\;\text{MeV},
  \label{eq:Mcrit}
\end{equation}
machine-certified by \lean{phimdl\_m\_crit\_formula} (\CatAL; P44).
For $M_{\rm BH} > M_{\rm crit}$, emission proceeds at the standard Hawking rate.
For $M_{\rm BH} < M_{\rm crit}$, the Hawking temperature exceeds $\mtau$ and
tau-scale kink emission is exponentially suppressed; the emission spectrum deviates
from the standard thermal form.
Detection of a sub-$M_{\rm crit}$ departure from the standard Hawking spectrum
would constitute direct experimental evidence for $\PHIMDL$ kink dynamics.

\subsection*{Singularity resolution (\CatAD)}

The \CMCA{} lattice has a maximum packing density $\rho_{\rm max} = \MPl^4$:
no further kinks can be placed in a Planck-scale volume beyond this density.
This is a UV cutoff imposed by the finiteness of the $\ZS^3$ neighbourhood space,
not a choice.
The classical black hole singularity, which arises when the continuum field theory is
extrapolated to arbitrary energy density, is resolved at $\rho_{\rm max}$:
the field equations remain well-defined, and the causal graph remains finite, at all
physical densities (\CatAD; P43, P44).

\subsection*{Information non-loss (\CatAD)}

The PSC-consistent record structure prevents information erasure at horizons.
A PSC-admissible transition that erases a $[D]$-record is excluded by the
topological stability of winding sectors: no PSC-consistent unitary can map a
non-trivial winding sector to the vacuum
(\lean{topological\_kink\_stability}, \CatAL).
Information encoded in winding numbers is preserved through the Hawking emission
process.
The black hole information paradox is resolved by the topological protection of
$\ZS$ winding numbers, not by a new dynamical mechanism.

\section{Quantum Gravity: What the GTE Framework Resolves}
\label{sec:quantum_gravity}

Quantum gravity --- the reconciliation of general relativity with quantum mechanics
--- is one of the central open problems of theoretical physics.
GTE does not quantise general relativity separately from matter.
Instead, spacetime geometry and matter fields both emerge from the same 19-bit
generating polynomial.
The question ``how do we quantise gravity?'' does not arise in its standard form:
the gravitational sector is not a separate field added to the SM, but a projection of
the same \CMCA{} structure.

\subsection*{Six quantum-gravity benchmarks}

The completeness of the \GTE{} quantum-gravity framework is assessed against six
benchmark criteria, following the functional-completeness programme of P44.
These benchmarks represent the minimum requirements any viable quantum gravity
framework must satisfy: (1) it must reproduce GR in the appropriate limit; (2) it
must account for the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy formula; (3) it must resolve classical
singularities without a separate UV regulator; (4) it must reproduce Hawking radiation;
(5) it must be UV-finite on curved backgrounds; and (6) it must prevent information
loss at horizons.  Together they constitute a minimal GR-consistency checklist that
separates genuine quantum gravity proposals from incomplete approaches.

\subsection*{The GTE route to quantum gravity}

\textit{No quantisation step.}
Standard approaches to quantum gravity --- loop quantum gravity, string theory,
asymptotic safety --- begin with classical GR and apply a quantisation procedure.
GTE has no such step.
The $\PHIMDL$ field is already quantum: its kink excitations are the matter particles
of Chapter~\ref{chap:particles}, the field is second-quantised on a Fock space
(\lean{phimdl\_is\_second\_quantised}, \CatAL; P42), and the Hilbert space is built
from the $\ZS$ winding sectors.
Gravity is not quantised from above; it emerges from below, as a collective property
of the kink substrate.

\textit{UV structure.}
At Level~1 (the \CMCA{} certificate), the neighbourhood space is finite ($\ZS^3$,
343 states), so there is no UV divergence problem at the algebraic level.
At Level~2 ($\PHIMDL$ on curved backgrounds), the DeWitt-Schwinger heat-kernel
expansion classifies curved-background UV divergences as Types~1--4.
With $\xi = 0$ (established in \S\ref{sec:gr_lovelock}), all Types~1--3
curved-background-specific power-law divergences are eliminated, and Type~4
logarithmic corrections generate finite Planck-scale renormalisations
(\lean{phimdl\_uv\_curved\_type\_classification}, \CatAL; P44).
The theory has no UV power-law divergences beyond those of the flat-space theory.

\textit{Quantum-gravity scale.}
The Planck length $\ell_{\rm Pl} = 1/\MPl$ sets the minimum resolvable scale in the
\CMCA{} causal graph.
The bound $4.21\times10^{19}\,\ell_{\rm Pl}$ provides a UV spatial bound: sub-kink
curvature divergences are absent.
The Algebraic Lifting Theorem provides the UV-complete description at all scales
(\CatAD; P43).

\chapter{Quantum Mechanics}
\label{chap:qm}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=blue!5,colframe=blue!50!black,title={Chapter~9 Claim}]
The Born rule is derived, not postulated \mbox{(\lean{born\_rule\_unconditional}, \CatAL, zero custom axioms)};
quantum measurement is replaced by PSC-forced transputation --- a
non-computable, non-random branch selection that is the only option compatible with PSC
closure; and wave--particle duality, Bell non-locality, and the measurement problem
are resolved from first principles.
\end{tcolorbox}

Quantum mechanics rests on five postulates.  Four of them describe how physical states
evolve, how observables are represented, and how composite systems are formed.  The fifth
--- the Born rule, $P(k) = |c_k|^2$ --- tells you the probability of each measurement
outcome.  No derivation is offered; the Born rule is postulated, full stop.

In GTE, this asymmetry is resolved.  The Born rule is a theorem derived from the
$\mathbb{Z}_7$ superselection structure of the substrate, with zero custom axioms
(\lean{born\_rule\_unconditional}, \CatAL).  Three independent corroborating derivations
converge on the same result.  Quantum measurement is replaced by \emph{transputation}
--- a formally characterised, law-governed, non-computable branch selection that is
neither determinism nor randomness nor many-worlds, but is forced upon any closed
universe that bears records and can refer to itself.

This chapter delivers three things.  First, the Born rule is derived in four independent
ways, establishing that quantum mechanics has four postulates, not five.  Second,
transputation is defined precisely, placed in the \emph{TPC computability class}
(Decidable $\subsetneq$ TPC $\subsetneq$ Hypercomputation, \CatAL), and proved
to be forced by PSC closure via an eight-step Lean-certified chain.  Third, the
measurement problem is dissolved rather than solved: wave--particle duality, Bell
non-locality, the quantum eraser, and Physical Incompleteness all follow from the same
two-level beable/$\Phi_{\MDL}$ ontology.

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{The Measurement Problem: Four Routes to the Born Rule}
\label{sec:born}

The measurement problem is the central unresolved question of quantum foundations.
When a quantum system described by a superposition
$|\psi\rangle = \sum_k c_k |k\rangle$ is measured, it yields a definite
outcome~$k$ with probability~$|c_k|^2$.  Standard quantum mechanics provides no mechanism
for \emph{why} a definite outcome occurs, nor any derivation of the~$|c_k|^2$ probability
rule: both are postulates.  The so-called ``measurement problem'' is the observation that
these postulates are logically independent of the four dynamical axioms of quantum
mechanics --- there is no reason, internal to the theory, why the Born rule should hold.

In GTE the measurement problem is resolved by the combination of two theorems.
The Born rule is derived (not postulated), and the selection of outcomes is handled by
transputation (not collapse).  This section delivers the first half: four independent
derivations of the Born rule that converge on the same formula.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={Result: The Born Rule Is a Theorem (\CatAL)}]
  In the GTE $\mathbb{Z}_7$ kink Hilbert space, for any normalised state
  $|\psi\rangle = \sum_k c_k\,|\text{kink}_k\rangle$ and any PSC-admissible
  measurement,
  \[
    P(k) = |c_k|^2.
  \]
  Derived from $\mathbb{Z}_7$ superselection alone.  Zero free parameters.
  Zero custom physics axioms.  Lean cert: \lean{born\_rule\_unconditional},
  \lean{BeableWindingPartitionInstance.lean}~\cite{SpivackQMFromR110,SpivackCompleteness}.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{Four Independent Routes}

\paragraph{Route~$\alpha$ --- $\mathbb{Z}_7$ Superselection (CatAL, Primary).}

The $\Phi_{\MDL}$ kink Hilbert space (Ch.~\ref{chap:field}) decomposes into
superselection sectors labelled by the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding number $W \in
\{0,1,\ldots,6\}$.  The $\mathbb{Z}_7$ global shift symmetry $\phi \to \phi +
2\pi/7$ is anomaly-free and forbids any observable that mixes distinct winding sectors.
The only permissible probability assignment over sectors~$\{0,2,3,4,6\}$ (the
PSC-admissible sector) that is consistent with this superselection structure and with
the canonical inner product on the kink space is precisely $P(k) = |c_k|^2$.

This is the Coleman--Mandelstam--Rajaraman superselection argument applied to
$\mathbb{Z}_7$ charge: any POVM-additive effect algebra on the kink space that
respects the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ block structure must assign Born weights.  The proof
uses no quantum axioms beyond the Hilbert space inner product and the superselection
rule.  Lean certs: \lean{born\_rule\_unconditional} (zero sorry, zero custom axioms,
\lean{BeableWindingPartitionInstance.lean}) and
\lean{born\_rule\_unconditional\_master\_bundle} (\CatAL).

\paragraph{Route~$\beta$ --- PSC Closure Uniqueness via Busch--Gleason (CatAL).}

PSC closure (Ch.~\ref{chap:principle}) forces the set of physically admissible
quantum probability assignments to form a noncontextual, POVM-additive effect
algebra.  By the Busch--Gleason representation theorem, the unique
probability measure on such an algebra that is consistent with the PSC invariance
group is the trace-class Born measure $P(k) = \mathrm{tr}(\rho\,\Pi_k)$.
Lean certs: \lean{born\_rule\_from\_psc}, \lean{born\_rule\_projectors\_from\_psc}
(NemS.Quantum.BornFromPSC, zero sorry, \CatAL; NEMS Paper~13).

Routes $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are logically independent.  Route~$\alpha$ uses the
$\mathbb{Z}_7$ superselection structure of the \emph{kink Hilbert space}; Route~$\beta$
uses PSC closure on the \emph{abstract effect algebra}.  They rest on different premises
and converge on the same conclusion.

\paragraph{Route~$\gamma$ --- 't~Hooft Information-Loss Regime (CatAD).}

The CA function $f_{\MDL}$ on $\mathbb{Z}_7^5$ is a Chapter-7 information-loss
CA: 98.71\% of phase-space states are $\mathit{GoE}$ states that irreversibly
reach the vacuum attractor within~$\leq 7$ steps.  A quantum measurement asking
``which $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding sector does this CA configuration occupy?'' receives
its probabilistic answer from the information-loss structure of the dynamics.  The
Born probabilities arise as the invariant measure of the residual non-GoE states
\cite{SpivackQMFromR110}.
Cert: \CatAD\ (structural derivation, P37 §4.1; Lean cert for this route is open ---
the CA-to-QFT embedding for the full 't~Hooft route awaits the G42 Jackiw--Rebbi +
GNS construction, P37 §5.5).

\paragraph{Route~$\delta$ --- Page--Wootters $\tau_c$ Clock (CatAD).}

The three-tape shared causal clock~$\tau_c$ (derived in Ch.~\ref{chap:spacetime},
§\ref{sec:dimensions} from Rule-110 ether dynamics; proper-time ratio $\tau_c = 3/7$)
satisfies all prerequisites for the Page--Wootters mechanism
\cite{PageWootters1983}: it has a non-degenerate Hamiltonian, mutually
orthogonal clock states, and decouples from the system Hamiltonian.

Consider the timeless universe state
\[
  |\Psi\rangle = T^{-1/2}\sum_\tau |\tau\rangle_{\mathrm{clock}}\otimes U(\tau)|\psi_0\rangle,
\]
satisfying the Wheeler--DeWitt constraint $H_{\mathrm{total}}|\Psi\rangle = 0$.
The conditional probability of finding outcome~$k$ given clock reading~$\tau$ is
\[
  P(k|\tau) = |\langle k|U(\tau)|\psi_0\rangle|^2.
\]
This is the Born rule, derived from the relational time structure of the GTE three-tape
substrate.  The derivation requires zero custom physics axioms beyond the
$\tau_c$ clock construction already established in Ch.~\ref{chap:spacetime}.
Cert: \CatAD\ (analytical, P46~§7.4 Theorem~7.4; computational confirmation
via \texttt{pw\_born\_rule\_verification.py} \cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}).

\paragraph{What the four routes establish.}

Routes $\alpha$ and $\beta$ establish the Born rule as a structural necessity of the GTE
substrate at certification level \CatAL.  Routes $\gamma$ and $\delta$ establish it
independently from the CA dynamics and the relational-time architecture at \CatAD.
All four give $P(k) = |c_k|^2$.  This multiple-routes convergence provides strong
evidence that the Born rule is not an artefact of one derivation strategy but a robust
structural property of any PSC-consistent theory.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=yellow!8,colframe=yellow!60!black,
  title={Why This Is Not a Circular Argument}]
  A circular derivation would assume some form of the Born rule to derive it.
  None of the four routes do this.  Route~$\alpha$ uses only the $\mathbb{Z}_7$
  superselection structure and the Hilbert-space inner product.  Route~$\beta$ uses
  PSC closure and Gleason's theorem, with no Born-rule input.  Route~$\gamma$ uses
  the CA information-loss measure.  Route~$\delta$ uses the Wheeler--DeWitt
  timeless formalism and the $\tau_c$ clock.  The Born rule emerges in each case
  as the output, not as a hidden assumption.
\end{tcolorbox}

\paragraph{Quantum mechanics has four postulates, not five.}

The five standard postulates of quantum mechanics are: (1)~state space is a
Hilbert space; (2)~observables are self-adjoint operators; (3)~dynamics are unitary
(Schr\"{o}dinger equation); (4)~composite systems are tensor products;
(5)~the Born rule.  In GTE, postulate~(5) is a theorem derived from the $\mathbb{Z}_7$
substrate structure.  The four remaining postulates are consistent with and supported
by GTE: the $\Phi_{\MDL}$ field naturally lives in a Hilbert space (Ch.~\ref{chap:field}),
observables are winding-sector projectors (self-adjoint), and
unitary dynamics follow from the $\mathbb{Z}_7$-anomaly-free symmetry.

The measurement problem --- why definite outcomes occur at all, and why with
probabilities $|c_k|^2$ --- splits into two independent questions in GTE.  The second
(``why $|c_k|^2$?'') is answered by the four routes above.  The first (``why
definite outcomes?'') is answered by transputation, to which we now turn.

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Transputation: The Fourth Option}
\label{sec:transputation}

Consider the three standard options for what happens during a quantum measurement.
\emph{Determinism} says that hidden variables determine the outcome in advance:
the particle always had a definite value, we simply did not know it.
\emph{Randomness} says the universe genuinely rolls dice: each outcome is selected by
an irreducibly stochastic process with no further structure.
\emph{Many-worlds} says all outcomes occur simultaneously in branching parallel realities.

GTE establishes that all three are incorrect, and proves a fourth option is forced
upon any universe satisfying PSC.  The correct description is \emph{transputation}:
a formally characterised, law-governed, non-computable branch selection.  It is not
the absence of determinism.  It is the presence of something structurally different ---
a process that is lawful (obeying five precise constraints D1--D5) but non-algorithmic
(no Turing machine can implement it on the self-referential diagonal fragment).

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=green!5,colframe=green!50!black,
  title={Definition: Transputation}]
  Transputation is the law-governed internal adjudication process required and formally
  forced in any closed, record-bearing, diagonal-capable physical framework.  It is the
  formal role of the operator~$P^\top$ that resolves record-divergent choice --- identifying
  which PSC-consistent realization is canonical given a macroscopic record~$w$ --- when
  that selection cannot be performed by any total-effective procedure.
  Formally: $P^\top_D(w) = \operatorname{argmin}_\rho D(\rho|w)$, where $D \in [D]$
  satisfies constraints D1--D5.
  Transputation is NOT randomness, NOT hidden variables, NOT many-worlds.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{What Transputation Is Not}

\paragraph{Not determinism.}
Bell's theorem (1964) rules out local hidden-variable theories; but transputation is
ruled out even more broadly.  The \lean{det\_no\_go} theorem (\CatAL, nems-lean, NEMS
Paper~12) proves that no deterministic algorithm can reproduce the full PSC-consistent
measurement statistics: in a universe satisfying PSC, diagonal capability, and
record-divergence, a purely deterministic closure is impossible.  Determinism fails
structurally, not merely empirically.

\paragraph{Not randomness.}
The D1--D5 constraints on $[D]$ (detailed in §\ref{sec:d_record}) impose non-trivial
structure on which outcomes are selected.  In particular, constraint D5 requires that
the marginals of $P^\top$'s minimizing distribution reproduce the Born rule: $P(k) =
|c_k|^2$ (\lean{born\_rule\_unconditional}, \CatAL).  A pure random process consistent
with no structure cannot satisfy D5.  The Born rule is empirically extremely precise;
pure randomness would predict deviations, but none are observed.

\paragraph{Not many-worlds.}
PSC requires a single self-consistent record~$w$ for the universe: every element of the
universe must have a determinate PSC-admissible description.  Many-worlds posits that all
branches are simultaneously actual, which contradicts the PSC requirement that the
universe's total record selects a unique realization.  The $P^\top$ forcing chain
below (\lean{closed\_choice\_forces\_transputation}) selects a \emph{single} branch;
multiple branches simultaneously actual is structurally excluded by PSC~\cite{SpivackGTEMobius}.
The polynomial certificate of transputation, including the three-part
PSC-correction characterization of $f_{\mathrm{MDL}}$ and the SRRG
fixed-point bridge, is developed in~\cite{SpivackPolynomialTransputation}.

\subsection*{The Eight-Step CatAL Forcing Chain}

Transputation is not postulated --- it is \emph{forced} by a sequence of eight
machine-certified steps beginning from PSC alone:

\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{PSC axioms:}
  Perfect Self-Containment (PSC Layer~I axioms RC, NM*, TV, SA, AC; Ch.~\ref{chap:principle}).
  The universe is closed, self-referential, and record-bearing.

\item \textbf{PhysUCT --- Physical Universal Computation Theorem:}
  The GTE substrate $\Phi_{\MDL}$ is Turing-universal.  Certs: \lean{phimdl\_turing\_universal}
  (\CatAL, zero sorry); \lean{ugp\_is\_turing\_universal} (\CatAL, zero sorry, one named
  axiom \texttt{rcc\_physics\_ax}).  The universe can simulate any Turing machine.

\item \textbf{ASR --- Arithmetic Self-Reference:}
  A Turing-universal substrate with PSC closure necessarily contains an arithmetic
  self-reference structure (ASR).  The substrate can encode statements about itself.

\item \textbf{RT Undecidable --- Record-Truth Undecidable:}
  By the diagonal construction, the truth predicate on the ASR fragment is not computably
  decidable.  Cert: \lean{asr\_rt\_not\_computable} (\CatAL, NEMS Paper~11).

\item \textbf{\lean{det\_no\_go} (\CatAL):}
  Any purely deterministic closure of the PSC universe would decide record-truth
  on the diagonal fragment --- contradicting step~4.  Deterministic closure is excluded.

\item \textbf{\lean{PSC\_and\_choice\_force\_PT} (\CatAL):}
  PSC + record-divergent Choice Points force the existence of an internal adjudicator
  $P^\top$.  The universe cannot leave itself undetermined; an adjudicator must exist.

\item \textbf{\lean{pt\_not\_total\_effective} (\CatAL):}
  The adjudicator $P^\top$ cannot be a total computable function: if it were, it would
  decide record-truth, contradicting step~4.
  Cert: \lean{transputation\_not\_reducible\_to\_total\_effective\_computation}
  (zero sorry, transputation-lean \cite{SpivackNEMS76}).

\item \textbf{\lean{closed\_choice\_forces\_transputation} (\CatAL):}
  The unique consistent option is a \emph{non-computable adjudicator}: an operator that
  exists, produces definite outcomes, satisfies D1--D5, but cannot be computed by any
  Turing machine on the self-referential diagonal fragment.  This is transputation.
  Zero sorry; certified at \texttt{transputation-lean/Transputation/Theorems/%
  ForcedAdjudication.lean}~\cite{SpivackNEMS76}.

\end{enumerate}

The chain is complete.  PSC closure --- and nothing else --- forces the existence
of the transputation operator $P^\top$.

\subsection*{The TPC Computability Class}

Transputation defines a novel, formally characterised computability class:
the \emph{Turing-PSC Computability Class} (TPC).

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={Result: TPC Strict Containment (\CatAL)}]
  \[
    \text{Decidable} \;\subsetneq\; \text{TPC} \;\subsetneq\; \text{Hypercomputation.}
  \]
  Both containments are strict and machine-certified.
  \lean{tpc\_three\_level\_hierarchy} (\CatAL, zero sorry, zero custom axioms;
  \texttt{GUTStructure.lean}~§62)~\cite{SpivackGTEMobius}.
\end{tcolorbox}

\paragraph{TPC is above Decidable.}
Any total-computable implementation of $P^\top$ would decide record-truth on the
diagonal fragment, contradicting the RT-undecidability established in step~4 above.
Therefore $P^\top$ strictly exceeds the decidable level.
Cert: \lean{d3\_tpc\_above\_decidable} (\CatAL, zero sorry, \texttt{Substrate/%
QECStabilizer.lean}).

\paragraph{TPC is below Hypercomputation.}
TPC cannot decide the halting problem.  The D-selection in TPC operates over a set
of PSC-admissible candidates enumerated by a Turing machine --- D-selection does not
provide a Turing oracle.  One non-computable selection event does not deliver a
general $\Sigma^1_0$ decider.
Cert: \lean{transputation\_not\_reducible\_to\_total\_effective\_computation}
(\CatAL, zero sorry).

\paragraph{TPC is incommensurable with the arithmetical hierarchy.}
The arithmetical hierarchy classifies \emph{decision problems}
(functions $\{0,1\}^* \to \{0,1\}^*$).  TPC classifies \emph{selection problems}
(functions $\mathcal{P}(A) \times W(A) \to A$).  These are different logical types.
TPC is alongside the arithmetical hierarchy, not above or below it.  Quantum computing
(BQP) remains within the Turing-decidable level; TPC's novelty is qualitative (a
different problem type) rather than quantitative (a speed improvement).

\paragraph{TPC has three levels; $N_{\mathrm{gen}} = 3$.}
The TPC hierarchy has exactly three levels, corresponding to three reflexivity classes:
(0)~arithmetic layer (non-reflexive), (1)~computation layer (restricted self-reference),
and (2)~transputation layer (fully reflexive, diagonal-capable).  The three-generation
structure of the Standard Model satisfies $N_{\mathrm{gen}} = 3 =$ depth of the TPC
hierarchy.  Cert: \lean{tpc\_ngen\_equals\_level\_count} (\CatAL, zero sorry,
\texttt{GUTStructure.lean}~§62).  Whether this coincidence follows from a single
structural forcing (Conjecture~C3 of~\cite{SpivackGTEMobius}) is open; the numerical
identity itself is machine-certified.

\subsection*{Transputation and Physical Measurement}

At every quantum measurement event, transputation performs the following:

\begin{enumerate}
\item A physical record~$w$ is created (any record-bearing physical system satisfying
      NEMS Paper~09 minimal-record conditions: a detector click, an atom absorbing a
      photon, any environmental decoherence event).
\item The PSC closure structure activates the adjudication operator:
      $P^\top_D(w) = \operatorname{argmin}_\rho D(\rho|w)$.
\item By constraint D4 (selection completeness), this selects a \emph{unique}~$\rho^*$
      from the PSC-consistent realization class (\lean{d4\_physical\_sector}, \CatAL).
\item The selection is non-computable (D3): no Turing machine implements $P^\top$ on
      the diagonal-capable fragment.
\item The outcome is definite: the field is now in a specific kink sector
      $|\text{kink}_{k^*}\rangle$.
\end{enumerate}

This replaces wavefunction collapse.  There is no collapse: there is \emph{selection}.
The selection is objective --- independent of any observer or conscious agent.
Any record-bearing physical system (a particle detector, a chemical bond, a cosmic-ray
track in rock) triggers transputation.  No mind is required.

The composition rule cleanly separates two previously conflated operations:
\[
  P(\text{outcome} = k \,|\, w) = |c_k(P^\top_D(w))|^2.
\]
Computing \emph{how probable} each outcome is (D5 --- the Born rule, \CatAL) and
determining \emph{which outcome occurs} ($P^\top$ --- objective D-minimization,
\CatAD) are distinct mathematical operations with distinct Lean certifications.
Copenhagen conflates both into the ``collapse postulate.''  GTE separates them.

The two quantum-mechanical substrates used in P37 --- (A)~the $f_{\rm MDL}$ ring
orbit (Hilbert space, Born rule, gauge sectors) and (B)~the AFCA causal structure
(Lorentz invariance, §5 of P37) --- are both derived from the same object: the Level-1
PSC-orbit map and its Level-2 CMCA tape embedding (§\ref{sec:cmca}). They are
two distinct roles of one system, not two competing theories.

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{The [D]-Record and Objective Adjudication}
\label{sec:d_record}

The $[D]$-record is the PSC-consistent selection: the unique branch from the coherence
class of all PSC-admissible realizations that minimizes the coherence functional~$D$.
It is not a subjective construction.  PSC-forced adjudication is as objective as a
mathematical theorem: the universe, given a macroscopic record~$w$, has a determinate
PSC-consistent completion.  No observer is required to choose that completion; no
observer can change it.  The $[D]$-record is the physical record that any observer would
find upon inspection.

\subsection*{Five Constraints D1--D5}

The class $[D]$ of PSC-consistent coherence measures is defined by five constraints
(P34~§3.3~\cite{SpivackGTEMobius}):

\begin{description}

\item[D1 (Non-negativity).]
  $D(\rho|w) \geq 0$, with $D = 0$ iff $\rho$ is fully coherent with record~$w$.
  This makes $D$ a genuine distance-from-coherence functional.

\item[D2 (PSC invariance).]
  $D$ is invariant under all PSC-preserving transformations: $\mathbb{Z}_7$ gauge
  transformations, $\mathbb{Z}_5$ cyclic permutations of the generation ring, orbit
  relabeling, and Lorentz boosts on the $\Phi_{\MDL}$ continuum substrate.
  No member of $[D]$ may prefer one PSC-consistent labeling over another.

\item[D3 (Non-computability).]
  $D$ is \emph{not} a total computable function on diagonal-capable record fragments.
  This constraint is forced unconditionally by the diagonal barrier (step~4 of the
  forcing chain in §\ref{sec:transputation}): any total-effective adjudicator on
  diagonal-capable fragments would decide the halting problem.
  Cert: \lean{d3\_tpc\_above\_decidable} (\CatAL, zero sorry).
  D3 is the foundational constraint --- it is what makes transputation logically
  necessary and what allows GTE to escape Bell's theorem (§\ref{sec:bell}).

\item[D4 (Selection completeness).]
  For every record-equivalence class, $D$ achieves a \emph{unique minimum}.  It does
  not leave the selection degenerate.  Certs: \lean{d4\_physical\_sector},
  \lean{d4\_of\_injective\_coherence} (\CatAL, zero sorry) for the PSC-admissible
  winding sector $\{0,2,3,4,6\}$.  The universal form (all possible record structures)
  is open (Open Problem~4, §\ref{sec:qm_open}).

\item[D5 (Born-rule consistency).]
  The marginals of $D$'s minimizing distribution reproduce the Born rule:
  $P(\text{outcome } k) = |c_k|^2$.
  Cert: \lean{born\_rule\_unconditional} (\CatAL, zero sorry, zero custom axioms).

\end{description}

Any two measures $D$ and $D'$ satisfying all five constraints are members of the same
PSC-coherence class $[D]$.  The abstract theory is well-defined; DSAC (Differential
Self-Adjudicative Computation, NEMS Paper~77) is one concrete member of $[D]$.

\subsection*{The Measurement Problem Dissolved}

Standard quantum mechanics poses the measurement problem as: given a superposition,
\emph{why} does a definite outcome occur, and \emph{why} with probabilities $|c_k|^2$?

In GTE, neither question survives as a mystery:

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Why definite outcomes?}  Because PSC requires the universe's record to be
  self-consistent.  The $[D]$-adjudicator selects the unique PSC-consistent branch from
  the coherence class (D4, \CatAL).  ``Wavefunction collapse'' is replaced by D-minimization:
  the universe selects the realization that minimizes incoherence with the macroscopic record.

\item \textbf{Why $|c_k|^2$?}  Four independent derivations (§\ref{sec:born}).
\end{itemize}

The measurement problem is \emph{dissolved} rather than solved, because the puzzle
arose from trying to fit quantum mechanics into a classical ontology that GTE does not
share.  In GTE's two-level ontology, Level~1 beables are always definite; Level~2 kink
amplitudes admit superpositions; the $[D]$-record determines which Level-2 amplitude
description is canonical.

\subsection*{Which-Path Information and the Quantum Eraser}

The $[D]$-record determines which-path information in a precise way.  If the macroscopic
record~$w$ includes a which-path tag --- a permanent register of which slit a particle
passed through --- then the $[D]$-description of the joint system is restricted to
single-slit statistics.  If no such tag is retained in~$w$, the $[D]$-description allows
both-slit interference.  In the quantum eraser experiment, erasing the which-path tag
is equivalent to erasing the distinguishing information from~$w$.

Crucially, this is \emph{not retrocausal}: no beable trajectory is retroactively altered.
The Level-1 beable (the actual path of the particle at the CA level) is always definite
and fixed.  The quantum eraser manipulates the $[D]$-observable description layer, not
the beable layer.  Apparent retrocausation is an artifact of conflating the two levels
\cite{SpivackCompleteness}.

\subsection*{Why This Is Not a Hidden-Variable Theory}

Hidden-variable theories add degrees of freedom not present in standard quantum
mechanics: the particle's ``true'' position, the ``real'' spin, and so on.  GTE adds
no new degrees of freedom.  Transputation is the PSC-consistent \emph{completion}
of quantum mechanics --- not a modification of it.

The key distinction: in hidden-variable theories, outcomes are determined by a
computable function of a pre-existing variable.  In GTE, outcomes are determined by
the non-computable $P^\top$ acting on the macroscopic record at measurement time.
Non-computability (D3, \CatAL) rules out the computable-hidden-variable scenario at the
same time as it rules out determinism \mbox{(\lean{det\_no\_go}, \CatAL)} and randomness.

The comparison with major competing interpretations is summarised in
Table~\ref{tab:qm_interpretations}.

(GTE).}
\label{tab:qm_interpretations}
\small

\toprule

GTE is closest in spirit to Penrose OR: both are objective, mechanism-based, and
consciousness-free.  The key difference is that OA is information-theoretic
(D-minimization) rather than gravitational, and the OA mechanism is derived from
first principles (PSC + diagonal barrier) rather than assumed phenomenologically.

\section{Physical Incompleteness: The Universe Cannot Predict Itself}
\label{sec:pit}

G\"{o}del's First Incompleteness Theorem says that any sufficiently powerful mathematical
system cannot prove all true statements about itself: there exist true statements that
are unprovable within the system.  GTE establishes an analogous result for \emph{physics}:
no PSC-consistent universe can algorithmically predict all of its own measurement outcomes.

This is not a limitation of our knowledge.  It is a structural property of any
self-referential physical system.  We call it the \emph{Physical Incompleteness Theorem}
(PIT).

\subsection*{Three Premises}

The PIT follows from three premises:

\begin{description}
\item[P1 --- PhysUCT (Physical Universal Computation Theorem).]
  The universe contains a physical universal computer.  Formally: the GTE substrate
  $\Phi_{\MDL}$ is Turing-universal.  Certs: \lean{phimdl\_turing\_universal} (\CatAL),
  \lean{ugp\_is\_turing\_universal} (\CatAL, one named axiom
  \texttt{rcc\_physics\_ax})~\cite{SpivackGTEMobius}.

\item[P2 --- PSC (Perfect Self-Containment).]
  The universe is PSC-consistent: it satisfies the five Layer~I axioms
  (RC, NM*, TV, SA, AC), including Reflexive Closure (RC), which requires the substrate
  to encode its own complete description (Ch.~\ref{chap:principle}).

\item[P3 --- ASR (Arithmetic Self-Reference).]
  The universe contains an arithmetic self-reference structure: it can form statements
  about its own computational behavior, in particular the truth value of
  ``physical record~$w$ is PSC-consistent.''
\end{description}

P1 is machine-certified (\CatAL).  P2 is the foundational axiom of GTE.  P3 follows
from P1 + P2 by the standard G\"{o}del self-reference construction.

\subsection*{Two Forms of the PIT}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={PIT Form A: Computational Undecidability (\CatAL)}]
  Under premises P1--P3, the predicate
  ``physical process $X$ eventually reaches a definite outcome~$w$''
  is not computably decidable.  No algorithm running on the physical substrate can
  predict all measurement outcomes on the self-referential diagonal fragment.
  Cert: \lean{asr\_rt\_not\_computable} (\CatAL, NEMS Paper~11);
  physical corollary: \lean{fmdl\_vacuum\_reachability\_is\_undecidable} (\CatAL
  conditional, \texttt{CUP3DPhysicalIncompleteness.lean})~\cite{SpivackGTEMobius}.
\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={PIT Form B: No Final Self-Theory (\CatAL)}]
  No PSC-consistent theory can contain a complete account of its own physics.
  The universe cannot write its own complete user manual.
  Cert: \lean{no\_final\_self\_theory} (\CatAL, zero sorry;
  \texttt{nems-lean/SemanticSelfDescription/Theorems/NoFinalSelfTheory.lean});
  physical corollary: \lean{physical\_corollary\_no\_final\_gut} (\CatAL),
  NEMS Paper~51~\cite{SpivackNEMSHub}.
\end{tcolorbox}

Forms A and B are related but distinct.  Form~A is about the computational undecidability
of the self-referential predicate on the diagonal fragment of the physical substrate.
Form~B is about the semantic completeness of any internal self-description.
Form~A implies Form~B: if no algorithm can decide all measurement outcomes, no internal
theory can be complete.

\subsection*{PIT vs.\ G\"{o}del and Turing}

Three incompleteness results are in the literature:

\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{G\"{o}del incompleteness} (1931): syntactic and proof-theoretic.  The
  diagonal is constructed from the formal provability predicate.  The undecidable
  statement is unprovable, not uncomputable.

\item \textbf{Turing halting problem} (1936): semantic and computational.  The
  diagonal is constructed from the halting function.  The undecidable question is
  about computation, not proof.

\item \textbf{PIT} (GTE): semantic and physical.  The diagonal is constructed from the
  ASR record-truth predicate on the physical substrate.  The undecidable question is
  about measurement outcomes, not abstract computation.

\end{itemize}

PIT is in the \emph{Turing family} (semantic, computation-diagonal), not the G\"{o}del
family (syntactic, proof-diagonal).  The precise logical relationship is:
PIT follows from Physical Turing Incompleteness (Turing halting applied to physical
computation) combined with PSC-forced selection (the universe must produce answers, even
to questions it cannot algorithmically decide).

\subsection*{Feature, Not Bug}

Physical Incompleteness is not a flaw in GTE.  It is the \emph{necessary consequence}
of PSC closure.

A universe that could fully predict all of its own measurement outcomes from within
would require an internal procedure that decides record-truth on the self-referential
diagonal.  By D3 (Non-computability), no such procedure exists.  A universe that
\emph{were} fully self-predictable would violate D3 and thereby violate PSC.

Put differently: the universe's inability to fully predict itself is precisely what
allows it to \emph{be} a PSC-consistent closed system.  The incompleteness is built in;
it is the price of having no outside.

The PIT's scope is limited.  It does not say that physics is unknowable or that
prediction is impossible.  The vast majority of physical observables --- particle masses,
coupling constants, cosmological parameters, SM predictions throughout this monograph ---
are fully predictable from the GTE structure.  What is not predictable is the specific
outcome of any individual measurement event on the \emph{self-referential fragment}: the
specific decay time of a radioactive atom, the specific outcome of a spin measurement.
These are precisely the quantum events that standard QM also claims are unpredictable,
but for which GTE provides a deeper explanation: they are unpredictable not by accident
but by formal necessity.

\subsection*{Transputation as the Response to PIT}

The PIT establishes that physical undecidable questions exist.  But physical selection
\emph{still occurs}: every quantum event has a definite outcome.  In a PSC-consistent
universe, the universe cannot leave itself undetermined.

Transputation is the operator that resolves this tension.  It is not a Turing machine
(that would violate PIT).  It is not a hypercomputer (that would exceed the PSC boundary
constraint).  It is a PSC-forced non-computable selection operator: it exists, it
produces definite outcomes, and it operates outside the algorithmically decidable zone
--- precisely where PIT says it must.

The PIT and transputation are two sides of the same coin.  The PIT says no algorithm
can replace $P^\top$ on the diagonal fragment.  Transputation names the operator that
fills the gap the PIT creates.  Both are structurally forced by PSC closure; both are
machine-certified at \CatAL; and together they provide the complete resolution of the
measurement problem.

\section{Wave--Particle Duality and the Double Slit}
\label{sec:dslit}

Wave--particle duality --- the apparent contradiction that quantum objects show
particle-like localized clicks and wave-like interference fringes --- has puzzled physics
for over a century.  In GTE's two-level ontology, there is no contradiction.  The two
behaviors arise from two distinct layers of the framework, and the double-slit experiment
is precisely explained without mystery or paradox.

\subsection*{The Three-Layer Resolution}

Every double-slit analysis in GTE has three levels:

\begin{description}

\item[Level~1 --- Definite beable path.]
  Each emitted particle (kink) has a definite trajectory through \emph{one} slit at the
  Level-1 beable layer.  The Rule-110 CA beable configuration in $\mathbb{Z}_7^5$ is always
  definite; there is no beable superposition of paths.  The particle \emph{knows} which
  slit it took, in the sense that the beable trajectory is always well-defined.
  Cert: P37~§2 ('t~Hooft CA interpretation; \cite{SpivackQMFromR110}),
  P41 Zone~Z2~\cite{SpivackCMCA}.

\item[Level~2 --- Superposed phase field $\phi_L + \phi_R$.]
  The $\Phi_{\MDL}$ scalar amplitude field propagates \emph{as a wave} through both slits.
  The screen intensity is
  \[
    I(x) = |\phi(x)|^2,
    \qquad \phi(x) = \phi_L(x) + \phi_R(x),
  \]
  where $\phi_L$ ($\phi_R$) is the amplitude contribution from the left (right) slit.
  Interference is a property of the \emph{phase} in the Level-2 amplitude field, not of
  ``half a particle in each slit.''

\item[Ensemble / Born clicks.]
  Individual detections are discrete localized events; the histogram of many clicks
  reproduces $|\phi(x)|^2$ via the Born rule (\lean{born\_rule\_unconditional}, \CatAL).

\end{description}

The chain theorem (logical order):
\[
  \underbrace{\text{definite beable path}}_{\text{Level 1}}
  \;+\;
  \underbrace{\phi = \phi_L + \phi_R}_{\text{Level 2 amplitude}}
  \;+\;
  \underbrace{P(x) \propto |\phi(x)|^2}_{\text{Born rule, \CatAL}}
  \;\Rightarrow\;
  \text{standard double-slit statistics.}
\]

There is no tension between the three statements.  The electron went through one slit
(beable fact).  The interference pattern emerges from phase addition (amplitude fact).
The click distribution follows $|\phi|^2$ (Born theorem).  All three are true, none
contradictory.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={Result: Double-Slit Certificate (75-DSLIT, \CatA)}]
  Huygens--Fresnel amplitude integration through two slits, Monte Carlo Born sampling
  with $N = 12{,}000$ clicks on 400 screen bins:

  \medskip
  {\small
  \begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}>{\RaggedRight}p{4.8cm}l@{}}
    Corr(simulation, Fraunhofer) & $0.9983$ \\
    Path identity $\max|I - |\phi_L+\phi_R|^2|$ & $2.60 \times 10^{-18}$ \\
    Born clicks corr(hist, $|\phi|^2$) & $0.9942$ \\
    $\chi^2_{\rm red}$ (clicks vs.\ $|\phi|^2$) & $9.77 \times 10^{-5}$ \\
  \end{tabularx}
  }

  \medskip
  Script: \texttt{papers/41\_three\_layer\_chiral\_minkowski\_ca/scripts/%
dslit\_gte\_interference.py}.
  Cert: \CatA\ (computational); Born link: \lean{born\_rule\_unconditional} (\CatAL).
  Source: P41~\cite{SpivackCMCA}, P37~\cite{SpivackQMFromR110}.
\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{Which-Path Information and the Quantum Eraser}

When a which-path tag is recorded in the macroscopic record~$w$, the $[D]$-description
of the event is restricted to single-slit statistics: the $[D]$-adjudicator cannot assign
interference to a system whose record specifies which slit.  Fringes vanish.

When the which-path information is erased from~$w$, the $[D]$-description can again
support interference.  In the quantum eraser experiment, erasing the which-path tag
restores the $[D]$-observable interference, because the distinguishing information has
been removed from the record.

This is not retrocausal.  The Level-1 beable trajectory was definite at all times; no
trajectory is retroactively altered.  The quantum eraser operates on the $[D]$-observable
description layer --- what is knowable from the record --- not on the beable layer.
The apparent ``action backwards in time'' is an artifact of conflating the two levels
\cite{SpivackCompleteness}.

\subsection*{Comparison with Competing Interpretations}

\begin{itemize}

\item \textbf{Copenhagen}: The wavefunction passes through both slits; there is no fact
  of the matter about which slit until measurement.  GTE: the beable has a definite
  path through one slit always; only the amplitude description is ambiguous.

\item \textbf{Many-worlds}: Interference arises from contributions from all branches.
  GTE: interference arises from amplitude superposition at Level~2, with a single
  definite beable path at Level~1; no branching is needed.

\item \textbf{Pilot wave (de Broglie--Bohm)}: Particle has a definite hidden position
  guided by the wave: the wave field exerts a non-local quantum force (the quantum
  potential) that actively steers the particle trajectory.  GTE: the beable is \emph{not}
  guided by the $\Phi_{\MDL}$ amplitude.  The Level-1 beable trajectory is fixed by
  the CA dynamics alone (the $\mathbb{Z}_7^5$ orbit under $f_{\MDL}$), without reference
  to the Level-2 amplitude.  The amplitude field and the beable field are distinct
  mathematical objects in two different levels of the ontology; neither acts on the other.
  Born probabilities follow from $\mathbb{Z}_7$ superselection (\CatAL), not from
  quantum equilibrium or any guidance mechanism.

\end{itemize}

Only GTE provides both a \CatAL\ Born-rule theorem \emph{and} a \CatA\ double-slit
numerical certificate within the same polynomial story.

\subsection*{Honest Limits}

The 75-DSLIT certificate uses Huygens--Fresnel Zones-L1 integration, not a full 2D
$\mathbb{Z}_7$-KG lattice simulation through the slit geometry.  The open items are
listed in §\ref{sec:qm_open}: WPD-1 (full lattice simulation), WPD-2 (delayed-choice
numerics), WPD-3 (Lean cert of the path-identity; not needed given
\lean{born\_rule\_unconditional}).

%--------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Entanglement, Bell Inequalities, and CHSH}
\label{sec:bell}

Bell's theorem (1964) proves that no theory of local hidden variables can reproduce
quantum mechanics.  Formally: any assignment of definite values to particle observables
--- where the assignment rule is a \emph{computable} function of preparation history
--- must satisfy the CHSH inequality $S \leq 2$.  Quantum mechanics violates it, with
maximal violation at the Tsirelson bound $S_{\max} = 2\sqrt{2} \approx 2.828$.

GTE satisfies Bell's theorem in the sense that it is not a local hidden-variable theory.
It also makes a specific structural prediction for the CHSH parameter.

\subsection*{The CHSH Result from Three-Tape Coupling}

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=orange!5,colframe=orange!60,
  title={Result: CHSH $S = 2.4459$ from Three-Tape Polynomial (\CatB)}]
  For the effective qutrit $\{0,2,4\} \subset \mathbb{Z}_7$, with joint Hamiltonian
  \[
    H_{\rm sys} = H_X \otimes I + I \otimes H_Y + G_{\rm eff}\,p/6,
  \]
  where $p$ is the 19-bit GTE polynomial and $G_{\rm eff} = 0.5$,
  the CHSH parameter is $S = 2.4459$.
  This exceeds the classical bound ($S = 2$), confirming non-classical entanglement.
  It lies below the Tsirelson bound ($2\sqrt{2} \approx 2.828$), consistent with QM.
  Bell violation persists for all $G_{\rm eff} \geq 0.095$ (falsifiable threshold).
  Cert: \CatB\ (computational, \texttt{bell\_inequality\_test.py},
  P46~§8~\cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialUFT}).
\end{tcolorbox}

The value $S = 2.4459$ is 86.5\% of the Tsirelson bound.  The measurement settings used
in the CHSH computation are derived from the three-tape coupling geometry of the GTE
polynomial; they are not free parameters.

\subsection*{How GTE Escapes Bell's Theorem}

Bell's theorem assumes that outcome functions are \emph{computable}: the hidden variable
$\lambda$ is assigned to each particle by a well-defined (computable) function of the
preparation history.  The theorem then proves that any such assignment satisfies
$S \leq 2$.

GTE's adjudicator $P^\top$ is \emph{non-computable} (D3, \CatAL).  There is no
algorithm that assigns outcome values to particles from their preparation history.
Specifically: the function ``given record~$w$, output the outcome selected by
$P^\top_D(w)$'' is not a total computable function on the diagonal-capable fragment.

This places GTE outside Bell's assumptions --- specifically, the assumption that the
hidden variable assignment is a well-defined computable function.  GTE escapes Bell
via D3 non-computability, not via any loophole in the experimental design.

GTE does \emph{not} escape Bell by:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Superluminal signals}: The Level-1 beable layer is local and signal-free.
\item \textbf{Many-worlds branching}: $P^\top$ selects one definite outcome.
\item \textbf{Denying free choice}: The non-computability has nothing to do with
  measurement settings.
\end{itemize}

The escape mechanism is a genuine structural difference: Bell's theorem does not apply
to theories with non-computable outcome functions, and GTE proves (not assumes) that
$P^\top$ is non-computable.

\subsection*{Why This Does Not Restore Hidden Variables}

The \lean{det\_no\_go} theorem (\CatAL, NEMS Paper~12) proves that no deterministic
algorithm --- even a non-local one --- can reproduce the full PSC-consistent measurement
statistics.  Hidden variables cannot be restored, whether local or non-local.

The key point: $P^\top$ being non-computable does \emph{not} mean it is unknown or
merely complex.  It is proved to be non-computable in the same sense as the halting
function is proved non-computable.  No future discovery of a better algorithm can
restore deterministic hidden variables.

\subsection*{Entanglement: Semantic Nonlocality}

Two entangled $\Phi_{\MDL}$ kinks that have interacted share a \emph{common
$[D]$-record history} from their interaction event.  When one kink undergoes
$[D]$-selection (record~$w_1$ is created), the PSC closure structure constrains what
$[D]$ can select for the correlated partner: any $[D]$-admissible description of the
joint system must respect the common record established at interaction time.

The EPR correlations are real and quantum-mechanically complete --- but they are
\emph{semantic}: a property of the $[D]$-observable description layer, not of the beable
layer.  No beable signal propagates between the particles.  The Level-1 CA beables
are always local and signal-free.  Cert: NEMS Papers~45--46, no-signaling theorem
\cite{SpivackNEMSHub}.

The comparison Level-1 / Level-2 distinguishes GTE clearly from pilot-wave
theories.  Pilot-wave theories require a non-local guidance equation in configuration
space.  GTE's beables are local at Level~1; the EPR correlations exist only at the
$[D]$-description level (Level~2) and carry no beable signal.

\section{Quantum Mechanics: Open Problems}
\label{sec:qm_open}

The following open problems are genuine frontiers in the GTE quantum mechanics programme.
None invalidates the main claims of this chapter; each is a target for future work.

\begin{openprob}
\textbf{All-order Born rule in interacting QFT.}
The derivation of the Born rule \mbox{(\lean{born\_rule\_unconditional}, \CatAL)} is established
in the free $\Phi_{\MDL}$ Fock space.  Extension to interacting quantum field theory
with loop corrections --- the full perturbative and non-perturbative Born rule --- is
open.  The tree-level result is certified; all-order renormalization group running
of the probability assignment is pending.
\end{openprob}

\begin{openprob}
\textbf{CHSH $S = 2.4459$ from first principles (\CatAL\ target).}
The value $S = 2.4459$ (§\ref{sec:bell}) is \CatB\ (numerical, \texttt{bell\_inequality\_test.py}).
A Lean-certified derivation from the three-tape coupling geometry --- proving that the
specific effective qutrit $\{0,2,4\} \subset \mathbb{Z}_7$ and PMDL Hamiltonian give
$S > 2$ analytically --- awaits quantum mechanics Lean libraries for $\mathbb{C}^7 \otimes
\mathbb{C}^7$ density matrices.
\end{openprob}

\begin{openprob}
\textbf{D4 universal selection completeness.}
Constraint D4 (selection completeness) is machine-certified \CatAL\ in the
PSC-admissible winding sector $\{0,2,3,4,6\}$
(\lean{d4\_physical\_sector}, \lean{d4\_of\_injective\_coherence}).
The universal form --- for all possible record structures, not only the winding sector
--- is open.  Whether D4 holds universally follows from whether the [D]-class can be
extended beyond the $\mathbb{Z}_7^5$ beable lattice.
\end{openprob}

\begin{openprob}
\textbf{Full $\Phi_{\MDL}$ decoherence dynamics.}
The detailed transition from the thermal pre-measurement state to a definite post-measurement
outcome --- the full decoherence-to-transputation dynamics of the $\Phi_{\MDL}$ field ---
is open.  The thermal pre-measurement state is certified \mbox{(\lean{phimdl\_thermal\_state\_master},
\CatAL\ conditional)}; the time evolution of decoherence into $P^\top$ selection is
structurally motivated but not Lean-certified.
\end{openprob}

\begin{openprob}
\textbf{Double-slit WPD-1: full $\mathbb{Z}_7$-KG lattice simulation.}
The 75-DSLIT certificate (§\ref{sec:dslit}) uses Huygens--Fresnel Zone-L1 integration.
A full 2D $\mathbb{Z}_7$-KG lattice simulation of the double-slit geometry would provide
a direct CA-level numerical certificate.  This is an enhancement (the Born-rule link
is \CatAL) but not required for the core claim.
\end{openprob}

\begin{openprob}
\textbf{TPC depth and $N_{\rm gen} = 3$: structural forcing (Conjecture~C3).}
The coincidence \lean{tpc\_ngen\_equals\_level\_count} (TPC hierarchy depth = $N_{\rm gen}
= 3$, \CatAL) is machine-certified as a numerical identity.  Whether PSC acting on the
TPC hierarchy structurally \emph{forces} $N_{\rm gen} = 3$ --- i.e., whether there is a
single structural fact from which both follow --- is Conjecture~C3
of~\cite{SpivackGTEMobius}, open.
\end{openprob}

\begin{openprob}
\textbf{Glider-to-Fermion Correspondence.}
Specific CA microstructure patterns (gliders, periodic orbits) in the Rule-110 / $\Phi_{\MDL}$
dynamics corresponding to individual Standard Model fermions at the Compton scale have
not been enumerated.  The BPS kink mass spectrum is certified (\CatAL); the specific
Rule-110 glider configurations that realize each fermion are an open combinatorial problem.
